# ISO Audit — Task Hub Integration Plan

**Written for:** the model executing inside this repo across several sessions (Opus or Sonnet).
**Author:** planning session 2026-09-29 (Opus), after a full inspection of Task Hub and the prototype.
**Revised:** 2026-09-29 (Opus) — the user's ten additions R1–R10 (brief §33) folded in: ISO boards
behave like Task Hub boards (menu, access, client, time, billing, reports, digest, Gantt), never open a
new tab, and reuse everything already improved. See D10–D18, the rewritten Phase 5, Phase 7b and §3.5.
Evidence is SharePoint-link-first to keep storage down (D18). Every anchor in §3.2 was re-verified
against the tree at `8863987` on 2026-09-29.
**Revised:** 2026-10-02 (Opus) — the user's additions R11–R13 (brief §34): evidence review and reuse
(Phase 8b), document generator prompts with no AI in the app (Phase 8c), and the standard library as
reviewed seed files with editions (Phase 4b). See D19–D25 and 🛑 STOP R (cleared 2026-10-02; built 2026-10-02/03, A69–A85).
**Status:** IN PROGRESS — superseded by the ⏸ RESUME HERE block below; read that, not this line. As of
2026-10-05: Phases 0–10.1 built and gated; the ISO board redesign (D35–D41) is approved, R1 (library
swap), R2 (status model, Lead Auditor, roles) and R3 (Clauses view, including the cross-standard clause
grouping) are all done; R4's data layer (document catalog, bulk evidence linking, D40's prompt-template
re-key) is done; R5's Overview tab is done; the board now matches the approved wireframe end to end —
one-card shell, four tabs, R4b's Documents tab and R6's Actions tab — plus the Approve/Return UI suite R2
left open, gate-confirmed (4341 / 0, 82 suites; uncommitted, hard rule 2). Next: build docs/iso-audit/release-1-completion-plan.md (approved 2026-10-06, D44–D56), starting with B0.

## How to start (paste this to the executing model)

> Read `docs/iso-audit-integration-plan.md` top to bottom, then `docs/iso-audit/brief.md` (the user's
> brief — every "the brief's §N" reference points there; §33 holds the user's later additions R1–R10,
> §34 holds R11–R13),
> then the `⏸ RESUME HERE` block. Follow its hard rules exactly. Execute phases in order, starting with
> the next unchecked one. Stop at every **🛑 STOP** marker and report. Do not commit, push, deploy, or
> run SQL anywhere. Update the RESUME HERE block and the Progress Log at the end of every session.

> **Found and fixed on the way (worth knowing):** A80 (Supabase generic updates dropped every op's activity
> description — reason-required changes would have lost their reason). A81 (stored script injection through inline
> handlers — evidence Copy link, cause-tree ids, due dates — now passed in `data-*` attributes and checked on the
> server). Timestamps showed the UTC date, a day early in WA before 8 am (`fmtTs`). A race where an older prompt
> preview could overwrite a newer one (A85). index.html had lost CRLF (A70). **A87's four metric bugs:**
> `conformance_pct` divided by the wrong denominator (excluded unassessed requirements instead of counting them
> against the figure); "assessed" didn't actually require a recorded result; `shared_actions` counted two
> requirements of the *same* standard as "shared", and never excluded a link already rejected by `action.unlink`;
> `actions_by_owner` counted closed actions and never split etcher/client. One existing test had the
> `shared_actions` bug's wrong behaviour pinned as correct — corrected, with the real positive case moved to the
> new `iso-calc-test.cjs`. Suite counts in some earlier log rows were each one too high; corrected. **A88:** a
> register's own quick view could re-hide rows a dashboard click had correctly filtered (all three registers); fixed
> and pinned by a regression test.
> **Corrections — both resolved in 9.4 (A89):** A87's "nothing writes the baseline" was wrong (the workbook import
> does — baseline vs current is now built); Not applicable is now an applicability decision only (D30).

> machinery the register's own inline date editor already uses.
> **Everything named anywhere in Phase 7.3/7.4's own scope notes is now built.** Before starting
> Phase 7b (below), read `iso/iso-core.js`'s Findings block
> (`buildFindingsCtx` through `_fndAddComment`), Actions block (`ACTION_STATUS_LABEL` through
> `_rcTreeEdit`), and `openRequirementDrawer`'s own traceability-panel additions as the templates to
> mirror, and read A49 in full — it now documents FOUR non-obvious traps, several already hit more
> than once, that anything new in this phase will hit again if not copied carefully: (1) never call
> the browser's native `window.prompt`/`alert`/`confirm` for anything — always the app's own
> `showPrompt`/`showAlert`/`openConfirm` (a native dialog silently hangs a Puppeteer test with no
> dialog handler, and is also just worse UX — `mutateReasoned` already does this correctly, reuse it
> rather than writing a new reason-prompt path; `iso-ui-test.cjs` also keeps a permanent
> `page.on('dialog')` listener that fails fast, not a hang, if this recurs); (2) after ANY mutation
> that changes a row's `row_version` (a status transition, verify, reopen, decide-review,
> extension-decide), update that row inside its local cache (`Object.assign`) before re-rendering
> anything from it; (3) also call `renderFindingsTable`/`renderActionsTable` after every one of
> those same mutations — the drawer and the register are refreshed independently, and a drawer-only
> refresh leaves the *register* showing pre-mutation data until the whole tab is next re-visited;
> (4) **the same lesson applies to any other local editing draft, not just the row cache** — the
> guided root-cause editors kept an in-memory `_capaDraft` for their own add/remove UI, and it had
> to be explicitly resynced from the server's own response after every save, or a silently
> incomplete save would still LOOK successful in the drawer (this was the one adversarial check this
> session that didn't catch its own bug on the first attempt — see A49's own entry for the full
> story of how that surfaced). All four traps are one underlying lesson: a UI can hold more than one
> cached/drafted copy of the same data (row cache, register render, local edit buffer), and a
> mutation must resync every one of them from the server's own authoritative response, never assume
> local state is already correct. A fifth, smaller lesson: if a UI panel needs a fact that lives
> only in a server-internal join table (no client-facing op ever returns it), prefer attaching that
> fact onto an existing `list` op's rows (like `finding.list`'s own `requirement_id`) over inventing
> a whole new op.

## 1. Hard rules (non-negotiable)

1. **Never touch production.** `.env` points at the LIVE Supabase project (`wowwiafaqzovlfwzyktj`)
   with the service-role key. Develop and test ONLY with `node serve.mjs --offline`. Never run
   SQL against any Supabase project. Never read or print `.env` values. Migration files are
   written, never applied — the user applies them. **No test or script may ever start `serve.mjs`
   without `--offline`.** A suite that needs its own server runs
   `node serve.mjs --offline --port <n>` with `ISO_OFFLINE_DIR=<temp dir>`.
2. **No commits, pushes or deploys** unless the user explicitly says so. Leave work in the tree.
   At each 🛑 STOP, tell the user they can say "commit phase N" if they want a checkpoint.
3. **Never commit or read `appdata.json`** (real data, plaintext passwords).
4. **Do not modify** `workboard/ISO Audit/` (the prototype — reference only), the source
   workbook `workboard/ISO Audit/source/*.xlsx`, or `Etcher_ISO9001_Gap_Analysis.xlsx` (repo root).
   Copy them if you need to.
5. **`index.html` is CRLF and grep sees it as binary.** Always `grep -an`. Treat any
   "Binary file matches" line as a failed search. Preserve CRLF on every edit. After any
   scripted (Bash/Node) text substitution, re-read the changed bytes to confirm backslashes and
   regexes survived — earlier sessions corrupted files through shell escaping. **Never run `sed -i` on
   `index.html` (or any CRLF file) from Git Bash: it silently rewrites every line ending to LF** (seen
   2026-09-29 on this plan). Use the Edit tool, or Node with explicit `\r\n` handling, and confirm with
   `node -e` that the CRLF count equals the line count afterwards.
6. **Keep ISO logic out of `index.html`; put only hooks there.** ISO is part of Task Hub (hard rule 14), so
   `index.html` gets small, named hooks that call `ISO.*` — never ISO business logic, and never
   monkey-patching of Task Hub globals from `iso/*.js`. The allowed `index.html` changes are exactly:
   the Phase 1–2 adapter refactors; the Phase 5 hooks (ISO nav section markup + `NAV_SECTION_HEADERS.iso`,
   one call in `updateSidebarWorkboard`, `applyRolePermissions`, `viewTitles`/`renderView` cases `iso` and
   `iso-admin`, the topbar client line in `navigate`, `_acBlockedViews`/`_STAFF_BLOCKED`, the Admin-section
   "ISO library" item, script/link tags); the 7.6 notification merge; the Phase 7b hooks (Billing
   permission gates + staff-form tick, Timesheets ISO target, Reports ISO tab + filter, Pro forma line
   labels, Digest items, `_ganttPdf` in the shared Gantt layer); the 8.3 preview extraction; the 13.2/13.7
   portal hooks. Each hook is listed in its task with its anchor. Anything else → stop and add it to this
   list with a reason first. Everything else lives in `iso/` (front end) and `api/` (server).
   **Release-1 B6 (go-live; added 2026-10-11 before building, per this rule):** Task Hub-wide security changes outside the ISO hook list — (a) `serve.mjs` + `vercel.json` security headers from one source (`security-headers.cjs`, written into `vercel.json` by `scripts/gen-vercel-headers.cjs`) so local and live can't drift (live had only the CSP meta tag: no HSTS, no frame-ancestors, no X-Frame-Options); (b) the `index.html` supabase-js `<script>` tag pinned to a vendored exact version with an integrity hash (a floating `@2` CDN tag runs whatever the CDN serves next, on the login page); (c) an `index.html` login-screen "Sign in with Microsoft" button, shown only when `/api/config` reports it switched on (`api/config.js` + `serve.mjs`), calling the Supabase Azure provider — the password login stays as the emergency path; (d) `.github/dependabot.yml`. Each is in the B6 Progress Log row.
6b. **Every `_isoDb` method is implemented in both adapters in the same task.** A "not implemented" throw
   in either adapter means the task is not done.
7. **Tests must be able to fail.** A suite that reports 0 passes is a flake, not a pass. For
   every rule you implement, show the new test failing against the pre-change code (or a
   deliberately broken version) before trusting it passes. Add every new suite to
   `.agents/gate.sh` `SUITES`, printing `PASS (n):` / `FAIL (n):`.
8. **Refactors of shared Task Hub code are behaviour-preserving.** Phases 1–2 must leave the
   Workboards Main Table and Gantt byte-for-byte identical in output (golden tests) and the full
   gate green.
9. **No fake data, placeholder numbers or dead buttons.** If a feature is not done, it is not
   shown. Never claim a phase complete with failing or skipped tests.
10. **Server enforces everything.** Every ISO permission, visibility rule and state transition
    is checked in `api/_iso.js`, never only in the browser.
11. Follow `DESIGN.md` for all UI (Jost structure / Lato content, navy-tinted layered shadows,
    teal = one signal per region, 38px rows, focus-visible rings, animate transform/opacity only).
12. When something is ambiguous: inspect first, pick the least destructive reversible option,
    record it under §14 "Assumptions log", continue. If it is destructive, stop and ask.
13. **Never open a new browser tab or window — under any circumstances (user requirement R8).** ISO code
    (`iso/*.js`, ISO hooks in `index.html`, ISO server-rendered pages) never calls `window.open`, never
    sets `target` on any element, never assigns `location`/`location.href` to another origin, never calls
    `_clickDownload` without a filename (it falls back to `target=_blank`, index.html ~3439), and never
    prints via a popup (the `quotePrint` pattern). Files open in the in-page preview; PDFs/Excel/CSV
    download with an explicit name; external URLs (evidence links, SharePoint, document-register URLs)
    render as selectable text with a **Copy link** button, never as a clickable anchor. Enforced by
    `iso-ui-test` (static scan + a runtime `window.open` trap, see Phase 5 tests).
    Task Hub itself still has new-tab sites — `quotePrint` (`window.open`, index.html 13801), file-link icons
    (22383, 22395), custom-column URL cells (23787, 23880), markdown/notes links (15707, 15725, 17270),
    meeting Teams links (6659), and `_clickDownload` without a name (3439). They are pre-existing and out of
    scope (A15): do not fix them, and never route ISO through them. Allowed and same-tab: `doc.save(...)`
    (jsPDF downloads, used by every PDF export today) and the Digest's `window.open(mailto:…,'_self')`
    (14770).
14. **ISO is part of Task Hub, not a separate module (R8).** Where Task Hub already has a concept, ISO
    uses it or its exact look and behaviour: the left-menu section (like WorkBoards, with "+" and a live
    list), board chrome (topbar client identity, tab strip, header buttons), the "Shared with" access
    model and modal look, the Client Manager's clients, Timesheets, Reports, Pro forma, Digest, the
    notification bell with jump-to-row, toasts/confirms/modals, skeletons, the mobile drawer. ISO never
    creates clients, never has its own login, never has a separate "app" page. Admin-only ISO setup lives
    under Task Hub's Admin menu section.
15. **Everything already improved stays improved (R3).** Read §3.5 before touching shared code. Follow the
    established patterns exactly: delete tombstones for any blob collection (`_tombstoneRemoved` + the
    `_mergeDB` loop), `escHtml(JSON.stringify(x))` or `data-*` attributes inside inline handlers (never raw
    interpolation), jump-to-row on notification click (`notifGoToTask` behaviour), layout-matched skeletons
    (`._sk`/`._skl`), CAS saves via `saveDB` only, "pro forma edits never write back to approved time
    entries". The gate TOTAL may only go up; every suite named in §3.5 stays green.

---

## 2. Decisions already made with the user (do not re-litigate)

| # | Decision | Source |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | ISO data lives in **new relational Supabase tables** (`iso_*`), reached only through a server endpoint. **Nothing ISO goes into the `app_state` blob** except the optional ISO fields on ordinary time entries (D12). *(Revised: the earlier "linked Workboard task" exception is gone — see D12.)* | Forced by the brief's own requirements (server-side isolation, safe numbering, locked history, immutable reports, no bulk loading into the browser) — the blob can satisfy none of them. |
| D2 | Build and review **offline** (`serve.mjs --offline`) against a local file-backed ISO store behind the same API. Before go-live, verify the real SQL on a **separate dev Supabase project** the user creates. | User choice |
| D3 | **Refactor the Workboards Main Table and Gantt so they accept any record type, then reuse them** for ISO. No second table or Gantt engine. | User choice |
| D4 | Corrective actions are carried out by **both** Etcher staff (billable) and client staff. Etcher reviews and verifies all. | User choice |
| D5 | **Release 1 includes** root-cause + CAPA fields, report builder + immutable snapshots, client factual-accuracy review + management response (client portal ISO tab), and registers (risk, legal/obligations, controls, documents). | User choice |
| D6 | Excel export per standard reproduces `Etcher_ISO9001_Gap_Analysis.xlsx` exactly (style, 3 sheets, formulas), generated per standard for 9001/14001/45001. One deliberate change: *Not applicable* rows leave the "Overall conformance" denominator. | User instruction + default flagged to user |
| D7 | Single organisation (Etcher). "Organisation isolation" = not applicable; isolation is by **client + audit + role**. | Task Hub has no organisation entity |
| D8 | The standard library holds Etcher's paraphrased requirement wording from the workbook, not verbatim ISO text. | Copyright caution |
| D9 | Items after "Trend a…" in the user's brief were truncated; the user said to proceed with what exists. | User |
| D10 | **An ISO audit is an "ISO board" in Task Hub.** One ISO board = one audit covering one, two or all three standards for one client. It looks and behaves like a workboard (menu section, board chrome, access, client identity) but its record lives server-side in `iso_audits` — **not** in `DB.boards`. Integration with Task Hub is **opt-in at named hooks** (hard rule 6), never by adding records that ~30 existing `DB.boards` loops, the portal slice (`api/_portal.js` `sliceForClient`) and `deleteWorkboard` would treat as workboards. | User R4/R5/R8; research 2026-09-29 found that a `DB.boards{kind:'iso'}` record would leak into the portal "engagements", pickers, dashboard, search, triage and archive flows. |
| D11 | **Access works exactly like workboards (R10), chosen when the board is created, and is enforced by the server.** Same two options, labels and meaning as workboards (`canViewBoard` 5070, `openBoardShareModal` 6139): **All internal staff** (`all_internal`) or **Private (invite only)** (`private`) + owner + named people; admin sees all. Unlike workboards (whose create form 6110/6126 only picks visibility — people are added afterwards), the ISO create flow picks visibility **and** people in step 3 (R10 "when add new iso board we should be choosing who has access"); both stay editable later through a "Shared with" button + modal with the same look. Source of truth is server-side: `iso_audits.visibility`, `owner_uid`, `iso_audit_members` (people with access + their ISO role) — never `DB.boardShares` (blob, browser-only, and its deletes have no tombstone, A12). ISO roles (§6.4) only decide *what* someone who has access may do. Restricted staff may view as reviewer but **cannot create** ISO boards (A13). | User R10; hard rule 10 (server enforces) — ISO is stricter than workboards, whose checks are browser-only. |
| D12 | **Hours on ISO work are ordinary Task Hub time entries (R1)** with the audit's `clientId` plus optional snapshot fields `isoAuditId`, `isoLabel`, `isoActionId`, `isoActionRef`. Timesheets gains a third target mode **ISO audit** next to Task and Client (`_teMode` 10007). No linked tasks. The entries flow through the existing submit → approve → Reports → Pro forma path unchanged, so "pro forma edits never write back to approved time entries" still holds (now with a test). | User R1/R7; verified 2026-09-29: `_teCreateEntry` (10800) already writes `taskId:null` + `clientId` for Client-mode entries; `_cascadeDeleteTasks` (16863) and three other delete paths (15335, 15347, 21690) drop every **unapproved** entry of a deleted task — so the old linked-task idea would have lost hours. |
| D13 | **ISO shows up in Task Hub's own Reports, Pro forma and Digest (R7, R9)**: ISO hours are filterable by ISO board in the Timesheet report and labelled as their own project in Pro forma; Reports gets an "ISO audits" tab; the Digest's auto-items include client-safe ISO progress for that client. | User R7/R9 |
| D14 | **Sending a Gantt (R2):** the ISO action-plan Gantt (Phase 2 adapter, same look as workboards) gets **Export PDF** (downloaded, never a tab) with a **Client version** switch (only client-visible actions, no internal text), and the same Gantt appears read-only in the client's portal. It is built in the shared Gantt layer so Workboards can switch it on later (not in release 1 — goldens stay unchanged). Workboards have no Gantt send/export today. | User R2; research: no Gantt export/print/send exists; Share links carry no start dates. |
| D15 | **Clients come only from the Client Manager (R6).** The New ISO board flow picks from existing clients (`clientDisplayTitle`, sorted); no client can be created or edited from ISO. "Active client" = `c.active !== false && !c.archived` (both fields exist, neither is set to false today). The ISO menu lists clients that have at least one open ISO board. | User R5/R6 |
| D16 | **Who may invoice ISO work (R2 — Sam): a per-person Billing permission.** Reports, Pro forma and time approval are admin-only today and Sam is `staff`. Add a **Billing** tick to the staff record (edited only by admins in the existing staff edit form). A non-admin staff member with Billing may open Reports (Timesheet, Pro forma and the ISO tab) and approve time; everything else about their role is unchanged, and it never applies to `restricted`, `pm` or client users. It is a Task Hub-wide permission (Workboard and ISO hours alike — Pro forma cannot split them), set per person, off by default, logged to the Audit Trail when changed. Exact gates in Phase 7b.1. | User answer 2026-09-29 ("Add Billing permission") |
| D17 | **"New ISO board" is three steps, then the populated template (R4, R6, R10).** From the ISO section's "+": (1) **Client** — pick an active client from the Client Manager (no creating clients here); (2) **Standards** — ISO 9001, 14001, 45001, any one, two, or "All three"; optional "Start from template"; (3) **Access** — visibility + people + their ISO role. "Create ISO board" copies every requirement of the chosen standards from the pinned library version and lands on the Requirements tab, grouped standard → clause, every row "Not started" — the template ready to populate. The brief's 5-step wizard fields (§8: title, type, dates, scope, exclusions, units, certification body, lead auditor, portal sharing) move to **Settings → Details**, filled in whenever the user wants; sensible defaults at creation (title `<client code> ISO <codes>`, type `gap_analysis`, dates empty, portal sharing off). "Import workbook…" (Phase 4.6) is a second entry in the same "+" menu. *(Landing tab superseded by D27, 2026-10-03: a board now opens on its Dashboard.)* | User R4 ("chose new client board, add which standards or all 3 and then show the template ready to be populate") wins over brief §8's order (brief §33). |
| D18 | **Evidence is SharePoint-link-first (user, 2026-09-29: "i can just attach a sharepoint link to keep storage down").** "Add evidence" opens on **Paste a SharePoint or web link** (`evidence.addLink`; kind `sharepoint` when the host is `*.sharepoint.com`, else `link`); **Upload a file instead** is the secondary option, capped per file by `iso_config.evidence_max_upload_mb` (default **10**, admin-settable up to 25). Settings shows the audit's storage used (sum of `size_bytes`). Links render as text + **Copy link** (hard rule 13) — Task Hub stores only the URL and does not check who can open it in SharePoint (A17). Reports PDFs and imported workbooks are still stored (small, required for immutability). | User instruction |
| D19 | **Evidence keeps three layers (R11).** (1) The client's controlled document — `iso_documents` (Phase 12, per client: revision, effective date, approval status, owner, next review, superseded, SharePoint URL). (2) Audit evidence — `iso_evidence`, one record per thing examined, fixing the revision and date seen; it may link to many requirements/findings/actions across all the board's standards. (3) The per-target link — `iso_evidence_links`, holding that target's own verdict. There is **no single cross-audit "Evidence Library" record**: a document's revision change must never rewrite past audit evidence, and next year's audit must never inherit last year's acceptance. Reuse on one board = `evidence.link` (no duplicate records); reuse across a client's boards = the same `iso_documents` row (pull Phase 12's table forward only if needed). | Review of the R11 brief, 2026-10-02 |
| D20 | **Each evidence link has exactly two judgement fields (R11).** `role` (`primary` / `supporting`) and one reviewer verdict, `review_status`: `submitted` (shown as "Not reviewed", the default) · `under_review` · `more_info_required` · `accepted` · `partially_sufficient` · `insufficient` · `rejected` (not relevant to this target) · `superseded`. The brief's separate "applicability" and "sufficiency" fields are folded in (partial applicability = `partially_sufficient`; "supporting only" = `role`), because a third and fourth status field allow contradictions such as accepted + insufficient. Only a reviewer sets a verdict; the person adding or uploading evidence never does (no self-review). Whether a requirement conforms stays solely on `assessment_result`. `requested` moves off the link — evidence requests have their own table. | Review of the R11 brief, 2026-10-02 |
| D21 | **Cross-standard evidence suggestions are derived when read, never stored as rows (R11).** A suggestion for (evidence E, requirement R2) exists when: E has a non-`rejected` link to R1; R1↔R2 is a **confirmed** relationship of an evidence-sharing kind (`same_requirement`, `integrated_topic`, `shared_evidence` — never `supporting`); it is one hop only; E is not marked "use for this requirement only"; and no link row of any status exists yet for (E, R2). Only decisions are stored: confirming creates a real link row (its own role, verdict "Not reviewed"); rejecting creates a `rejected` link row, which migration 010's unique (evidence, target) constraint makes permanent. This works whichever came first (the relationship or the evidence), leaves nothing stale when a relationship is later rejected, and writes nothing in bulk. The selector's "Used on related requirements" group reads the same derivation. | Review 2026-10-02 — the brief's "create suggestion records when evidence is linked" misses any relationship confirmed afterwards |
| D22 | **Document generator prompts contain no AI (R12).** No AI service, API key, endpoint, provider setting or model choice exists in the app. `prompt.build` is a pure server function: (approved template + client's confirmed profile + requirement + confirmed related requirements + the user's answers + an explicit prepared-on date) → text, so the same inputs always give byte-identical output. The label is "Download generator prompt", never "Generate compliant document". Neither the prompt nor anything made from it counts as evidence by itself; a resulting document enters through the normal evidence workflow as **Draft**. Copy and download are logged (the moment client data leaves the app); merely opening the dialog is not. The dialog always shows a data notice (client information is going to a third-party tool of the user's choosing), with an include switch for each profile section — sections the template needs start on — and the activity entry records which sections went out. | User R12 + review 2026-10-02 |
| D23 | **One Company Profile per client; templates and sources as reviewed seed files (R12).** Per client (user, 2026-10-02), shared by all that client's ISO boards and edited from a board's Settings tab. Every value carries `confirmed`/`by`/`at`; only confirmed values appear as fact in a prompt. Hazards/risks and legal and other obligations are kept as confirmed list items until the Phase 12 registers (`iso_risks`, `iso_obligations`) exist, then read from those registers (one source of truth). Prompt templates are versioned JSON seed files reviewed in the code (user, 2026-10-02) — `iso/seed/prompt-templates/<key>.json`, each with `version`, `review_status` (only `approved` is offered), `sources` (title, publisher, URL, date checked) and its mapping to workbook rows; an in-app editor is a later release. Sources are official and free only (ISO/TC 176 Auditing Practices Group papers, Safe Work Australia model codes, state regulators' codes). They shape structure and checklists only: they never supply ISO text (D8), and never assert that a law applies — obligations for a jurisdiction come only from the client's confirmed profile. | User answers 2026-10-02 |
| D24 | **The standard library becomes reviewed seed files with editions (R13) — pending the user's OK at 🛑 STOP R.** No upload needed: one seed file per edition (`iso/seed/library/9001-2015.json`, `14001-2015.json`, `45001-2018.json`), built from the Coastal Electrics workbook's Etcher wording with source rows and stable keys identical to the import's, loaded as approved `iso_standard_versions` on first start. The library import stays as an optional path, and the audit workbook import (a client's filled-in gap analysis) is unchanged. **Editions are versions, not replacements:** ISO 9001:2026 (published 16 Sep 2026) and ISO 14001:2026 (published Apr 2026) get their own seed files once Etcher has written its wording from a licensed copy, and a board keeps the edition it was created on. Claude does not generate standard text: ISO's copyright covers it (D8), Claude has no access to the published 2026 texts, and anything written from memory would be unverified — not a source of truth. UI and prompts label the text "Requirement (Etcher wording)" and say to check the licensed standard for the authoritative text. | User R13. TC 176: from 31 Mar 2028 new ISO 9001 certifications are only to 2026, and 2015 certificates must transition by 30 Sep 2029 — both editions stay in use for about three years. |
| D25 | **Guidance stays apart from requirement text (R11).** Kept in reviewed seed files `iso/seed/guidance/<edition>.json`, keyed by clause (clause-level) and library `stable_key` (requirement-level), because one clause holds several requirement rows. It is never stored on library rows (immutable once approved). Shown in the Assessment tab labelled "Internal guidance — does not replace the standard requirement". The workbook's evidence examples keep the label "Guidance only. Suggested evidence is non-mandatory and non-exhaustive." The first release seeds only the workbook's examples; plain-language explanations are written and approved by Etcher over time, and no unreviewed AI text is shown to auditors. | Review of the R11 brief, 2026-10-02 |
| D26 | **The ISO dashboard is the Dashboard tab inside one ISO board: per client, and separate from Task Hub's own Dashboard.** Every ISO board belongs to exactly one client, so its dashboard only ever shows that board's records — never another client's, never another audit's. No roll-up across boards or clients (a client's yearly boards would count the same requirements twice; year-on-year comparison is D31's explicit link instead). It never changes Task Hub's home Dashboard (`navigate('dashboard')`), the Workboards views, or the ISO landing portfolio (the only cross-client ISO view, staff-only). Staff-only until Phase 13 (`dashboard.audit` refuses client callers); a client version must be computed from client-visible records only, never by hiding parts of the staff numbers. | User, 2026-10-03 (review of a ChatGPT-drafted dashboard brief) |
| D27 | **Dashboard is the first tab and the tab a board opens on.** Order: Dashboard · Requirements · Findings · Actions · Action plan · Evidence · Settings. Opening a board from anywhere (sidebar, landing page, new-board wizard) lands on Dashboard; a reload keeps the tab you were on. Supersedes D17's landing on the populated register and plan 5.4b's phase-ordered tab list. | User, 2026-10-03 |
| D28 | **Conformance stays, counts first: "X of Y conforming (Z%)".** X = applicable requirements with result `implemented_effective`; Y = applicable requirements (unassessed stay in Y) — the same formula as `conformance_pct` and the export's "Overall conformance". The formula is shown on the page, not only on hover. Never combined with assessment coverage, action completion or evidence review into one score. | User, 2026-10-03 |
| D29 | **Dashboard filters: standard only in the first stage.** A standard scopes every dashboard number and chart: requirements of that standard; findings whose source requirement is of it; actions with at least one confirmed link (direct, or through a linked finding) to a requirement of it — so an action spanning 9001 and 14001 counts under both, by design; evidence links and requests whose target is in scope; suggestions targeting an in-scope requirement. Click-through keeps the standard. Owner, result, visibility and unit filters come later, each defined in the calculation dictionary first. Kept in the session route, not the URL (ISO has no URL routing). | User chose standard-only; the scoping rules are Claude's recommendation |
| D30 | **Not applicable is an applicability decision only.** N/A is recorded under Applicability with its justification (already required); `requirement.update` refuses `assessment_result = 'not_applicable'` and the result pickers no longer offer it. Two ways to say N/A made every count ambiguous (§7 excludes both, the calculations excluded one) and the filter schema has no OR to express "either". 009's check constraint still allows the value (never applied; harmless). | Claude's recommendation, taken under the standing take-the-recommended-answer instruction; the user may revisit |
| D31 | **Yearly maintenance audits: a new board each year, linked to the last.** Etcher's own maintenance internal audit for the client, full scope every year. When a board is completed it receives a maintenance audit date (default 12 months after completion, editable). "Start maintenance audit" creates the next board from it: same client and standards (or a newer approved edition, matched by stable key); applicability and N/A reasons, owners, units and visibility copied; last year's final results become the new board's baseline, so its dashboard shows "last audit vs now"; every assessment reset to Not started; open findings and actions carried over, tagged with the previous ref; last year's accepted evidence offered as suggestions, never auto-accepted. The previous board stays closed as history; each board keeps its own dashboard (D26). The ISO landing page lists maintenance audits due and overdue; no emails (scheduled email needs a cron, §13). Built as Phase 9b. | User, 2026-10-03 (internal maintenance audit; full scope; a date per completed board); design: Claude |
| D32 | **Senior review gives the verdict.** A second person reviews each requirement's assessment and gives the verdict: **Compliant / Partially compliant / Non-compliant** (a Not applicable row gets **N/A confirmed**), or **sends it back** with a note the assessor sees. The assessor's Result pre-suggests the verdict (implemented effective → Compliant; partially implemented or implemented ineffective → Partially compliant; not implemented or no evidence → Non-compliant); the reviewer may pick another. Who: the same people who may verify an action — admin, audit manager, or an auditor holding `can_verify`, which the board's access popup now shows and changes per person as "Senior reviewer" (not "Reviewer": that already names the read-only audit role). Separation of duties: nobody gives the verdict on an assessment they recorded (`assessed_by`, which now changes only when the state or result really changes). A verdict or a send-back drops back to pending as soon as the assessment changes. Bulk: accept each row's suggested verdict, or reset — never send back in bulk. Never shown to a client. The dashboard keeps counting the assessor's Result for now; a "verified" figure can come later. (User, 2026-10-03.) |
| D33 | **Staff comment thread inside the clause popup.** A Comments tab, oldest first, with names, times, Ctrl+Enter to post, labelled "Staff only — never shown to the client". Staff comments are stored internal by the server (comment.add) and comment.list never returns them to a client. (User, 2026-10-03.) |
| D34 | **Export column mapping (10.2) — reconciled with §11.** §11 already specified Work Status (a roll-up of confirmed linked actions), Priority (the highest priority among linked open actions) and Progress (mean of confirmed linked actions' progress, ÷100, default 0) in more detail than this decision first asked about — §11 stands for those three, unchanged. Two points §11 did not settle are decided here: **Compliance Status never has a "Not applicable" value** — 10.1's decoding of the real template found only 4 dropdown values (Fully/Partially/Not conforming, To be updated), not 5; §11's own text naming "Not applicable" as a value was written before the real file was decoded and is corrected by this row. A Not applicable requirement is excluded from the Register's data rows entirely (noted separately, never given a row with a status the dropdown doesn't offer). **Original Status is blank on a board with no `previous_audit_id`** (§11's "same mapping on baseline_result" pre-dates D31/maintenance audits and never considered a board with no baseline at all, where every row's baseline_result is null); on a maintenance board, the requirement's own `baseline_result` run through the Compliance Status mapping, as §11 already said. User, 2026-10-04. |
| D35 | **ISO board redesign approved** (`docs/iso-audit/redesign-proposal.md`, `docs/iso-audit/redesign-wireframes.html`): 4 tabs (Overview, Clauses, Documents, Actions) replacing 7; one status per clause (Compliant/Partially Compliant/Non-Compliant/OFI/N/A) replacing the 7 status-like fields; clauses expand in place (no 6-tab popup); library-shipped cross-standard relationships (Column P, 407 pairs) with "Apply to related"; document-first evidence (Documents tab, add-any-document chooser, custom documents); Lead Auditor bulk sign-off per standard/section with per-row return; "Senior Auditor" renamed "Lead Auditor" everywhere; v3 replaces the built-in library, old seeds archived (not deleted). Full problem-by-problem reasoning, before/after workflows and the remove/merge/hide/redesign list are in the proposal; not repeated here. | User, 2026-10-04 (iso-compliance-architect skill; 4 review rounds) |
| D36 | **OFI exports as Fully conforming**, with "OFI:" prefixed to the Current System text (the register's export vocabulary has no OFI value). | User, 2026-10-04 (Q1, recommended) |
| D37 | **Phase 10.2's export keeps the 10.1 template** (13-column single-standard gap-analysis workbook) — **not** v3's one-workbook/three-register/A–P layout. | User, 2026-10-04 (Q2 — overrides the architect's recommendation) |
| D38 | **Findings are dropped as separate records.** A clause's status (Partially Compliant/Non-Compliant/OFI) plus its Planned Action *is* the finding; the Findings tab and `iso_findings`-style records go. Formal finding references/classification are generated only when a report needs them (Phase 11, not yet built). | User, 2026-10-04 (Q3, recommended) |
| D39 | **Linking a document is the evidence review** for staff-added documents — no separate accept/reject verdict step. Verdicts return only for documents a client uploads once the portal exists (Phase 13). | User, 2026-10-04 (Q4, recommended) |
| D40 | **Document generator (Phase 8c) re-keyed and scoped:** the 20 templates move from the 2015 workbook's clause `stableKey` to v3 **document names** (18 of 20 map cleanly). **Corrective Action Form** and **Training and Induction Record** have no clean v3 document match and stay available from a standalone template list in the Documents tab, not attached to any document. **Custom documents** (added via "Add '…' as a new document", not in the v3 catalogue) go straight into the **shared library** — visible as a suggestion on every audit, not just the one that added it — so the catalogue grows from real use; no separate review step before that happens. | User, 2026-10-04 (Q5 "keep but unlinked", Q6 "yes everywhere") |
| D41 | **No bulk "mark all ticked Compliant"** in the add-document panel. Every ticked clause shows its 5 status buttons and a pre-filled evidence note (`Evidence — <document>, <revision> · <date>`) directly on the row — marking a status is one click, but it is always a deliberate per-clause choice, never a bulk default. | User, 2026-10-04 (Q7 + follow-up, "prefilled: buttons on row + note filled") |
| D42 | **The multi-edition "what changed" diff and edition picker are removed, not kept dormant or re-based on v3.** The old library let a drafted amendment/new-edition sit "based on" an already-approved edition, with a per-clause diff shown to auditors and a picker when a board could choose between two live editions (`iso-editions-test.cjs`). v3 fully replaces the library rather than amending it, so there is nothing left to diff against. The underlying version/edition mechanism (`iso_standard_versions`, `pickApprovedVersion`, pinning a board to an edition) stays in the server untouched, ready for a real future second edition (e.g. ISO 45001:2027, already on the waiting list); only the diff display and the now-pointless two-edition picker UI and their test coverage go. | User, 2026-10-04 (recommended) |
| D43 | **The 3 v3 editions load as `approved`, not `draft`.** The old library's three *base* editions (9001:2015, 14001:2015, 45001:2018) shipped approved so a board could always be created; only later amendments/new-year drafts, written from secondary summaries, were held back pending a licensed-standard check. v3 is Etcher's own working register (not a secondary-source draft) carrying the same "not ISO text, check the licensed standard" caveat the approved 2015 base always carried — so it follows the base-edition precedent, not the amendment one. Loading all three as draft left **no approved library at all**, so `audit.create` had nothing to create a board against — this broke board creation everywhere and crashed most of the ISO suites (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'id')`) the moment R1 ran the gate; found and fixed before R1 closed. | User, 2026-10-04 (recommended; reverses an earlier unasked choice made mid-R1) |

Export unchanged in meaning: Compliant+OFI → Fully conforming + "OFI:", Partial+OFI → Partially conforming + "OFI:". Refines the 5 statuses confirmed 2026-10-04 — the visible buttons do not change. (Q1, B1.3) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |

(Q2, B1.4) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D46 | **Every conclusion needs a basis** (the "Current system" text) before approval, board close and report issue — not before clicking a status. Pre-filled when concluding from the add-document panel. (Q3, B1.1) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D47 | **Apply to related proposes, never decides.** It copies the source's live documents, basis and planned action; each target status arrives *derived — confirm* (approval proposed) for everyone, Lead and sole auditor included; one click confirms. (Q4, B1.5) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D48 | **Gap reason on Partially Compliant / Non-Compliant:** Not implemented · Implemented but ineffective · No evidence provided — required with the basis for approval. Covers "insufficient evidence" without a new status. (Q5, B1.2) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D49 | **Track this action** creates a real action (existing lifecycle) and, on formal audit types only (internal, surveillance, recertification, certification prep, follow-up), a finding record classified from the existing configurable list; gap analyses get actions only. Findings are never merged because action text matches. (Q6, B2.1) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D50 | **A finding closes only when every linked action is closed and verified effective;** a closed finding can be reopened by the Lead Auditor with a reason. (Q7, B2.3–B2.4) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D51 | **The audit programme is the client's chain of boards** — no new programme record — plus a small per-client settings record (next audit due, frequency, industry, notes). (Q8, B4.4–B4.5) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D52 | **A follow-up audit is the maintenance flow plus a scope choice**; unselected clauses become Outside this audit's scope; audit type `follow_up`; a Lead Auditor may start one (fixing `audit.startMaintenance`, which omitted lead_auditor). (Q9, B4.2, B2.6) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D53 | **Recurrence sets the next audit due date and reminders; it never creates boards.** (Q10, B4.5) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D54 | **Reports:** three templates (audit, follow-up, maintenance), PDF via jsPDF, a frozen snapshot per issue, acknowledgement worded "received and discussed", never "agreed". **Notifications:** in-app only in release 1; due reminders computed when the bell is read (no scheduler); email arrives with the client portal. (Q11, B5) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D55 | **Local checkpoint commits** on branch `iso-release-1` at each batch close, never pushed without the user; the test suites are tracked in git (`.agents/*-test.cjs`, the shared harness and helpers, `gate.sh`) — not scratch, fix scripts or golden captures. (Q12, B0.1) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |
| D56 | **Client industry is a fixed list:** Mining & resources · Construction · Manufacturing · Transport & logistics · Professional services · Government · Other. (Q13, B4.5) | User, 2026-10-06 ("accept all" on release-1-completion-plan.md §2) |

## 3. Task Hub facts you are building on (verified 2026-09-29 — re-check anchors, lines drift)

### 3.1 Architecture
- Front end: one file `index.html` (~25.5k lines). Data = `window.DB`, one JSON blob in Supabase
  row `app_state` (id=1), read/written by staff with compare-and-swap. Clients never touch it;
  they use `api/portal-data.js` / `portal-action.js` / `portal-file.js`.
- Relational tables from migrations 001–004 exist but are **empty and unused**. Do not use them.
  `db-sync.js` is dead code. ISO starts fresh at migration `009`.
- RLS helpers that work today: `is_internal()`, `auth_user_role()`, `auth_user_client_id()`.
  `can_read_board()` / `client_can_access()` read empty tables → always false. Don't use them.
- Roles: DB enum `admin, pm, staff, client`. Front end also uses `restricted`.
  **Known bug, out of scope:** `_acBlockedViews` (index.html:5841) returns `'*'` for `pm`, so pm
  users are blocked from every view. ISO inherits this; mention it to the user, don't fix it.
- Identity: `currentUser.id` is the **DB.users legacy id** (`u1`…); servers see the **auth uuid**
  (`caller.id`). Map with `_staffAuthUid(s)` (index.html:2841) / `DB.users[].supabase_uid`.
  **ISO stores auth uuids** (offline: `offline:<DB.users id>`).
- **People mapping:** `ISO.userToUid(u)` / `ISO.uidToUser(uid)` in `iso/iso-core.js` (offline
  `offline:<id>`; Supabase mode via `_staffAuthUid`). Staff without a `supabase_uid` cannot be ISO owners
  yet — pickers show them disabled ("has not signed in yet"). Every list response includes
  `people:{<uid>:{name,initials}}` resolved server-side (staff from the blob's `users`, clients from
  `profiles`), so the portal never needs `DB.staff`.
- Staff → server calls: `fetch('/api/x',{headers:{...await _adminApiAuthHeaders()}})` (index.html:3080).
- Server helpers: `api/_authAdmin.js` (`requireStaffCaller`, `requireClientCaller`, …),
  `api/_storage.js`, `api/_email.js`, `api/_blob.js`. House style: CommonJS, zero npm deps, raw
  `fetch`, 405 → auth → validate → pure rules in `_x.js` → I/O; errors `{error:'<sentence>'}`;
  `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- `serve.mjs` runs real `api/<name>.js` handlers locally, but only flat names
  (`/^[a-z0-9-]+$/`) — **no nested `api/iso/*` routes**. Offline mode passes no Supabase env, so
  handlers answer "Server not configured" unless given an offline path (Phase 0).
- Vercel: **11** routed functions today — `config`, `intake/pending`, `invite-user`, `notify-email`,
  `oauth/[...path]` (also serves `/api/mcp` and both `/.well-known/*` via `vercel.json` rewrites),
  `portal-action`, `portal-data`, `portal-file`, `set-password`, `share-view`, `upload-state`. Hobby plans
  cap at 12. **ISO adds exactly one: `api/iso.js` — the 12th, so there is no headroom left afterwards**
  (A2). Request bodies are capped at 4.5 MB on Vercel (6 MB locally) → evidence files go direct to
  Storage via signed upload URLs.
- CSP (index.html:25 meta tag and serve.mjs:78–101): scripts from `'self'`, cdnjs, jsDelivr;
  `connect-src` hard-codes the production Supabase host.
- Role gates worth knowing: `_STAFF_BLOCKED` (5838) = capacity, reports, audit, staff, **clients** — staff
  cannot open the Client Manager; restricted add `notes` (5840). Timesheets (`time`) and Digest are open to
  admin + staff. Time approval and pro forma creation are admin-only by explicit checks inside the
  functions (11189…11351, 11973).
- `serve.mjs` today: `PORT = 3001` hard-coded (line 8), `listen(PORT)` on every interface (311), and the
  static handler serves any file under the root including dotfiles, `.env` and `appdata.json` — Phase 0.2b
  and 0.3b still apply.
- Every tracked file is publicly served (no build step). Never put client data or secrets in
  tracked files.

### 3.3 Things that do NOT exist (ISO must build them)
Per-object permissions (only global roles), organisation entity, configurable workflows,
server-side numbering (all numbers today are browser max+1), a tamper-proof audit log (today's is
in the blob, capped at 1,000, editable by staff), hash routing, a reusable people/client picker,
a chart library, a styled-xlsx template pipeline.

In Task Hub, parse **in the browser** with the already-vendored `vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.full.min.js` and send rows to the server. |

### 3.5 Already improved — do not regress (R3)
Every row is a fix or behaviour the user already asked for. ISO hooks and the Phase 1–2 refactors must keep
each one working; the named suites must stay green on every gate run (gate = 50 suites, 1247 passes at
`8863987`).

| Improvement | Pattern to follow / keep | Guarded by |
|---|---|---|
| Deletes never come back from a stale peer | Every deletable blob collection records ids with `_tombstoneRemoved(before, after, 'deleted<X>Ids')` and `_mergeDB` filters them (15 keys today: boards, checklist items, clients, comments, dependencies, groups, internal comms, meeting notes, note sections, notifications, task files, tasks, time entries, users, work requests). ISO adds **no** blob collection, so it adds no key — but any hook that deletes blob records must use this. | `merge-test`, `comms-notes-test`, `board-delete-test` |
| Inline handlers survive quotes | Never interpolate raw values into `onclick="…"`; use `escHtml(JSON.stringify(x))` or `data-*` + `this.dataset`. | `comment-reply-test` |
| No blank screens | Layout-matched skeletons (`._sk`, `._skl`; boot `#login-skeleton`) instead of spinners; ISO views render a skeleton from `ISO.render` while data loads (§4.6a). | `boot-skeleton-test`, `layout-test` |
| Notification click lands on the row | `notifGoToTask` jump-to-row (open board, clear filters, expand parent, scroll, `ws-row-jump`, then panel). ISO rows do the same through `ISO.openRecord` (7.6). | `notif-test` |
| Pro forma never edits time entries | Pro forma line edits (`pfiSaveLineField`, `pfiSyncAll` pulls one way) never write to `DB.timeEntries`; approved entries are payroll truth. | `pf-import-test` (+ the new 7b.4 test) |
| Deleting a task keeps approved hours | Cascades drop only unapproved entries (16863 and three siblings). ISO entries have `taskId:null`, so no task cascade may touch them. | `board-delete-test`, `four-fixes-test` |
| One client per board, shown in the topbar | `boardClient`/`clientDisplayTitle`; the topbar shows the client inside a board. ISO boards show their client the same way. | `board-client-test`, `client-title-test` |
| Main Table behaviour | columns, resize (`etcher_ws_colmeta`), subitems, roll-ups, bulk bar (incl. "Move to board…"), sticky tabs | `mt-phase1…5-test`, `mt-colresize-test`, `subitem-rollup-test`, `tp-tab-sticky-test`, `tp-regress`, `triage-route-test` |
| Gantt and dates | sticky Gantt, roll-ups, popovers, calendar, typed dates, double-click day | `gantt-sticky-test`, `tp-rollup-test`, `timeline-*` (4), `date-typing-test`, `dblclick-day-test` |
