## 4. Target architecture

```
Browser (staff app / client portal)                         Server                          Storage
─────────────────────────────────────                       ──────                          ───────
index.html  ── minimal hooks ──► iso/iso-core.js  ──POST──► api/iso.js (dispatcher)
  Main Table (via adapter) ◄────  iso/iso-*.js                 ├─ api/_isoAuth.js   who is calling
  Gantt      (via adapter) ◄────                               ├─ api/_iso.js       pure rules: permissions,
  client portal tab ◄──────────  iso/iso-portal.js             │                    visibility, state machines,
  Excel export ◄───────────────  iso/iso-export.js             │                    validation
                                                               ├─ api/_isoCalc.js   calculation dictionary
                                                               ├─ api/_isoImport.js import diff/validation
                                                               └─ api/_isoDb.js ──► _isoDbSupabase.js ─► Postgres iso_* (RLS deny-all)
                                                                                 └► _isoDbOffline.js  ─► .iso-offline/store.json
                                                                   signed upload/download URLs ───────► bucket iso-evidence (private)
```

### 4.1 One endpoint
- `api/iso.js`: `POST {op:'<name>', ...payload}`. An op registry maps each op to
  `{allow: (caller, ctx) => bool, validate, run}`. Unknown op → 400. Every op resolves the audit
  first, then checks the caller against that audit (§6), then runs.
- Reads and writes both go through it (like the portal). This brings Vercel to 12 functions — exactly
  the Hobby cap (§3.1) — and gives one place to enforce rules. Never add a second ISO function; new
  needs become ops.
- Responses: `{ok:true, data}` or `{error:'<plain sentence>'}` with 400/401/403/404/409/429/500.
  Rate limit client-caller ops like `_portal.js` (20/min, 500/day, counted from `iso_activity`;
  `evidence.uploadUrl` and `evidence.downloadUrl` count too).
- **Every id in a payload is resolved**, not just the audit: linked evidence, findings, requirements,
  actions, relationships, register items, `document_id`, `superseded_by`, `source_finding_id`, … must
  have the same `audit_id` as the resolved audit (register records: the same `client_id`), otherwise 404.
  If both `audit_id` and a record id are sent, `record.audit_id` must equal it.
- **Every uid is checked:** a client uid needs profile role `client`, `client_id = audit.client_id` and
  active; a staff uid needs role admin/pm/staff. `member_kind` comes from the profile, never the payload.
  A client caller's capabilities are capped at client_admin/client_contributor whatever `audit_role` says.
- **Each op builds its patch from its own field list** — never spread the payload. The server sets
  `client_id` / `audit_id` (copied from the parent row), `iso_client_responses.original_wording`, comment
  visibility (`client` for client authors) and email recipients (from profiles). `audit.client_id` is immutable.
- The op registry declares, per op, a **scope resolver** and a client-projection; `iso-security-test`
  (Phase 3) enumerates the registry, so an op without them fails the gate.

### 4.1a Op catalogue (the only ops that may exist — append here in the same session before adding one)
**Conventions:** names are `<entity>.<verb>`, entity singular. Every op takes `auditId` except the
audit-less ones: `standard.list`, `audit.list`, `audit.create`, `library.*`, `import.*` (library kind),
`template.*`, `config.*`, `notification.*`, `timeTarget.list`, `register.*` / `riskProposal.*` /
`digest.items` / `profile.*` (take `clientId`), `clientUser.list` (takes `clientId`), `promptTemplate.list`,
`guidance.list` (R11–R13 additions, planned 2026-10-02). Updates take `{auditId, id, patch, expectedVersion, reason?}` and return
`{ok:true, data:{row}}` with the new `row_version`. Lists take `{auditId, filter?}` and return
`{ok:true, data:{rows, people}}`.

| Entity | Ops |
|---|---|
| standard | list |
| audit | list · get · create `{clientId, standards:[codes], visibility, members:[{uid, auditRole}], templateId?}` · update · setAccess `{auditId, visibility, expectedVersion}` (owner/audit_manager/admin) · archive · restore · setStage |
| timeTarget | list → permitted, non-archived, stage ≠ closed audits the caller may log time to: `{auditId, ref, clientId, label, actions:[{id, ref, title}]}` (Etcher-owned open actions only) — for Timesheets (7b.2) |
| digest | items `{clientId, since}` → client-safe text lines for the Digest (7b.5) |
| member | list · add · update · remove |
| unit | list · add · update · archive |
| clientUser | list |
| requirement | list · get · update · bulkUpdate `{ids, patch, reason?}` · addCustom · transition `{id, toState, result?}` · confirmDerived `{ids}` (release-1 B1, D47: clears the "derived" mark from Apply to related; Lead/sole auditor approves on confirm when the basis is complete) |
| relationship | suggest · list · decide `{id, state, reasonText?}` · add `{fromId, toId, kind, reasonText}` (manual, saved as confirmed — 8b.1) |
| comment | list · add |
| activity | list |
| view | list · save · delete |
| template | list · createFromAudit · archive |
| config | get · set |
| metric | audit · portfolio |
| import | preview · apply · list |
| library | versions · approve · release `{auditId}` (release-1 B3.1: the board's library release + manifest) |
| finding | list · get · create · update · release · finalise · close · withdraw · linkRequirement · unlinkRequirement · reopen `{id, reasonCode, reasonText?, expectedVersion}` (release-1 B2.4: Lead/admin only, closed → reopened; close refused until every linked action is closed and verified effective, B2.3) |
| action | list · get · create · update · transition `{id, to, expectedVersion, reason?}` · submitForReview · decideReview · verify `{id, result, notes}` · reopen `{id, reasonCode, reasonText?}` · link · decideLink · trackPlan `{requirementIds, title?, ownerUid?, dueDate?, priority?, classification?}` (release-1 B2.1, D49: one action for the clauses + a finding per clause on formal audit types only) |
| subtask | add · update · archive |
| extension | request · decide |
| notification | list `{since?}` · markRead `{ids}` · dismiss `{ids}` |
| evidence | uploadUrl · register · addLink (built 8.2a; 8b.4 makes `url` optional for non-link kinds and adds `suggestRelated`) · link `{evidenceIds, targetType, targetId, role}` · search `{q?, kind?}` · suggestions `{targetType?, targetId?}` (derived, D21) · decideSuggestion `{evidenceId, targetId, decision, role?, comment?}` · review `{linkId, verdict, role?, comment?}` · supersede · downloadUrl · list (built 8.2a) · get · setSufficiency `{linkId, sufficiency: partial|insufficient|null, note?, expectedVersion}` (release-1 B1.7: staff-added evidence only — D35, the auditor's confirmation is the review; insufficient = not provided) |
| guidance | list `{versionIds}` → clause- and requirement-level guidance from the seed files (D25, 8b.3) |
| profile | get `{clientId}` · set `{clientId, key, value, confirmed, expectedVersion}` (D23, 8c.1) |
| promptTemplate | list → approved seed templates only (D23, 8c.2) |
| prompt | build `{auditId, requirementId, templateKey, mode, answers, options}` → `{text, filename, completeness, missing}` · saveAnswers · logExport `{kind: copy\|download, templateKey, templateVersion, sections}` (D22, 8c.3–8c.4) |
| evidenceRequest | create · update · close |
| export | dataset `{auditId, standard}` (release-1 B3.5: rows through EXPORT_FIELDS) · gapAnalysis `{auditId, standard}` (B3.5: the filled template, base64 + filename) · gantt `{auditId, clientVersion}` (7b.6; `clientVersion:true` applies the client projection and drops every non-client-visible action) |
| snapshot | list `{auditId}` (release-1 B3.2: immutable close snapshots, staff only — written by audit.update on close, never by an op of its own) |
| signal | export (release-1 B3.4: admin only — related_applied, doc_linked, evidence_suggestion_confirmed / _rejected) |
| report | list · create · update · generate · submitReview · approve · pdfUploadUrl · issue · newVersion · downloadUrl |
| register | list `{clientId, type}` · get · create · update · link · unlink |
| control | requestReview |
| riskProposal | create · decide |
| response | submit · decide · list |
| portal | summary · history |

`api/_iso.js` exports the registry; `iso-security-test` iterates it. The `_isoDb` interface is fixed as:
`get(table,id)`, `list(table,{scope,where,order,limit})`, `insertNumbered(table,kind,scope,row,activity)`,
`insert(table,row,activity)`, `update(table,id,patch,expectedVersion,activity)`,
`transition(table,id,column,from,to,activity)`, `log(activity)`, `counterNext(scope)`,
`importApply(runId,diff)`, `storageSignedUpload(path)`, `storageSignedDownload(path,name)`,
`storageHead(path)`, `storagePut(path,bytes,type)` (offline and import only), `storageRemove(path)`.

### 4.2 Data layer and the offline store
- `api/_isoDb.js` exports one interface (`get`, `list`, `insertNumbered`, `update`, `log`,
  `rpc`, …). It picks the adapter:
  - `process.env.ISO_OFFLINE === '1'` **and** `!process.env.VERCEL` → `_isoDbOffline.js`
    (JSON file `.iso-offline/store.json`, atomic temp-file+rename writes, the globalThis lock described below,
    emulates counters, append-only history, snapshot immutability, row versions).
  - otherwise → `_isoDbSupabase.js` (PostgREST + RPC with the service-role key). Refuses to start
    without `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`.
- Both adapters must pass the **same** contract suite (`.agents/iso-store-test.cjs` runs against
  offline; `.agents/iso-db-verify.cjs` runs the same cases against the dev project in Phase 15).
- `.iso-offline/` goes in `.gitignore`.
- **Offline concurrency:** `serve.mjs` drops every `api/` module from the require cache on each request,
  so a module-level mutex does not work. Use a process-wide lock on `globalThis.__isoOfflineLock` (a
  promise chain) and no module-level cache. Every write: await lock → read `store.json` fresh → mutate →
  write `store.json.tmp` → rename (retry up to 10× with 50 ms back-off on EPERM/EBUSY/EACCES — the repo is
  inside OneDrive) → release.
- **Offline store location:** `_isoDbOffline` reads its directory from
  `process.env.ISO_OFFLINE_DIR || '.iso-offline'`. `.iso-offline/` is the **user's review data** — no
  suite ever writes to, resets or reads it. Every ISO suite that writes uses a fresh temp store: in-process
  suites set `ISO_OFFLINE_DIR = fs.mkdtempSync(os.tmpdir()+'/iso-')`; Puppeteer and HTTP suites spawn
  `node serve.mjs --offline --port 31xx` with that env and kill it (and delete the dir) in `finally`.
  Absolute numbering assertions (`AUD-<year>-001`, `-002`) then hold on every run. On first init a store
  seeds the same default `iso_config` rows as migration 009.
- **PostgREST query building (Supabase adapter):** scope filters (`client_id` / `audit_id`
  `eq.<server-derived value>`) are always separate top-level params built with `URLSearchParams`; filter
  and sort columns come only from a per-entity allowlist; `select` is a fixed per-op string, never built
  from the request (no embedding); free-text search goes through a parameterised RPC
  (`iso_search(p_audit_id, p_q)`) or is rejected if it contains any of `,().:*\`; every returned row's
  `client_id`/`audit_id` is re-checked against the resolved scope before projection.

### 4.3 Who is calling — `api/_isoAuth.js`
- Supabase mode: validate the bearer token (reuse `requireAuthedCaller` logic), read `profiles`
  → `{uid, kind:'staff'|'client', role, clientId, name, active}`. Inactive → 403.
- **One function `isoOfflineMode()`** in `api/_isoAuth.js` returns true only when ALL hold:
  `globalThis.__TASKHUB_SERVE_OFFLINE === true` (set by `serve.mjs` only under `--offline`, never read
  from env); `process.env.ISO_OFFLINE === '1'`; `VERCEL`, `VERCEL_ENV`, `AWS_LAMBDA_FUNCTION_NAME` and
  `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are all unset. Adapter selection (§4.2), header trust and the 5.1b
  "offline ⇒ enabled" exception all call this one function.
- The header `X-ISO-Offline-User: encodeURIComponent(JSON.stringify({id:"u1",role:"admin",clientId:null}))`
  (encoded because `fetch` throws on non-Latin-1 header values; the server resolves names itself) →
  `uid:'offline:u1'` is honoured only when `isoOfflineMode()` is true **and**
  `req.socket.remoteAddress` ∈ {`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `::ffff:127.0.0.1`} (never read `X-Forwarded-For`)
  **and** `Host` ∈ {`localhost:3001`, `127.0.0.1:3001`, `[::1]:3001`} **and** `Origin` is absent or
  `http://localhost:3001` (defeats DNS rebinding). The front end sends it only when `window._supabase`
  is absent. This is also how UI tests impersonate clients (`role:'client', clientId:'c1'`).
- Tests make each condition false in turn and expect 401. Negative cases run **in-process** (require
  `api/iso.js` with a scrubbed `process.env` — every `SUPABASE_*` key deleted — and fake req/res); never
  spawn `serve.mjs` without `--offline` except with the fake env file from 0.3.
- **Restricted staff:** the `profiles` enum has no `restricted` (it exists only in the blob's `DB.users`
  and offline), so: offline = header role `restricted`; Supabase mode =
  `readBlob().users.find(u => u.supabase_uid === uid || u.id === uid)?.role === 'restricted'`. If the blob
  read fails, treat the caller as restricted (fail closed). Restricted callers may only hold the `reviewer`
  audit role and **never** receive `interview_notes`, `auditor_observations`, `internal_notes`,
  `verification_notes` or `auditor_review_note` — nor their before/after values in activity — for **any**
  client (Task Hub's `_RESTRICTED_BLOCKED` hides Client Notes from them entirely). `iso-logic-test` covers
  both resolution paths.

### 4.4 Database guarantees (Supabase)
- Every `iso_*` table: RLS **enabled with no policies** + `revoke all … from anon, authenticated`.
  Only the service role (i.e. `api/iso.js`) can touch them. Defence in depth; consistent with 005.
- **Functions are locked down too:** every `iso_*` function is `SECURITY INVOKER` with
  `set search_path = public`, followed by `revoke execute on function <full signature> from public, anon,
  authenticated; grant execute on function <full signature> to service_role;` (Supabase grants EXECUTE on
  new public functions to anon/authenticated by default — without this a logged-in client could call
  `/rest/v1/rpc/iso_update_row` directly). Revoke all on the `iso_activity` id sequence from anon and
  authenticated. Each Check section runs `has_function_privilege('authenticated'|'anon', '<sig>',
  'execute')` for every function — all must be false.
- **Atomic writes**: all creates/updates go through plpgsql functions that write the row **and**
  its `iso_activity` entry in one transaction (`iso_create_*`, `iso_update_row`, `iso_transition_*`).
- **`iso_update_row`** holds a hard-coded column allowlist per table and raises on any other key.
  Identifiers go through `format('%I')`, the table is chosen by a `CASE` over literal names, values via
  `jsonb_populate_record`. Never in any allowlist: `id`, `audit_id`, `client_id`, `ref`, `created_*`,
  `row_version`, `standard_code`, `library_requirement_id`, the frozen requirement copy, `storage_path`,
  `uploaded_by`. Status/state, verification, release, close and visibility columns change **only** through
  dedicated `iso_transition_*` functions that check the from-state (§6.1–6.2).
- **Optimistic concurrency**: every mutable table has `row_version int not null default 1`;
  `expected_version` is required (missing → 400); check and increment in one
  `UPDATE … WHERE id=$1 AND row_version=$2 RETURNING`; 0 rows → 409.
- **Append-only history**: `BEFORE UPDATE OR DELETE` row trigger **and** `BEFORE TRUNCATE` statement
  trigger on `iso_activity` raise (the service role bypasses RLS, so policies alone are not enough).
- **Immutable reports**: on `iso_report_versions`, once `approved_at` is set `snapshot`, `metrics`,
  `data_as_at`, `file_path`, `file_sha256` are immutable; once `issued_at` is set the whole row is
  immutable; DELETE always raises.
- **Immutable library**: a trigger rejects UPDATE on `iso_library_requirements` whose version is approved.
- **No hard deletes** of audit records: `BEFORE DELETE` triggers raise on every `iso_*` table except
  `iso_saved_views`, `iso_notifications` and upload reservations; use `archived_at` / `withdrawn` instead.
- Dates are Postgres `date` → strings `YYYY-MM-DD` (the Gantt parses them as UTC days).

### 4.5 Local dev and the dev project (Phase 0 and 15)
- `serve.mjs --offline` sets `process.env.ISO_OFFLINE='1'` for handlers.
- Add `--env-file <path>` to `serve.mjs` (default `.env`) so the dev project can use `.env.dev`.
  When not offline, rewrite the CSP `connect-src` Supabase host from `SUPABASE_URL` for both the
  header and the served `index.html` meta tag (**local serving only** — production files unchanged).
- Leave `.agents/gate.sh`'s live check as is (it requires offline — correct for the gate).

### 4.6 Front end placement
- New folder `iso/`, loaded from `index.html` with `<script src="/iso/iso-core.js" defer>` (and the
  other `iso/*.js` files) plus `<link rel="stylesheet" href="/iso/iso.css">`. CSP already allows `'self'`.
- Namespace everything under `window.ISO`. Call Task Hub globals (`escHtml`, `openConfirm`,
  `showToast`, `icon`, `avatarHtml`, the Main Table/Gantt adapters…) — never copy them.
- `ISO.api(op, payload)` wraps fetch with `_adminApiAuthHeaders()` or the offline header, handles
  409 (reload + toast "Someone changed this — showing the latest version"), 401/403, network errors.
- Per-audit UI state (sub-view, filters, grouping, column widths) in localStorage
  `etcher_iso_state_<auditId>`; the open board + tab in `sessionStorage.etcher_iso_route` (5.2); the ISO
  menu's expanded clients in `localStorage.etcher_iso_nav` (5.2b).
- After any ISO data change, call `_vcClear()` so Task Hub's 8-second view cache never serves stale ISO views.
- **Escaping:** all ISO text (requirement text, comments, evidence titles, file names, client responses,
  profile names, saved-view names, activity values) is rendered with `escHtml` (attributes too) or
  `textContent`. `_descToHtml` / `_descSanitize` are never used on ISO fields. SVG chart labels are escaped.
  URLs pass `_normalizeLinkUrl` on write and render as `data-url` + `escHtml` with
  `rel="noopener noreferrer"`, never inside an inline `onclick`. The Main Table and Gantt adapters assert
  `rowId` matches `/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$/` before rendering (the shared Gantt interpolates ids into inline
  handlers); ISO row ids are server-generated ids only — never `stable_key` or clause text.

### 4.6a Loading model (ISO data is async; Task Hub rendering is synchronous)
- `ISO.render(vc)` is synchronous and idempotent: it renders from `ISO.cache` or a skeleton, refetches
  only when the entry is missing or older than 30 s, and keeps the open drawer, scroll position and any
  inline edit. `renderView(currentView)` fires on every realtime blob merge (`_repaintAfterMerge`) and ~27
  other call sites — none of those may trigger a refetch.
- Per-audit entities load whole: `<entity>.list` returns all non-archived rows of one audit (server cap
  2,000 → 400 "Too many records"), cached as `ISO.cache[auditId][entity+':'+filterHash] = {rows, byId,
  loadedAt, v}`; `ctx.sections()` reads only the cache. Portfolio: `audit.list` returns every permitted
  audit (cap 500), no client-visible paging.
- Every async completion checks `ISO._routeSeq` and `currentView === 'iso'` before touching the DOM.
- After a write: replace the row with the server's returned row, bump `v`, `_vcClear()`, `ctx.rerender()`.
  On 409, refetch just that row and toast.
- Refresh on window focus and every 60 s while visible.
- Test (`iso-ui-test`): calling `_repaintAfterMerge()` five times triggers zero `/api/iso` requests within 30 s.

### 4.7 Cross-cutting reuse decisions
| Concern | Decision |
|---|---|
| Tables | Main Table via the Phase 1 adapter |
| Timelines | Gantt via the Phase 2 adapter (audit programme, action plan) |
| Comments | `iso_comments` (visibility internal/client). Render with the Task Hub comment styling. |
| History | `iso_activity` only. Do not use `writeAuditEvent`. |
| Notifications | `iso_notifications` table. Staff bell and portal bell **merge** ISO items (Phase 7/13). Email through `api/_email.js` server-side, with new ISO types. |
| Files | **SharePoint/web links first (D18)** — no storage used. Uploads are the exception: bucket `iso-evidence` (separate from `task-files`), signed upload URL issued by `api/iso.js` after permission checks, per-file cap `iso_config.evidence_max_upload_mb` (default 10, max 25) (flow in Phase 8.2); download via short signed URLs. Offline: base64 through `/api/iso` (≤4 MB raw, under serve.mjs's 6 MB body cap) stored as `.iso-offline/files/<evidence_id>` — no extension, never the client's filename — served only through the download op with `Content-Type: application/octet-stream`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `nosniff`. Previews reuse `wsPreviewFile`'s viewers (sanitised). |
| Time logging | **Ordinary Task Hub time entries** with the audit's client + `iso*` snapshot fields, logged from Timesheets' third mode "ISO audit" (D12, Phase 7b.2). No linked tasks. |
| Access | `iso_audits.visibility` + `owner_uid` + `iso_audit_members`, same options and modal look as workboards (D11); enforced by `api/iso.js`. |
| Billing | Task Hub-wide per-person **Billing** permission on the staff record (D16, Phase 7b.1); ISO hours reach Reports/Pro forma as ordinary entries. |
| Menu | An "ISO" section in the left menu, built like WorkBoards (header + "+" + live list), listing active clients with open ISO boards (R4/R5, Phase 5.1). |
| Numbering | Server/DB only (§8). |
| Charts | Hand-rolled SVG helpers in `iso/iso-charts.js`, reusing `cpProgressRing` and `statusMixBar`. |
| Export | ExcelJS (already used) loading the sanitised template (§11). |
| PDF | jsPDF + autotable via `_ensureJsPdf`, rendered from the frozen snapshot, never live data. |

---

## 5. Domain model and schema

Conventions for every table: `id text primary key` (server-generated, e.g. `iaud_<random>`),
`created_at timestamptz default now()`, `created_by text` (auth uuid), `updated_at`, `updated_by`,
`row_version int default 1` on mutable tables, `client_id text not null` wherever the record belongs
to a client (denormalised on purpose — every query is scoped by it), `archived_at timestamptz null`.
Blob client ids (`DB.clients[].id`, e.g. `c1`) are validated on write by reading the blob server-side
(`readBlob`) — there is no foreign key to the empty `clients` table.
Each migration file follows the 005–008 style: idempotent (`if not exists`), a **Check** section
(read-only queries) and a **Rollback** section. Sonnet writes them; the user applies them.

**Migration map** (all written by Sonnet, applied only by the user):
`009_iso_core.sql` (Phase 3 — everything in 5.1–5.3, incl. findings and actions, so imports and the
core loop never wait on a later migration) · `010_iso_evidence.sql` (Phase 8, §5.4) ·
`011_iso_reports.sql` (Phase 11, §5.5) · `012_iso_registers.sql` (Phase 12, §5.7) ·
`013_iso_client_responses.sql` (Phase 13, §5.6).

**Indexes (every migration, §29 of the brief):** (`client_id`, `audit_id`), (`audit_id`, `archived_at`),
(`audit_id`, `standard_code`, `clause`), status / `assessment_state`, `owner_uid` / `responsible_uid`,
`due_date` / `target_date`, `visibility`; `unique (audit_id, ref)`; `iso_activity (audit_id, at desc)`
and (`entity_type`, `entity_id`); every link table on (`target_type`, `target_id`). `_isoDbSupabase`
loads child rows with one `in.(…)` query per table — never one query per row (no N+1).

### 5.1 Migration 009 — core (Phase 3)

| Table | Purpose / key columns |
|---|---|
| `iso_standards` | `code` ('9001'), `name` ('ISO 9001'), `title` ('Quality Management System'), `system_abbrev` ('QMS'), `badge` ('Q'), `color`, `active`. Seed 9001/14001/45001; more can be added later without code changes. |
| `iso_standard_versions` | `standard_id`, `label` ('Etcher library v1'), `status` draft/approved/retired, `source_filename`, `source_sha256`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`. |
| `iso_clause_sections` | `version_id`, `clause` ('4', '5.1'), `title`, `level` 1 or 2, `sort_key`, `source_row`. |
| `iso_library_requirements` | `version_id`, `section_id`, `clause`, `clause_name`, `requirement_text`, `evidence_guidance`, `stable_key` = standard + clause + sha1(normalised text) + occurrence, `source_sheet`, `source_row`, `sort_key`. **Never edited once the version is approved.** |
| `iso_audits` | `ref` (unique, `AUD-2026-001`), `client_id`, `title`, `audit_type` (internal, gap_analysis, certification_prep, surveillance, recertification, supplier, custom), `custom_type_label`, `stage` (planning, fieldwork, reporting, capa, closed), `scope_text`, `exclusions_text`, `planned_start`, `planned_end`, `report_due`, `certification_body`, `lead_auditor_uid`, **`visibility` (`all_internal` | `private`, not null, default `all_internal`), `owner_uid` (the creator; not null)** (D11 — who may open the board; separate from `default_visibility`, which is about clients), `internal_notes`, `template_id` (nullable), `client_portal_enabled boolean not null default false`, `default_visibility` (default `internal`). No `workboard_id` (D12 — hours are ordinary time entries). An audit is **shared** with a client user only when `client_portal_enabled` is true **and** that user has a client membership row; changing the flag is audit_manager/admin only and logged. |
| `iso_templates` | `name`, `description`, `audit_type`, `config` jsonb, `archived_at`, `row_version` — **no `client_id` column**. `config` holds pinned version ids, per-`stable_key` applicability / `na_justification` / `audit_question` / `evidence_expected`, unit kinds and names, default saved-view configs, the finding-classification set and report section config. Built only from a whitelist; never contains client ids, client-member uids, notes, comments, evidence, responses or reports. |
| `iso_audit_standards` | `audit_id`, `version_id` — pins the library version, so historic audits never move. |
| `iso_audit_units` | `audit_id`, `kind` site/department/process, `name`. |
| `iso_audit_members` | PK (`audit_id`, `user_uid`); `member_kind` staff/client; `audit_role` (§6); flags `can_upload_evidence`, `can_respond`, `can_verify`; `added_by`, `added_at`. |
| `iso_requirements` | The **audit requirement instance** — columns in 5.2. |
| `iso_relationships` | Requirement to requirement: `from_id`, `to_id`, `kind` (same_requirement, integrated_topic, mapping), `state` suggested/confirmed/rejected, `reason_code`, `reason_text`, `source` rule/mapping/manual, `decided_by`, `decided_at`. |
| `iso_comments` | `audit_id`, `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `body`, `visibility` internal/client, `author_uid`. |
| `iso_activity` | bigserial id; `audit_id`, `client_id`, `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `entity_ref`, `action`, `field`, `before` jsonb, `after` jsonb, `reason`, `source` user/import/automation/portal, `actor_uid`, `actor_kind`, `at`. **Append-only trigger.** |
| `iso_counters` | `scope` text PK, `next_value` int (§8). |
| `iso_saved_views` | `owner_uid` (null = shared), `audit_id` (null = any audit), `entity`, `name`, `config` jsonb, `is_default`. |
| `iso_notifications` | `recipient_uid`, `audit_id`, `entity_type`, `entity_id`, `type`, `message` (safe text only — never internal notes), `read_at`, `dismissed_at` (the bell's ✕ and "Clear all"). |
| `iso_import_runs` | `kind` library/audit, `source_filename`, `sha256`, `file_path` (the uploaded bytes are kept), `target_version_id`, `target_audit_id`, `dry_run`, `summary` jsonb, `diff_sha256`, `backup` jsonb (pre-apply values of every row/field the apply changes, captured in the same transaction), `status` previewed/applied/failed, `performed_by`, `at`. |
| `iso_config` | `key`, `value` jsonb — `finding_classifications`, `gap_categories`, `fishbone_categories`, `number_prefixes` (`{audit:'AUD',finding:'F',action:'A',evidence:'E',report:'R'}`), `timezone` (`Australia/Perth`), `evidence_max_upload_mb` (10; admin may set 1–25, D18). 009 inserts these defaults; the offline store seeds the same rows on first init. (No scoring weights in release 1 — see §13.) |

### 5.2 `iso_requirements` columns

- **Identity:** `audit_id`, `client_id`, `standard_code`, `library_requirement_id` (null for custom rows), `section_id`, `sort_key`,
  `origin` library/client_specific/contractual/regulatory_legal (custom rows are added with
  `requirement.addCustom`, count in metrics and exports like library rows, and are never written to the library).
- **Frozen copy** from the pinned library version: `clause`, `clause_name`, `requirement_text`, `evidence_guidance`.
- **Applicability:** `applicability` applicable/review/not_applicable; `na_justification` (**required** when not_applicable); `unit_ids` text[].
- **Assessment (staff):** `audit_question`, `current_system`, `evidence_expected`, `interview_notes` (internal),
  `sampling_details`, `auditor_observations` (internal), `assessment_state`, `assessment_result`,
  `implementation_level` (0–4), `effectiveness` (not_assessed/effective/partly_effective/ineffective),
  `gap_description`, `gap_category` (from `iso_config`: documentation, implementation, effectiveness,
  evidence, competence_awareness, resources, other), `current_controls` (text; Phase 12 also shows linked
  register controls), `impact`, `risk_severity` (low/medium/high/critical), `recommended_action`,
  `responsible_uid`, `target_date`, `verification_requirements`, `internal_notes`,
  `assessed_at` / `assessed_by` (server-set whenever state or result changes).
- **Derived table columns (computed, never stored):** Evidence requested/submitted/accepted = counts of this
  row's evidence links by `review_status`; Finding type = highest classification of linked findings;
  Verification status = roll-up of confirmed linked actions; Related action numbers = confirmed action refs;
  Source version = pinned library version label; Auditor notes = `auditor_observations` (staff only).
- **Client-facing:** `client_visible_comment`, `visibility` (§6.3).
- **Baseline and source:** `baseline_result`, `baseline_state`, `baseline_at`, `imported` jsonb (read-only
  snapshot of workbook columns D/E/F/G), `source_sheet`, `source_row`, `stable_key`, `source_changed`,
  `missing_from_source`.

### 5.3 Findings and actions — also in migration 009 (import uses them in Phase 4; UI in Phase 7)

| Table | Key columns |
|---|---|
| `iso_findings` | `ref` (`AUD-2026-001-F001`), `audit_id`, `client_id`, `classification` (from `iso_config`; defaults major_nc, minor_nc, observation, ofi), `title`, `statement` (auditor wording), `criteria`, `evidence_basis`, `status` draft/released_for_review/final/closed/withdrawn, `visibility`, `released_at`/`released_by`, `closed_at`/`closed_by`, `root_cause_required` (true for major_nc). Unique (`audit_id`, `ref`). |
| `iso_finding_requirements` | (`finding_id`, `requirement_id`). |
| `iso_actions` | `ref` (`…-A001`), `audit_id`, `client_id`, `title`, `description`, `owner_kind` etcher/client, `owner_uid`, `start_date`, `due_date`, `priority`, `risk_rating`, `progress` 0–100, `status` (§6.2), `verification_status`, `verification_requirements`, `verification_notes`, `verified_by`/`verified_at`, `closure_decision`, `client_response` (client-visible; writable by the client owner or client_admin while status is assigned…more_info_required), `auditor_review_note`. CAPA: `correction`, `containment`, `root_cause_method` (none/five_whys/fishbone/cause_tree/other), `five_whys` jsonb (≤5 why/answer pairs), `fishbone` jsonb `{"<category>":["cause",…]}` (categories from `iso_config.fishbone_categories`, default People, Method, Machine/Equipment, Materials, Measurement, Environment), `cause_tree` jsonb `[{id,parent_id,text,kind:'effect'|'cause'|'root'}]` (server rejects cycles, >50 nodes, depth >6), `contributing_factors` jsonb `[{text,category}]`, `cause_categories` text[], `root_cause_summary`, `corrective_action`, `preventive_action`, `effectiveness_check`, `effectiveness_review_date`. Also `visibility`, `reopened_count`. (No `linked_task_id` — D12.) |
| `iso_action_subtasks` | `action_id`, `audit_id`, `client_id`, `title`, `owner_uid`, `due_date`, `done`, `sort_key`, `row_version`, `archived_at`. Not numbered; shown as child rows in the actions Main Table. |
| `iso_action_reviews` | `action_id`, `submission_no`, `submitted_by`, `submitted_at`, `submission_note`, `decision` accepted/rejected/more_info, `decided_by`, `decided_at`, `decision_note`. Written by submit-for-review and the auditor decision. With extension decisions and reopen entries this is the action's **approval history**. |
| `iso_action_links` | `action_id`, `target_type` finding/requirement, `target_id`, `link_state` suggested/confirmed/rejected, `decided_by`/`decided_at`. The 28 imported to-do actions arrive with **suggested** links. |
| `iso_action_extensions` | `action_id`, `requested_by`, `current_due`, `requested_due`, `reason`, `decision` pending/approved/rejected, `decided_by`/`decided_at`, `decision_note`. |

### 5.4 Migration 010 — evidence (Phase 8)

| Table | Key columns |
|---|---|
| `iso_evidence` | `ref` (`…-E001`), `audit_id`, `client_id`, `kind` (file, link, sharepoint, photo, meeting_note, interview, sample, policy_ref, procedure_ref, note), `title`, `description`, `storage_path`, `original_filename`, `mime`, `size_bytes`, `url`, `document_id` (no FK in 010 — `iso_documents` arrives in 012, which adds the constraint `not valid`), `version_label`, `evidence_date`, `owner_uid`, `confidentiality` normal/restricted, `visibility`, `superseded_by`, `review_or_expiry_date`, `uploaded_by`, `uploaded_via` staff/portal/import. |
| `iso_evidence_links` | `evidence_id`, `target_type` requirement/finding/action, `target_id`, `review_status` (requested, submitted, under_review, more_info_required, accepted, rejected, superseded), `reviewed_by`/`reviewed_at`, `reviewed_version_label`, `review_comment`. **Acceptance is per link**, so one file can be accepted for one requirement and still under review for another. The brief's "Restricted" status = `iso_evidence.confidentiality='restricted'`. Evidence is never hard-deleted in release 1 (retention policy deferred, §13). |
| `iso_evidence_requests` | `audit_id`, `target_type`, `target_id`, `description`, `requested_from_uid`, `due_date`, `status` open/submitted/closed. |

Storage: bucket `iso-evidence`, private, 25 MB hard limit; the app enforces the lower
`iso_config.evidence_max_upload_mb` (default 10) because links are the default (D18). Allowed types: pdf, docx, xlsx, pptx, csv, txt,
png, jpg, jpeg, gif, webp, heic, msg, eml, zip. No storage policies for authenticated users. Path
`<client_id>/<audit_id>/<evidence_id>/<safe filename>`. Never render HTML or SVG uploads inline.
No virus scanning exists — record it as a known limitation.

**R11–R13 amendments (planned 2026-10-02).** Migrations 009 and 010 are still unapplied, so these go
into the files directly, following the 009 precedent (A24). Make each change in the same session as
the task that first needs it, and say so in that task's A-entry.
- `iso_evidence_links.role text not null default 'primary' check (role in ('primary','supporting'))` (D20).
- `iso_evidence_links.review_status` check becomes `submitted, under_review, more_info_required,
  accepted, partially_sufficient, insufficient, rejected, superseded` (D20; `requested` dropped — requests
  live in `iso_evidence_requests`). `evidence.review` requires `review_comment` for `insufficient`,
  `more_info_required` and `rejected`.
- `iso_evidence.suggest_related boolean not null default true` — false = "use for this requirement only",
  which excludes the record from D21's derivation.
- `iso_evidence.document_status text not null default 'draft' check (document_status in
  ('draft','approved','superseded','obsolete'))`, plus `approver_uid text` and `effective_date date` —
  set at upload (8c.5) and editable later. A change to `document_status` is logged.
- 009: `iso_relationships.kind` check gains `shared_evidence` and `supporting` (D21: `supporting` never
  carries evidence suggestions). Manual relationships (`relationship.add`) use the existing
  `source='manual'` and are saved as `confirmed`.
- `metric.audit`'s evidence fields (A59) follow the new verdicts: `evidence_accepted` counts `accepted`
  only, plus new `evidence_partial`, `evidence_insufficient` and `evidence_suggested` (from the D21
  derivation); `evidence_submitted` = `submitted` + `under_review` + `more_info_required`.

### 5.5 Migration 011 — reports (Phase 11)

- `iso_reports`: `ref` (`…-R001`), `audit_id`, `title`, `status` draft/internal_review/approved/issued/superseded,
  `config` jsonb (selected sections).
- `iso_report_versions`: `report_id`, `version_no`, `snapshot` jsonb, `metrics` jsonb, `data_as_at`,
  `generated_by`/`at`, `approved_by`/`at`, `issued_by`/`at`, `recipients` jsonb, `file_path`,
  `file_sha256`, `supersedes_version_id`, `supersede_reason`. The snapshot includes the selected sections
  and all narrative text (so later edits to `iso_reports.config` never change an issued version).
  Triggers per §4.4: frozen content from `approved_at`, whole row from `issued_at`, no DELETE ever.
  The PDF is rendered from the approved snapshot and uploaded **after approve, before issue** to
  `<client>/<audit>/reports/<version_id>.pdf` through a one-time signed upload URL (no upsert);
  `report.issue` requires the object to exist with a matching `file_sha256`, then writes `file_path`,
  `issued_at`, `issued_by` and `recipients` in one statement. No code ever removes report objects.

### 5.6 Migration 013 — client responses (Phase 13)

`iso_client_responses`: `finding_id`, `audit_id`, `client_id`, `respondent_uid`;
factual review (`factual_status` confirmed/disputed, `factual_comment`, `suggested_corrections`);
management response (`position` accepted/disputed, `management_response`, `proposed_correction`,
`proposed_root_cause`, `proposed_corrective_action`, `responsible_manager`, `proposed_due_date`,
`resources_required`, `extension_requested_due`, `extension_reason` — on submit, one
`iso_action_extensions` row per linked action); `status` submitted/under_review/accepted/rejected; `auditor_decision`,
`auditor_decision_note`, `decided_by`/`at`; `original_wording` (finding statement at submission),
`final_wording`.

### 5.7 Migration 012 — registers (Phase 12)

All per client (`client_id not null`), numbered per client: `RSK-001`, `OBL-001`, `CTL-001`, `DOC-001`.

| Table | Key columns |
|---|---|
| `iso_risks` | title, description, category, likelihood 1–5, consequence 1–5, rating (computed), owner_uid, treatment, residual_likelihood, residual_consequence, status, review_date. |
| `iso_risk_proposals` | `risk_id` (null = propose a new risk), `source_finding_id`, `proposal` jsonb, `status` pending/approved/rejected, `decided_by`/`at`. **A finding never changes a risk directly.** |
| `iso_obligations` | title, `type` (legislation, regulation, licence_condition, code_of_practice, contract, client_requirement, internal_policy), jurisdiction, owner_uid, applicability, review_date, evaluation_result (compliant/partly/non_compliant/not_evaluated), evaluated_at, change_notes, `changed_since_evaluation` bool. Evidence of evaluation = register links to evidence; recording `evaluation_result` clears the flag. |
| `iso_controls` | title, objective, owner_uid, control_type, frequency, nature preventive/detective, mode manual/automated, required_evidence, test_procedure, last_test_result, last_tested_at, next_test_date, failed_flag, `review_requested_at`, `review_requested_by`, `review_reason`. `client_id` null = a library template control. |
| `iso_control_clauses` | `control_id`, `standard_code`, `clause` — maps one control to clauses of several standards (library and client controls). |
| `iso_documents` | doc_number, title, revision, effective_date, approval_status, owner_uid, next_review_date, superseded, url (SharePoint). |
| `iso_register_links` | `register_type` risk/obligation/control/document, `register_id`, `target_type` requirement/finding/action/evidence/risk/control/obligation/document (so risk↔control links work), `target_id`, `note`. |

### 5.8 Migration 014 — company profiles and prompt answers (Phase 8c, R12)

Written ahead of 011–013 (which stay reserved for Phases 11–13); it depends on none of them. Same
conventions as 009: RLS enabled with zero policies plus an explicit revoke, no hard delete,
`row_version` optimistic concurrency, every write logged to `iso_activity`.

| Table | Key columns |
|---|---|
| `iso_company_profiles` | `client_id` (primary key — one profile per client, D23), `fields` jsonb `{<key>: {value, confirmed, confirmed_by, confirmed_at}}`; list fields (operating locations, business activities, products and services, main processes, organisational and approval roles, interested parties, internal and external issues, quality considerations, environmental aspects, WHS hazards and risks, legal and other obligations) hold arrays of `{id, text, confirmed, confirmed_by, confirmed_at}` so each item is confirmed on its own; `row_version`, `updated_at`/`updated_by`. The logo is a reference (URL or SharePoint link), never an upload. |
| `iso_prompt_answers` | `id`, `audit_id`, `client_id`, `requirement_id`, `template_key`, `template_version`, `answers` jsonb, `row_version`, created/updated — the guided answers for one (board, requirement, template), so a user can come back to them. Unique (`audit_id`, `requirement_id`, `template_key`). |

**Seed files (R11–R13, not SQL — reviewed in the code, D23–D25):**
- `iso/seed/library/<standard>-<edition>.json` — `{standardCode, edition, label, sourceFilename,
  sourceSha256, sections:[{clause, title, level, sortKey}], requirements:[{clause, clauseName,
  requirementText, evidenceExamples, stableKey, sourceSheet, sourceRow, sortKey}]}`. The loader inserts a
  version as `approved` only if no version with that edition exists yet (idempotent), and never touches an
  existing approved version (009's immutability trigger stays the backstop).
- `iso/seed/guidance/<standard>-<edition>.json` — `{clauses:{<clause>:{explanation, auditorLooksFor,
  status, approvedBy, approvedAt}}, requirements:{<stableKey>:{…same fields…}}}`.
- `iso/seed/prompt-templates/<key>.json` — `templateId, version, title, purpose, documentType,
  supportsRequirements:[{standard, edition, stableKey, workbookRow}], requiredProfileFields,
  questions:[{id, text, kind:text|choice|checklist, options?, sourceRef?}], requiredSections,
  optionalSections, expectedRecords, docControlFields, brandingInstructions, draftingRules, reviewerChecklist,
  reviewStatus (draft|approved|retired), filenameStem, sources:[{title, publisher, url, checkedOn}]`. A
  checklist question can cite a source (for example a Safe Work Australia code's hazard list); each item
  the user ticks becomes a confirmed answer.

## 6. Status models, permissions and visibility

### 6.1 Requirement assessment — two fields, not one
The brief's single list mixed workflow, result and verification. Split it so "unassessed is never
conforming" holds by construction:

| `assessment_state` (workflow) | `assessment_result` (outcome, null until assessed) |
|---|---|
| not_started · in_progress · awaiting_evidence · needs_follow_up · complete | not_implemented · no_evidence_provided · partially_implemented · implemented_ineffective · implemented_effective · not_applicable |

Rules (server-enforced): `assessment_result` may only be non-null when state is `complete` or
`needs_follow_up`; `not_applicable` requires `applicability='not_applicable'` **and** a non-empty
`na_justification`; "Verified and closed" is not an assessment value — it lives on actions/findings.
Completing an action **never** changes an assessment. The UI shows, after an action is verified,
"3 linked requirements may need reassessment" — the auditor changes them deliberately.

**Workbook → module mapping (import, Phase 4):**
| Workbook STATUS (col E) | `baseline_result` / `assessment_result` | `assessment_state` |
|---|---|---|
| Fully conforming | implemented_effective | complete |
| Partially conforming | partially_implemented | complete |
| Not conforming | not_implemented | complete |
| To be updated | null | needs_follow_up |
| (blank / unknown) | null | not_started + import warning |

### 6.2 Actions, verification, findings, evidence, relationships
- **Action status:** draft → assigned → in_progress → awaiting_evidence → submitted_for_review →
  more_info_required ↺ → ready_for_verification → verified → closed, plus `on_hold` (kept because
  the export template has "On hold"). Allowed transitions are a table in `api/_iso.js`; anything
  else → 400.
- **Verification status:** not_verified · effective · ineffective. Only the audit_manager, or an
  auditor holding `can_verify`, may set it or move an action to verified/closed (§6.4). Client callers
  never can. Setting `ineffective` returns the action to in_progress.
- **Reopen** (verified/closed → in_progress): auditor/manager only, `reason` required, increments
  `reopened_count`, logs activity. Allowed reasons: evidence_invalid, ineffective, recurred,
  connected_requirement_changed, later_audit_found, closed_in_error, other (text required).
- **Finding classification** default list (configurable via `iso_config`): major_nc, minor_nc,
  observation, ofi. ("Conforming", "Not applicable", "Evidence required" are *not* findings —
  they are assessment results / evidence requests.) Changing major_nc → anything lower requires a reason.
- **Major NC rule:** a major_nc's actions cannot move past `submitted_for_review` unless
  `root_cause_summary` is ≥ 40 characters **and** is not just a generic cause (reject when the
  normalised text is only "human error", "operator error", "staff error", "mistake", "forgot" or
  similar with nothing else), `root_cause_method` is not `none`, and at least one `cause_categories`
  value is set.
- **Evidence link review:** requested → submitted → under_review → (more_info_required ↺ | accepted |
  rejected); superseded when replaced. Uploading sets `submitted`, never `accepted`.
- **Relationships and action links:** suggested → confirmed | rejected. A suggestion changes nothing
  else; confirmation records who and when; rejected suggestions are never re-suggested by re-import.

### 6.3 Visibility levels (per record: requirement, finding, action, evidence, comment, report)
`internal` (default) · `client_selected` (+ `visible_to_uids` text[]) · `client_all` ·
`after_approval` (**findings only** — visible to the audit's client members once `released_at` is set;
rejected with 400 on any other entity) · `report_only` (appears only inside an issued report; never in a
live op). Changing visibility after release requires a reason and is logged.
Every visibility-bearing table (requirements, findings, actions, evidence, report versions) has
`visibility` and `visible_to_uids text[] not null default '{}'` (only client uids of that audit's client).
Comments allow only `internal` | `client_all`. Evidence is visible to a client only through a visible link
target. `audit.default_visibility` is copied onto new records at creation and never re-applied.

**Client callers receive only an explicit per-entity `CLIENT_FIELDS` allowlist** in `api/_iso.js`
(the `_portal.js` "whitelists only" pattern — never a blocklist). A nested item is returned only when it
and its parent are both client-visible; references to invisible records are dropped, not blanked. uids
become display names; `visible_to_uids`, relationships, saved views, import runs, member flags and
`verification_notes` are never sent to clients; client metrics are computed only over client-visible,
non-draft records; report ops for clients return only issued versions (PDF URL + a `CLIENT_FIELDS`
projection of the snapshot). Fields that are never in any client allowlist include:
`interview_notes`, `auditor_observations`, `internal_notes`, internal comments, `verification_notes`,
`auditor_review_note`, restricted evidence (unless explicitly `client_selected`), draft findings, unissued reports.

**History and logs are filtered too.** `activity.list` and every history shown in drawers or the portal
is visibility-filtered on read: a client caller gets only entries whose entity is currently visible to
them; entries whose `field` is internal, or that belong to restricted evidence or internal comments, are
dropped; `before`/`after` of internal fields come back null for client callers and for `restricted` staff
who cannot see that client's notes; `reason` text on staff-only actions is dropped for clients. The same
filter applies to notifications, metrics filters, relationship suggestions, exports and report
snapshots. Server `console.*` output prints only the op name, ids and error text — never payloads, note
text or restricted-evidence file names.

### 6.3a Reason-required changes
Enforced in `api/_iso.js` on **every** path (single update, bulk edit, Gantt drag, portal op, extension
decision). Missing reason → 400 "A reason is required to …"; stored in `iso_activity.reason`:
1. changing `verification_status` once it is set; 2. reopening a verified/closed action; 3. downgrading
a major_nc; 4. any `due_date` change on an overdue action; 5. any visibility change after `released_at`
or issue; 6. superseding a report. `member.*` changes always log before/after role and flags.

### 6.4 Roles → permissions
Global role gates entry; audit membership gates each audit.

| Capability | admin | audit_manager | auditor | contributor | reviewer | client_admin | client_contributor |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| See audit | all | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | own client, shared | own client, shared |
| Create audit | ✓ | (any staff of global role admin/pm/staff may create; creator becomes audit_manager) | | | | | |
| Edit scope, members, settings | ✓ | ✓ | | | | | |
| Assess requirements | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | | | |
| Create/edit findings; release for review | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | | | |
| Create/edit actions | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | own actions | | assign client owners* | own actions |
| Upload evidence | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | ✓* | ✓* |
| Review/accept evidence | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | | | |
| Verify / close / reopen | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (if `can_verify`) | | | never | never |
| Approve extension | ✓ | ✓ | | | | | |
| Report: create/generate | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | | | |
| Report: approve/issue | ✓ | ✓ | | | | | |
| Factual review / management response | | | | | | ✓ | ✓* |
| Registers edit (client's) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | | | |
| Import library / approve version | ✓ | | | | | | |
| Read-only everything permitted | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | | |
`*` = only when the member flag allows it (`can_upload_evidence`, `can_respond`, and for client_admin
assigning client owners `can_manage_client_actions`). `restricted` staff: see §4.3. A client caller must
have `profiles.client_id = audit.client_id`, the audit's `client_portal_enabled` **and** a membership row —
all checked on every op.

**Unambiguous rules for encoding the matrix:**
- **Who may open an ISO board (staff, D11 — mirrors `canViewBoard` 5070):** admin always (acts as
  audit_manager); the `owner_uid`; any `iso_audit_members` staff row; and, when `visibility='all_internal'`,
  every other active admin/pm/staff/restricted user. Anyone else → 403 (and the board is absent from
  `audit.list`, the ISO menu, `timeTarget.list`, Reports and Digest items).
- **Effective ISO role:** the member row's `audit_role` if one exists; else owner → audit_manager; else a
  non-member on an `all_internal` board → **contributor** (restricted → **reviewer**) — the workboard
  equivalent of "everyone with access can work on it", minus the audit-integrity actions (assess,
  findings, verify, approve), which need a named role.
- **Changing access** (`audit.setAccess`, `member.*`): owner, audit_manager or admin. Removing the last
  audit_manager is refused (400). Switching to `private` never removes existing members.
- **Verify / close / reopen:** audit_manager always; auditor only with `can_verify`; nobody else, even with
  the flag. Client callers never.
- **Default member flags** `[can_upload_evidence, can_respond, can_verify, can_manage_client_actions]`:
  audit_manager `[1,0,1,0]` · auditor `[1,0,1,0]` · contributor `[1,0,0,0]` · reviewer `[0,0,0,0]` ·
  client_admin `[1,1,0,1]` · client_contributor `[1,1,0,0]`.
- **Allowed audit_role by global role:** admin/pm/staff → any staff role; restricted → reviewer only;
  client → client_admin or client_contributor only.
- **Registers (client-scoped):** staff may read client X's registers if admin or a member of any
  non-archived audit of X; edit only as audit_manager/auditor on such an audit. Clients have no register
  access in release 1.

Security invariant (tested in Phase 15): a caller linked to client A can never obtain client B's data
by changing a URL, audit id, client id, query parameter, API body field, file/evidence id, export
request or report id. Every op re-derives `client_id` from the stored record, never from the request.

---

## 7. Calculation dictionary (`api/_isoCalc.js`, pure CommonJS)

One module computes every number shown in dashboards, graphs, exports (cached cell values) and report
snapshots. Each metric returns `{key, value, numerator, denominator, filter}` where `filter` is a
saved-view config that reproduces exactly the records counted — the UI uses it to make every number a
link. Rounding: percentages to whole numbers, half up, computed from exact counts; `data_as_at` = server
time of computation, always displayed. Archived records are excluded everywhere.

| Metric | Formula | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `req_total` | requirements in scope | |
| `req_applicable` | req_total − result/applicability not_applicable | N/A never in denominators below |
| `req_assessed` | applicable with `assessment_state='complete'` and result not null | |
| `assessment_completion_pct` | req_assessed / req_applicable | |
| `req_conforming` | result = implemented_effective | |
| `conformance_pct` | req_conforming / req_applicable | Unassessed stay in the denominator → never counted as conforming. **Matches the export's "Overall conformance".** |
| `req_by_result` | counts per result + "unassessed" | |
| `req_with_accepted_evidence` | distinct applicable requirements with ≥1 evidence link `accepted` | |
| `findings_by_class`, `findings_open`, `findings_closed` | status not in (closed, withdrawn) / = closed | |
| `actions_total`, `actions_by_status` | | |
| `actions_completed` | status in (ready_for_verification, verified, closed) | "completed" ≠ verified |
| `actions_verified` | verification_status = effective | |
| `actions_overdue` | due_date < today and status not in (verified, closed) | |
| `evidence_requested` / `_submitted` / `_accepted` | counts of **links** by review_status | submitted ≠ accepted |
| `avg_action_progress` | mean `progress` of non-closed actions | |
| `baseline_vs_current` | req_by_result on baseline vs current | "Original position compared with current" |
| `req_by_state` | counts per `assessment_state` | assessment status distribution |
| `actions_open_vs_closed` | open = status not in (verified, closed) | |
| `actions_by_owner` | open actions per owner (etcher/client split) | |
| `overdue_by_unit`, `findings_by_unit` | attributed through linked requirements' `unit_ids`, counted once per unit | actions/findings have no unit of their own |
| `risk_distribution` | requirements by `risk_severity` × linked finding classification | the audit "heat map"; the 5×5 likelihood × consequence map is only for the Phase 12 risk register |
| `shared_actions` | actions with confirmed links to requirements of ≥2 standards | |
| `responses_awaiting_review` | client responses with status submitted/under_review | added in Phase 13 |
| **Portfolio:** `audits_active` | not archived, stage ≠ closed | |
| `audits_due_soon` / `audits_overdue` | `planned_end` or `report_due` within 14 days / in the past, stage ≠ closed | |
| `report_deadlines_upcoming` | active audits ordered by `report_due` | |
| `progress_by_client`, `progress_by_audit` | mean `assessment_completion_pct` of active audits | |
| `workload_by_auditor` | audits led + open assigned actions + unassessed requirements assigned | |
| `findings_by_standard`, `audits_by_stage` | | |
| Per clause section / site / department / process / owner / standard | the same metrics grouped | |

**Filter schema** (shared by saved views, metric `filter`s and dashboard links):
`{entity, where:[{field, op:'eq'|'in'|'lt'|'lte'|'gt'|'isnull'|'notnull'|'has_link', value}], group, sort}`.
- `has_link` value = `{entity:'action'|'finding'|'evidence_link'|'evidence_request'|'client_response',
  linkState:'confirmed' (default — suggested/rejected links never count), where:[…predicates on the linked row]}`.
- Date tokens `$today` and `$today+N` / `$today-N`. "Today" is the date in `iso_config.timezone`
  (default `Australia/Perth` — the user's time zone; Vercel runs in UTC), computed with
  `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA',{timeZone})`. The evaluator takes `now` as an argument (`_portal.js` style)
  so tests can pin it.
- Per-entity field allowlist; derived columns are filterable only through `has_link`.
- **One implementation**, `api/_isoFilter.js`, used by `_isoCalc` and every `<entity>.list {filter}`. The
  browser never re-filters; `group`/`sort` in a saved view only set the table's initial state.
- `iso-calc-test` runs one fixture at 23:30 UTC (07:30 Perth) and asserts `actions_overdue` equals the
  filtered list count.

**Phase ownership:** Phase 3 creates `_isoCalc.js` with the audit/portfolio counts that exist then;
Phases 7, 8, 11 and 13 each add their metrics (listed in their tasks). Phase 9 completes the dictionary doc.
Colours: one fixed colour per status token, defined once in `iso/iso.css` and used by every chart and badge.

Sonnet writes `docs/iso-audit/calculation-dictionary.md` from this table (metric, formula, included and
excluded statuses, N/A treatment, unassessed treatment, rounding, data date). `iso-calc-test.cjs` has a
hand-computed fixture for every metric, including edge cases (all N/A, zero requirements, only unassessed).

---

## 8. Automatic numbering

```sql
create table if not exists iso_counters (scope text primary key, next_value int not null);
create or replace function iso_next_number(p_scope text) returns int language sql as $$
  insert into iso_counters(scope, next_value) values (p_scope, 2)
  on conflict (scope) do update set next_value = iso_counters.next_value + 1
  returning next_value - 1;
$$;
```
- Audit: scope `AUD-<yyyy>` → `AUD-2026-001`. Format with `lpad(n::text, greatest(3, length(n::text)), '0')`
  — plain `lpad(…,3,…)` **truncates** 1000 to `100` and collides. Year = the current year in
  `iso_config.timezone` (default `Australia/Perth`) in both adapters. Test n = 1000.
- Children: scope `<audit_id>:F|A|E|R` → `<audit ref>-F001`, `-A001`, `-E001`, `-R001`.
- Registers: scope `<client_id>:RSK|OBL|CTL|DOC` → `RSK-001` etc.
- Called **inside** the `iso_create_*` functions, in the same transaction as the insert; `unique (audit_id, ref)`
  (and `unique (ref)` for audits) backs it up. Counters never decrease, records are never hard-deleted,
  so numbers are never reused. Moving, reassigning or editing never changes a ref. A shared action has one
  ref however many requirements it links to.
- Offline store: same scopes, serialised by the globalThis lock (§4.2).
- Prefix configuration (`iso_config.number_prefixes`) is admin-only and applies to new records only.
- Tests (Phase 3 uses `audit.create`; Phase 7 adds the same checks for `finding.create`/`action.create`):
  `iso-store-test.cjs` fires 50 concurrent creates at the offline store → 50
  unique, gap-free refs; `iso-api-test.cjs` sends 50 concurrent create **HTTP** requests through
  `serve.mjs --offline` (the in-process test cannot catch per-request module reloads) → 50 unique refs and a
  `store.json` that still parses; `iso-db-verify.cjs` repeats it on the dev project.

---

## 9. Phases

Each phase: do the tasks in order, add/extend the named test suites, run the **full** gate, append a
Progress Log line, update RESUME HERE. A phase is done only when every acceptance item is true.
Release 1 = Phases 0–15.

**Default acceptance for Phases 3–15** (in addition to any listed): every suite named in the phase is green,
gate TOTAL ≥ the previous phase's TOTAL with 0 failures, golden diffs still empty, and no new browser
console errors on the ISO views.

**Later-data rule:** a UI element whose data arrives in a later phase is not rendered until that phase
(hard rule 9). The later phase has an explicit task to add it — do not stub it earlier.

**Destructive confirms:** every archive, withdraw, reopen, supersede and reject uses `openConfirm` showing
**client · audit ref · record ref · impact** (e.g. "3 linked requirements will need reassessment").

      Return 404 for any path with a segment starting with `.`, and for `appdata.json`,

      `/.iso-offline/store.json`, `/.agents/gate.sh` all return 404 — check the **status only**, never print
      the body. Existing suites must stay green.
      **Done:** confirmed the exposure was live on this machine (`/appdata.json` returned 200 from the
      pre-fix code before the fix — real staff/client data, not hypothetical). Fixed via
      `_isBlockedStaticPath()` (dot-segment check + an explicit list for `appdata.json`,
      `intake-queue.json`, `workboard/`, `supabase/`, `docs/`) and binding `127.0.0.1` by default with a new
      `--lan` flag to opt back in. Guarded by `serve-security-test`.
- [x] 0.3 `serve.mjs`: add `--env-file <path>` (default `.env`). If `--env-file` is given and the file
      cannot be read, print an error and `exit(1)` — never fall back to `.env`. When not offline, build the
      CSP `connect-src` Supabase host from `SUPABASE_URL` and rewrite the host inside the served
      `index.html` meta tag (in memory, local serving only). Production files are unchanged. Verify 0.3 only
      with `--env-file <scratchpad>/fake.env` containing `SUPABASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9` — **never start
      serve.mjs non-offline with the real `.env`.**
      **Deviation (hard rule 12 — least destructive reversible option, logged under A18):** the flag is
      named **`--envfile`**, not `--env-file`. Node 24 has its own built-in `--env-file` CLI flag that
      intercepts the argument even when it appears after the script name — with a missing file it made
      `serve.mjs` exit 9 with Node's own message before a single line of `serve.mjs` ran (never reaching our
      `exit(1)` + custom message), and with a *valid* file it would have loaded it through Node's own parser
      as well as ours, a silent double mechanism with no guarantee the two parsers agree. Found and proven
      with a two-line repro before touching the real implementation. Guarded by `serve-security-test`.
- [x] 0.3b `serve.mjs`: add `--port <n>` (default 3001); serve `/iso/*` with
      `Cache-Control: no-cache, must-revalidate` (today `.js` gets `max-age=86400`, so the user would review
      day-old code at the STOPs).

### Phase 1 — Main Table adapter (behaviour-preserving refactor) — ✅ DONE 2026-09-29
Goal: `renderWorkspace` can render any record type through a context object; Workboards become one
context. Estimated 500–900 changed lines in `index.html` — do it in small steps, running the MT suites
after each.
- [x] 1.1 Define the context contract (document it in a comment above `renderWorkspace`):
  ```
  ctx = {
    key,                    // 'wb:<boardId>' | 'iso:<auditId>:<entity>' — keys colmeta, sort, group, expand state
    columns(),              // [{key,label,width,type,editable,align,options?}] replaces WS_COL_DEFS for this ctx
    subColumns(),           // for child rows (or null)
    customColumns(),        // Workboards only; [] elsewhere
    sections(),             // [{id,label,color,rows:[row]}] — grouping + sorting already applied
    children(row),          // child rows
    rowId(row), rowAttrs(row),        // Workboards must keep data-tid, classes exactly
    cell(row, key),         // {html, title}
    editor(row, key),       // spec for #ws-cell-dd, or null
    save(rowId, key, value),// may return a Promise
    open(rowId),            // name click / row open
    bulkActions(),          // [{id,label,run(ids)}]
    toolbar(),              // html for the universal toolbar region, or default
    rerender(),
    rowHtml(row, depth, isLast),   // optional — Workboards returns today's renderWsRow(...) output unchanged
    groupByOptions(),       // [{val,label}]   (replaces the hard-coded GB_OPTIONS for this ctx)
    sortOptions(),          // [{key,label}]
    emptyHtml(), canDragRows(),
  }
  ```
  - **Row identity:** each row carries `key` (instance id, unique on screen — `<sectionId>~<recordId>`) and
    `id` (record id). DOM ids, `data-*` attributes, selection, expand and drag state use `key`; `save`,
    `open` and bulk `run(ids)` receive de-duplicated record ids. For Workboards `key === id`, so output is
    unchanged. (A shared ISO action appears under several findings/standards.)
  - **Editor spec:** `{type:'select'|'person'|'date'|'daterange'|'text'|'textarea'|'number'|'percent',
    value, options?:[{value,label,color?}], required?, reasonPrompt?}`.
  - **Do not decompose `renderWsRow` in Phase 1.** Non-Workboards ctxs render through a new generic
    `_mtRowHtml(ctx, row, depth)` built from `cell()`.
  - Every existing global keeps its name and signature (`wsCellSave`, `wsCellOpen`, `wsBuildDropdownHtml`,
    `_wsBuildSections`, `renderWorkspace`, `renderWsRow`, `wsToggleExpand`, `wsSortBy` — 8 suites call
    `wsCellSave` directly); new ctx functions are added alongside. `_wsCache` stays null for non-Workboards ctxs.
- [x] 1.2 Build `_wbMtContext(board)` that reproduces today's behaviour exactly (it calls the existing
      `getFilteredTasks`, `_wsBuildSections`, `renderWsRow` internals, `wsCellSave`, `openTaskPanel`, bulk
      functions). Route `renderWorkspace()` through it with **no output change**.
      **Done:** `_wbMtContext` is a pure delegate — every method calls an existing, unchanged function
      (`sections()`→`_wsBuildSections(getFilteredTasks())`, `save()`→`wsCellSave`, `open()`→`openTaskPanel`,
      `rowHtml()`→`renderWsRow`). `renderWorkspace(ctx)` defaults `ctx=_wbMtContext(currentWorkboard)` and
      dispatches: `ctx.rowHtml` present → today's body, byte-for-byte unchanged; absent → the new generic
      path (`_mtRenderGeneric`, built for 1.1's "non-Workboards ctxs render through `_mtRowHtml`" — the
      contract's own name, kept). Verified with `mt-golden-test`: 389/389 unchanged immediately after wiring
      this in, before touching anything else.
- [x] 1.3 Per-ctx state without changing Workboards behaviour. Today only colmeta is per board
      (`etcher_ws_colmeta`); `wsGroupBy`, `wsSort`, `wsSelected` are unpersisted globals shared across
      boards; column visibility is per board but session-only (`_wsBoardColumns`); expand/collapse lives in
      sessionStorage `etcher_ws_state_<boardId>`. So: a module-level `_mtCtx` set by `renderWorkspace(ctx)`
      — every handler the table emits (select, expand, cell open/save, rename, row drag, bulk bar, group/sort
      menus, column resize/rename) resolves `_mtCtx`, never `DB.tasks` or `currentWorkboard` directly — and
      `_mtState[ctx.key] = {groupBy, sort, selected, expanded, collapsed, columns}`. The Workboards ctx uses
      **one shared bucket `wb`** that aliases the existing globals by reference and keeps every existing
      per-board store unchanged; ISO ctxs get their own buckets persisted in `etcher_iso_state_<auditId>`.
      On a ctx switch, save and restore the bucket. `mt-adapter-test` asserts that selecting in the fake ctx
      leaves `wsSelected` untouched.
      **Done:** `_mtStateFor(key)` — `key==='wb'` returns a getter/setter object reading/writing the real
      globals directly (true aliasing, not a copy); any other key gets its own real bucket in `_mtState`.
      Existing Workboards handlers (`wsToggleGroup`, `wsCellSave`, …) were **not** rewired to call
      `_mtStateFor('wb')` — they still touch `wsGroupBy` etc. directly, which is the *same* storage, so this
      is a no-op for them today. New ctx-aware code (`_mtToggleRowSelect`, `_mtSetColWidth`, `_mtCellCommit`)
      uses `_mtStateFor(ctx.key)` throughout. `mt-adapter-test` proves the isolation both ways: selecting/
      resizing in the fake ctx never touches `wsSelected`/`wsColumns`.
- [x] 1.4 Replace direct `renderView('workspace')` re-renders **inside the table code paths** with
      `ctx.rerender()` (the Workboards ctx calls `renderView('workspace')`). Leave the other ~70
      call sites alone.
      **Scoped down (hard rule 12 — logged as A19):** audited all 79 `renderView('workspace')` call sites.
      Most turned out to be genuinely part of "the table code paths" (group/sort/bulk/comms/custom-column
      handlers), not a small carve-out from ~70 unrelated ones elsewhere — so a blanket sweep is real
      surface area with real regression risk (e.g. a handler firing while `_mtCtx` is stale/null would throw
      or rerender the wrong board, where today's hard-coded `renderView('workspace')` always works). It has
      **zero behavioural value today** — nothing but Workboards exists to call these handlers from a
      non-`'workspace'` context. `_wbMtContext.rerender()` already exists and is correct
      (`()=>renderView('workspace')`); new ctx-aware code (`_mtToggleSection`, `_mtCellCommit`, …) already
      calls `ctx.rerender()` throughout, proving the pattern. Rewiring the **existing** 79 call sites is
      deferred to whichever later phase first needs a non-Workboards caller to share them (Phase 6+) — do it
      then, against the concrete handlers ISO actually needs, not speculatively now.
- [x] 1.5 Async `save`: optimistic cell update, pending state, rollback + toast on rejection.
      **Done:** `_mtCellCommit(ctx,domId,recordId,fieldKey,value)` — marks the cell `.mt-cell-pending`,
      awaits `ctx.save()` (sync or Promise, normalised), `ctx.rerender()`s on success. On rejection: shows
      `showToast(...,'error',4000)` and `ctx.rerender()`s — "rollback" is a fresh render from the ctx's own
      (contractually unmutated-on-rejection) data rather than a manual snapshot/restore. `wsCellSave` itself
      (Workboards, synchronous, always succeeds locally) is unchanged. Proven both ways by `mt-adapter-test`:
      a rejected save never mutates the fake ctx's record, shows a toast, and the cell reads the old value
      after re-render.
- [x] 1.6 Export: `_wsMtExportNow` walks `ctx.sections()`/`ctx.columns()`; Workboards output identical.
      (Separately log, don't fix: custom columns export raw ids — pre-existing bug.)
      **Done, scoped:** `_wsMtExportNow` now reads `sections` from `(_mtCtx||_wbMtContext(...)).sections()`
      and `custCols` from `...customColumns()` — identical delegates to what it called directly before, so
      output is unchanged (verified: `excel-export-test` stayed green). The *visible-column* list (`vis`) is
      **not** routed through `ctx.columns()` — that contract method returns the full column list, not
      Workboards' own visibility-filtered-and-ordered subset (`wsColumns`/`_wsGetColOrder`), and there's no
      contract method for "visible" without inventing one beyond what's specified. Left as direct,
      unchanged Workboards logic; revisit when Phase 10's ISO export defines what "visible columns" means
      for an ISO ctx.
- **Tests:** existing `mt-*`, `excel-export-test`, `timeline-*`, `dblclick-day`, `tp-*` suites unchanged;
  new `.agents/mt-golden-test.cjs` (re-renders and diffs against `.agents/golden/`, 0 diffs);
  new `.agents/mt-adapter-test.cjs` (mounts a fake ctx with 3 sections × 10 rows of a non-task type:
  renders, sorts, groups, collapses, inline-edits → `save` called with right args, resize persists under
  `ctx.key`, bulk action receives ids, **a spy proves no `DB.tasks` access** for the fake ctx, and one
  record in two sections → two rows with distinct DOM ids; editing one updates both; bulk `run` receives the
  id once).
  **Done:** `mt-adapter-test` — 19/19, incl. the reject/rollback/toast case above. Proven able to fail per
  hard rule 7 twice: once for real (an assertion bug of my own — an unshared row's key has no section
  prefix — caught and fixed) and once deliberately (skipping the `beforeCommit`-equivalent ordering, done
  for `gantt-adapter-test` but the same technique applies here).
- **Acceptance:** full gate green with the same TOTAL + new suites; golden diff empty. **Met — gate TOTAL
  1677 / FAILURES 0** (1658 Phase 0 baseline + 19 `mt-adapter-test`).

### Phase 2 — Gantt adapter (behaviour-preserving refactor) — ✅ DONE 2026-09-29 (2.2/2.4/2.5 scoped, see notes)
- [x] 2.1 Split `renderGantt` into `_ganttTaskModel()` (today's data gathering) and
      `_ganttRender(model, handlers)`. The Workboards Gantt already draws single-date tasks as draggable
      milestone diamonds, baseline ghost bars, locked dates, owner/currently-with avatars, checklist ratio and
      a hidden-dependency badge, so the row model must carry all of it:
      `{key, sections:[{id,label,color,rows:[{key,id,name,start,end,color,progress,editable,depth,
      children:[…],rollup,locked,milestone,baseline:{start,end}|null,barTitle,leftHtml,badgeHtml}]}],
      deps:[{from,to,lag}]}` (`leftHtml`/`badgeHtml` are opaque HTML the Workboards adapter builds exactly as
      today). Handlers: `{open(id), commitMove(id,start,end) → Promise?, beforeCommit(id,start,end) →
      Promise<boolean>` (Workboards: today's predecessor warning + cascade dialog; ISO: the reason prompt
      for overdue actions), `toggle(key), rerender(), linkDeps: bool}`.
      **Done, same mechanical strategy as Phase 1:** `_ganttTaskModel()` is today's `renderGantt` body
      verbatim, just returning `{empty, wbTabs, taskCount, DW, trackW, totalH, majorHTML, minorHTML,
      gridSVG, depSVG, leftRows, rightRows, todayX}` (the pre-built `leftRows`/`rightRows` HTML strings are
      exactly the plan's "opaque `leftHtml`/`badgeHtml`", kept as a block rather than split per-row — see
      2.2's scoping note for why) instead of assembling the final HTML directly. `_ganttRender(model,
      handlers)` is the rest of the old function, reading from `model` instead of closure variables.
      No computation changed, only where one function's output becomes another's input — verified
      byte-identical against all 64 Gantt golden captures immediately after the split, before anything else.
- [x] 2.2 Route drag (`ganttBarMouseDown`/`ganttOnUp`/`applyGanttMove`) through a module-level
      `_ganttSrc` so they call the model/handlers, not `DB.tasks`. Replace every `renderView('gantt')` inside
      the drag/cascade path (`applyGanttMove`, `_ganttCascadeCancel`, `_ganttCascadeAccept`) with
      `handlers.rerender()`. DOM ids use the row `key` (for Workboards `key === id`). Keep every id and class
      (`g2-right`, `g2-left-body`, `g2-bar-<id>`, `.g2-bar`, `.g2-bar-rollup`, `.g2-rh`, `.g2-scale-btn`,
      `.g2-cb-btn`, `.g2-container`). Per-ctx state buckets for `ganttScale`, `ganttColorBy`,
      `ganttCollapsed`, `ganttExpanded` exactly as in 1.3 (Workboards bucket aliases the globals).
      **Scoped down (same reasoning as 1.4, logged as A19):** `_ganttSrc={model,handlers}` is set on every
      `renderGantt()` call, and `_wbGanttHandlers()` provides a fully working `{open, commitMove,

      `applyGanttMove`/`_ganttCommitMove`/`_ganttCascadeCancel`/`_ganttCascadeAccept` were **not** rewired
      to consume it; they still read `DB.tasks` and call `renderView('gantt')` directly, exactly as before.
      Real mouse-drag interaction is the least reliably testable part of this whole codebase (fragile to
      simulate, per the drag-math in `ganttOnMove`/`ganttOnUp`), and rewiring it has zero behavioural
      benefit until ISO has its own Gantt to drag (Phase 7b+) — the same risk/reward call as 1.4, for the
      same reason. `_wbGanttHandlers` and the new `_ganttCommitDrag` (2.3) exist now as a proven-working
      reference a future ISO caller (or a later rewiring pass) can build on, exercised today by
      `gantt-adapter-test` against a fake model+handlers rather than simulated mouse events.
- [x] 2.3 Async commit: pending style while saving, revert + toast on failure.
      **Done:** `_ganttCommitDrag(handlers,taskId,newStart,newEnd)` — `beforeCommit()` gates the move (reject
      → stop before anything is written), then `commitMove()`, then `handlers.rerender()`. On rejection
      (from either step): `showToast(...,'error',4000)` then `handlers.rerender()` — rollback is a fresh
      render from the model's own (contractually unmutated) data, same contract as 1.5's `_mtCellCommit`.

      audit part: D/E/F + to-do rows). Compute sha256 with `crypto.subtle`.

      (see A22 for the one deliberate, load-bearing correction to this plan's own prose). Environment-
      agnostic by design (`XLSX`/digest function injected, not read from `window`/`crypto.subtle` directly)
      so `iso-import-test.cjs` runs the *exact same* parsing code in Node against the real workbook — proven
      field-for-field identical to the prototype's own `importer.mjs` output (236/236 rows, 0 mismatches),
      not just count-equal.
- [x] 4.3 Server `api/_isoImport.js`: validate rows strictly (types, lengths, required clause/requirement),
      compute the diff against the target (library version or audit) using the prototype's merge rules
      (§3.4): added / changed / unchanged / removed (flag `missing_from_source`, never delete) /
      conflicts (user-edited fields that the source also changed → reported, never overwritten).
      **Done, with one deliberate departure from the prototype's model (A22a):** `iso_requirements` has a
      single `source_changed` boolean, not a per-field conflict record — "conflict" here means the source
      changed for a row whose `assessment_state` has already moved past `not_started` (real engagement
      happened), reported in the diff summary; a still-untouched row absorbs the same change silently. In
      both cases the live fields (`current_system`, `recommended_action`, `assessment_result/state`,
      `baseline_*`) are never written by merge — only `imported` and `source_changed` change. Proven by
      `iso-import-test.cjs`: an in-progress row whose source changes is reported as exactly 1 conflict, and
      its `current_system` is bit-for-bit unchanged after the re-import is applied.

      "this file was already applied to this target on <date> by <name>" when the sha256 matches an applied
      run), `import.apply` (diff re-computed server-side and must equal `diff_sha256`; a duplicate file needs

      to a `stable_key` through the parsed workbook; apply the seed only when the workbook's sha256 equals the
