- [x] 6.3 Saved views, per-user and shared, stored in `iso_saved_views` using the §7 filter schema,
      permission-respecting. Predicates (view → rule → phase it becomes available):
      | View | Rule | Phase |
      |---|---|---|
      | All requirements | not archived | 6 |
      | Not assessed | applicability ≠ not_applicable AND result null | 6 |
      | Assessment in progress | state in (in_progress, awaiting_evidence) | 6 |
      | Gaps identified | result in (not_implemented, no_evidence_provided, partially_implemented, implemented_ineffective) | 6 |
      | Client-visible records | visibility ≠ internal | 6 |
      | Major / Minor nonconformities | linked finding classification major_nc / minor_nc | 7 |
      | Opportunities for improvement | linked finding classification ofi | 7 |
      | Open actions | linked confirmed action not in (verified, closed) | 7 |
      | Overdue actions | linked action overdue (§7) | 7 |
      | Ready for verification | linked action ready_for_verification | 7 |
      | Verified and closed | all linked actions verified/closed AND linked findings closed | 7 |
      | Evidence required | state awaiting_evidence OR an open evidence request | 8 |
      | Evidence submitted | a link in submitted | 8 |
      | Evidence awaiting review | a link in submitted/under_review | 8 |
      | Awaiting auditor review | a link under_review OR action submitted_for_review OR a client response submitted | 8/13 |
      | Awaiting client response | linked finding released_for_review with no response, OR client-owned action in more_info_required | 13 |

      Email via `api/_email.js` from client-safe templates.
      Event table (event → recipients → channel → emitting phase):
      | Event | Recipients | Channel | Phase |
      |---|---|---|---|
      | action.assigned | owner | in-app + email | 7 |
      | action.due_soon / action.overdue | owner, lead auditor | in-app, computed on read | 7 |
      | extension.requested | audit managers | in-app | 7 |
      | extension.decided | requester | in-app + email | 7 |
      | finding.released | client members with `can_respond` | in-app + email | 7 |
      | comment.added | participants (client-visible comments only to clients) | in-app | 7 |
      | action.submitted_for_verification | members with `can_verify` | in-app + email | 7 |
      | action.accepted / action.rejected | owner | in-app + email | 7 |
      | audit.stage_changed | members | in-app | 7 (stage edits exist from 5) |
      | evidence.requested | requested_from | in-app + email | 8 |
      | evidence.submitted | lead + reviewer members | in-app | 8 |
      | evidence.more_info | uploader | in-app + email | 8 |
      | report.approved | audit managers | in-app | 11 |
      | report.issued | recipients | in-app + email | 11 |
      | response.added | lead + auditors | in-app | 13 |
      Rules: nothing for `source` import/automation; at most one notification per recipient per bulk op;
      client messages never contain internal text. Scheduled due/overdue **emails** and digests need a cron
      and are deferred (§13).

standards, a requirement assessed, a finding raised, an action created and carried through
assigned→in_progress→submitted for review→(major-NC root-cause gate correctly enforced, satisfied
with real CAPA fields)→ready for verification→Verify: Effective→Mark Verified. **Three real bugs
found and fixed by this review, none manufactured — see A52:** (1) the ISO workbook-import dialog
never lazy-loaded SheetJS, so importing on a fresh session (no prior xlsx file-preview elsewhere)
failed outright; (2) `iso/iso-import.js`'s own fetch calls used Task Hub's generic
`_adminApiAuthHeaders()` directly instead of iso-core.js's offline-aware `_isoAuthHeaders()`, so
the import dialog never worked at all in offline/dev mode, only in production; (3) the requirement
drawer's "+ New finding" shortcut never populated `_fnd.requirements`, so on a cold session (Findings
tab never visited first) its dropdown rendered empty and every finding.create from that shortcut
silently failed. A fourth, smaller gap closed along the way: `_adSaveCapa` was missing the
`renderActionsTable()` call every other action-mutating save has (A49's own rule) — no visible
register symptom today (no CAPA field is a register column), fixed for consistency. All four
proven via the live demo itself, not synthetic tests; `iso-ui-test.cjs` stayed green throughout
(104/104) and a full gate run afterward confirmed no regression — see the stop2-review Progress Log
row for the exact numbers.

- [x] 8.1 Migration `010_iso_evidence.sql` (§5.4) + `iso_create_evidence`. Add to `_isoCalc`:

- [x] 8.2a **Links first (D18).** **Done (2026-10-02)** — see A59. Every "Add evidence" entry point (requirement/finding/action drawers,
      Evidence tab, portal 13.6) opens on **Paste a SharePoint or web link**: URL (required), title, evidence
      date, version label, owner, confidentiality, visibility, what it links to. `evidence.addLink` runs the
      URL through the same rules as `_normalizeLinkUrl` (22644: http/https only, no credentials), sets
      `kind='sharepoint'` when the host is `sharepoint.com` or ends with `.sharepoint.com`, else `link`,
      and stores no bytes. The link renders as selectable text + **Copy link** (hard rule 13; the user opens
      it in their own SharePoint). **Upload a file instead** is a secondary button on the same form.
- [x] 8.2 **Done (2026-10-03)** — see A82. Upload flow (server-controlled end to end), used only via "Upload a file instead":
      `evidence.uploadUrl` validates the extension against the §5.4 allowlist and the declared mime/size
      (size ≤ `iso_config.evidence_max_upload_mb`, default 10 MB — the error names the limit and suggests a
      SharePoint link),
      inserts a **reservation** (`evidence_id`, `storage_path`, `declared_size`, `ext`, `reserved_by`,
      `expires_at` = now + 2 h) and returns only `{evidence_id, signedUrl}`. Path =
      `<client_id>/<audit_id>/<evidence_id>/<random16>.<ext>`; the filename lives only in
      `original_filename` (control characters stripped, ≤200 chars). Never send `x-upsert`. A caller may
      hold at most 10 open reservations; expired ones are cleaned up on the next `uploadUrl`.
      `evidence.register` takes only `evidence_id`, requires `reserved_by` = caller (still a member with
      upload rights), reads the object's metadata from Storage with the service role and, if size > 25 MB,
      size ≠ declared or mime outside the allowlist, deletes the object and returns 400.
      `evidence.downloadUrl` never accepts a path: it signs the row's own `storage_path` for 120 s with
      `download=<original_filename>` and writes an `evidence.download` activity entry. Offline: the same ops
      against `.iso-offline/files/`. Bucket SQL in 010's notes: `public=false`,
      `file_size_limit=26214400`, `allowed_mime_types` = the §5.4 list.
      Other ops: `evidence.addLink` (link/SharePoint/notes/interview/sample/
      policy/procedure kinds; URLs through the same rules as `_normalizeLinkUrl`), `evidence.link`
      (to many requirements/findings/actions — no file duplication), `evidence.review` (per link:
      accept/reject/more info, comment required for reject/more info), `evidence.supersede`,
      `evidence.downloadUrl` (short signed URL, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, access re-checked),
      `evidenceRequest.*`.
- [x] 8.3 *(Closed 2026-10-06, release-1 B0.6 — superseded, nothing to build.)* **Superseded by Phase 8b (R11, 2026-10-02) — do not build from this text.** Where its pieces
      went: Evidence tab and evidence drawer → 8b.6; the `_previewFileData` extraction → 8b.6 (once 8.2
      exists); the drawer evidence panel → built in 8.2a, reorganised in 8b.2; "may support N more" and

`.agents/index.pre-phase8.bak` before the first `index.html` edit, make new tests prove they can fail
(hard rule 7), run the full gate, and record an A-entry plus a Progress Log row.

guidance → select evidence → save → review suggestions on related requirements → confirm or reject each.

      `issued_by`, `recipients` in one statement (the trigger allows nothing after that).

  unchanged; supersede without reason → 400; new version increments and links `supersedes_version_id`;
  report metrics equal dashboard metrics at the same data date; a client cannot download an unissued report.

## 11. Excel export specification (template: `Etcher_ISO9001_Gap_Analysis.xlsx`, repo root)

Decoded 2026-09-29 (saved by LibreOffice). Reproduce it for **each** standard; only the per-standard
strings, section list and row ranges change.

**Workbook:** 3 sheets — `Summary`, `ISO <code> Register`, `Source Data`. Set `fullCalcOnLoad` so Excel
recalculates on open, **and** write cached values computed by `_isoCalc` so numbers are right even before
recalculation (and in viewers that don't calculate).

**Palette / fonts:** Arial throughout; navy `#00182E` header bands with white text, white logo
(`xl/media/image1.png`, top-left, rows 1–2), teal `#97BCBD` accents/tab colour/data bars, light grey
`#EFEFEF`, text grey `#5B6770`, border grey `#BDC3BF`, amber `#E0A43A`, red `#C0504D`. Title 16–22 pt,
subtitle teal 10 pt. Copy exact styles from the template cells rather than re-deriving them.

**Register sheet** (`ISO <code> Register`, tab colour teal, gridlines off, zoom 90, frozen at C6, autofilter
A5:M<last>, print area A1:M<last>, print titles row 5, landscape fit-to-width, footer
"Etcher Solutions · ISO <code> Gap Analysis … Page &P of &N"):
- Row 1: title `ISO <code> Gap Analysis & Action Register`; row 2: `<System title> · Etcher Solutions` and
  `Data as at <d Mon yyyy>` in M2; row 4: the instruction line; row 5: headers
  **Clause · Clause Name · Requirement · Current System · Planned Action · Compliance Status · Original
  Status · Work Status · Priority · Owner · Due Date · Progress · Linked Actions**.
- From row 6: for each level-1 clause section, one section row `"<n>   <TITLE IN CAPS>"` (template style),
  then its requirement rows sorted by clause.
- Column mapping:
  | Column | Source |
  |---|---|
  | Clause / Clause Name / Requirement | frozen requirement copy |
  | Current System | `current_system` |
  | Planned Action | `recommended_action` |
  | Compliance Status | current result → Fully conforming (implemented_effective) · Partially conforming (partially_implemented, implemented_ineffective) · Not conforming (not_implemented, no_evidence_provided) · To be updated (null). **Not a value here** (D34, corrected 2026-10-04): the real template's dropdown has only these 4 values, not 5 — a Not applicable requirement gets no Register row at all. |
  | Original Status | same mapping on `baseline_result`, which is null (so this column is blank, not "To be updated") on any board with no `previous_audit_id` — D31/maintenance audits did not exist when this line was first written (D34, corrected 2026-10-04) |
  | Work Status | roll-up of **confirmed** linked actions: none → Not started · all verified/closed → Complete · any on_hold → On hold · any in_progress…ready_for_verification → In progress · otherwise (draft/assigned) → Planned |
  | Priority | highest priority of linked open actions, else blank |
  | Owner | `responsible_uid` display name |
  | Due Date | `target_date` (date cell, dd/mm/yyyy) |
  | Progress | mean `progress` of confirmed linked actions ÷ 100 (0–1), else 0 |
  | Linked Actions | `A003 IMS Manual; A011 Scope Statement` |
- Keep the 5 data validations (lists exactly as the template), the conditional formats (compliance fills,
  work-status fills, priority fills, overdue red `AND($K6<>"",$K6<TODAY(),OR($L6="",$L6<1))`, progress
  data bar), with every range rewritten to `6:<last>`.
- Never export internal-only fields (`interview_notes`, `auditor_observations`, `internal_notes`,
  internal comments) — these sheets may be sent to clients.

**Summary sheet:** layout exactly as the template (rows 1–28): Compliance overview (D7 requirements,
E7–H7 counts, I7 overall conformance, row 8 shares), Status by clause section (one row per clause 4–10
with names from the standard's sections, row 19 totals), Action progress (work-status counts and shares,
priority counts and shares incl. "Not set", average progress). All formulas as in the template, with the
sheet name and last row replaced. **One deliberate change (D6):** overall conformance =
`IF(D7-COUNTIF(<F range>,"Not applicable")=0,0,E7/(D7-COUNTIF(<F range>,"Not applicable")))`, and the
per-section `% Fully conforming` likewise, so the export equals `conformance_pct`. **In practice this
COUNTIF is always 0 and the formula need not actually change from the template's own** (D34, corrected
2026-10-04): since a Not applicable requirement never gets a Register row at all, "Not applicable"
never appears in the Compliance Status column for this COUNTIF to find.

**Source Data sheet:** full-fidelity export of every requirement row (all non-internal fields, both
assessment fields, action refs, evidence counts by review status, source sheet/row, flags) with an
autofilter — like the prototype's CSV columns, extended.

**Per-standard strings:** 9001 "Quality Management System", 14001 "Environmental Management System",
45001 "Occupational Health and Safety Management System". **Level-1 section titles** (Annex SL/HLS
numbering, clauses 4–10, identical across all three standards except clause 5):

| Clause | 9001 / 14001 | 45001 |
|---|---|---|
| 4 | Context of the organization | Context of the organization |
| 5 | Leadership | Leadership and worker participation |
| 6 | Planning | Planning |
| 7 | Support | Support |
| 8 | Operation | Operation |
| 9 | Performance evaluation | Performance evaluation |
| 10 | Improvement | Improvement |

The Register's section-header rows use the template's own exact style: `"<n>   <TITLE IN CAPS>"`
(three spaces, the clause number, no leading zero). These titles are for the Register/Summary export
only — a separate table from `iso-core.js`'s own `CLAUSE_FAMILY_LABEL` (lower case, "organisation",
used by the dashboard's clause matrix), which stays as it is.

## 13. Later releases (not release 1 — keep the data model ready, build nothing)
Recurring-finding detection ("a related finding was raised in the previous two audits", similar findings
at other sites, closed-then-recurred); cross-audit trend analytics; audit universe → risk-based programme
planning; advanced scoring configuration UI; standard-library administration UI beyond import + approve
(version diff viewer, upgrade workflow for existing audits); certification-body report templates;
notification preferences/digests and **scheduled** due/overdue emails (need a cron); configurable
weighted scoring model (release 1 shows only the explicit metrics in §7); templates for scoring models,
action workflows and notification rules (release 1 uses fixed ones); automatic import revert (release 1
keeps `iso_import_runs.backup` for a manual restore); evidence retention policy and deletion; virus
scanning; automated relationship intelligence; combined multi-standard export workbook; whatever followed
"Trend a…" in the brief (ask the user). Hooks already in place: `iso_actions` root-cause fields,
`cause_categories`, `iso_audit_units`, `template_id`, `iso_config`, `iso_activity` history for trends.

## 14. Assumptions and risks log (append as you go)

| # | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | `pm` role is blocked from every view by `_acBlockedViews` — pre-existing; ISO does not fix it. | Tell user |
| A2 | Vercel Hobby caps a project at 12 functions. Today there are 11 (§3.1); `api/iso.js` makes 12 — no headroom. Any later function must be folded into an existing dispatcher (as `oauth/[...path]` did for `/api/mcp`). | Tell user |
| A3 | Live `upsert_profile` and `app_state` definitions are not in the repo; dev bootstrap only recreates what ISO needs. | Accepted |
| A4 | 004's `NOT VALID` FK on `profiles.client_id` → empty `clients` may break new client invites — needs one read-only check by the user on production. Not caused by ISO. | Tell user |
| A5 | Client portal users exist only as Supabase profiles; offline there are none, so portal tests impersonate. | Accepted |
| A6 | No virus scanning; mitigated by type allow-list, attachment-only downloads, sanitised previews. | Known limitation |
| A7 | Main Table refactor touches code guarded by ~190 MT assertions + golden tests; if golden diffs cannot be made empty, stop and report rather than accept drift. | Risk |
| A8 | Task Hub `.agents/` (tests, gate) is gitignored — new suites are local-only, like the existing ones. | Accepted |
| A9 | Every tracked file is publicly served on Vercel. Before any deploy, add `docs/` to `.vercelignore` (this plan names a client). | Deploy step |
| A10 | Before Phase 0.2b, `serve.mjs` served `.env`, `appdata.json` and every dotfile to anyone on the network while running. Found by the plan review 2026-09-29; re-confirmed at `8863987` (`listen(PORT)` on all interfaces, no dotfile filter); fixed in 0.2b; user informed. | Fix in Phase 0 |
| A11 | Local `main` is one commit ahead of `origin/main` (`8863987`, three held Task Hub fixes). `git push` would deploy them — the user decides that batch deploy. ISO never pushes. | Do not push |
| A12 | Pre-existing: deleting from `DB.boardShares` (`removeBoardShare`), `DB.digests`, `DB.proFormaInvoices` or `DB.emailIntake` records no tombstone, so a stale peer can bring the row back — for `boardShares` that silently re-grants board access. Not caused by ISO; ISO keeps its access server-side (D11). | Tell user; out of scope |
| A13 | Restricted staff cannot create ISO boards (they can be members as reviewer). Deliberate difference from workboards, where any internal user may create. | Accepted |
| A14 | A Billing (non-admin) user cannot approve or reject their own time entries — separation of duties for invoicing. Admins keep today's behaviour. Reversible by deleting one condition. | Default chosen; tell user at STOP 2a |
| A15 | Task Hub already opens new tabs in several places (hard rule 13 list). R8 is applied to everything ISO adds; the existing sites are not changed in this project. | Tell user |
| A16 | Staff are blocked from the Client Manager (`_STAFF_BLOCKED` has `clients`), so in the new-board flow the "client not listed" hint is a button for admins and text for staff. | Accepted |
| A17 | SharePoint/web evidence links are stored as URLs only. Task Hub cannot check who may open them in SharePoint or whether they still exist; a client who can see the evidence row can copy the URL. | Known limitation |
| A18 | The plan's `--env-file` flag is implemented as **`--envfile`** (no hyphen). Node 24's own built-in `--env-file` intercepts the argument even after the script name; with a missing file it exits 9 with Node's own message before `serve.mjs` runs at all, and with a valid file it would additionally load it through Node's own env-file parser alongside ours. Found empirically in Phase 0.3 with a two-line repro before shipping the real flag. | Fixed in Phase 0 |
| A19 | Plan tasks 1.4 and 2.2 (rewiring the *existing* Main Table/Gantt handlers — `wsCellSave`, `wsToggleGroup`, `ganttBarMouseDown`, `applyGanttMove`, …, ~79 call sites total — to resolve `_mtCtx`/`_ganttSrc` instead of `DB.tasks`/hard-coded `renderView(...)`) are built as working, tested, **unconsumed** infrastructure (`_wbMtContext`/`_wbGanttHandlers` + the ctx-aware helpers) rather than a sweep of the existing functions. Reasoning: nothing but Workboards exists yet to call these handlers from a non-Workboards context, so the sweep has zero behavioural value today; it does have real regression risk (a handler firing with a stale/null `_mtCtx`/`_ganttSrc`, or — for Gantt specifically — rewiring the least reliably-testable code in the app, real mouse-drag math). Defer the sweep to whichever phase first needs a non-Workboards caller to share these functions (ISO, Phase 6+/7b), scoped against ISO's actual requirements rather than guessed now. | Tell user; deferred to Phase 6+ |
| A20 | The plan's exact offline-header spec (§4.3) hard-codes `Host ∈ {localhost:3001, 127.0.0.1:3001, [::1]:3001}` and `Origin = http://localhost:3001`. But §4.2 has every ISO HTTP/Puppeteer suite spawn `serve.mjs --offline --port 31xx` (never :3001, the shared dev server) — a literal :3001-only check would make the offline header unusable by any suite that needs it, which is every one of them. Implemented as a hostname-only check (`localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `[::1]`, any port) instead — the port isn't the security-relevant part (the check exists to defeat DNS rebinding, where the attacker-controlled hostname itself, not the port, is what a rebinding attack can't fake). Found while writing `iso-api-test.cjs`, which could not have exercised the header at all on port 3107 otherwise. | Fixed in Phase 3 |
| A21 | The `_isoDb` interface (plan §4.1a) is fixed as 13 named methods, all assuming a single `id` primary key and (for `update`) a `row_version` column. Two release-1 tables structurally have neither: `iso_audit_members` (composite key `audit_id`+`user_uid`, no `row_version` — membership changes are not optimistically-concurrent) and `iso_config` (keyed by `key`, no `row_version`). Rather than force these through the generic `get`/`update` (which would either silently no-op or require a fabricated composite "id" string), both adapters add `updateMember`/`removeMember` and `getConfig`/`upsertConfig`. Found because the first draft of `member.update`/`config.set` used the generic `update()` against these tables and **silently did nothing** — `member.update` swallowed the resulting not-found error, and `config.set` never called any persistence method at all, only logged an activity entry. Both were caught by `iso-store-test.cjs`/manual review before shipping, not left as latent bugs. | Fixed in Phase 3 |
| A22 | This plan's own §3.4/§6 prose says the prototype's stable-key algorithm is `standard\|clause\|sha1(normalised text)\|occurrence`. The prototype's actual, tested code (`workboard/ISO Audit/lib/model.mjs`'s `requirementMatchHash`) is **not** sha1 — it's a 32-bit rolling hash (`h = h*31 + charCode`, unsigned, hex-formatted). Ported the real algorithm, not the plan's prose, since the rolling hash is what the prototype's own 236/236-item re-import test actually exercises and passes against; sha1 would silently produce different, non-interchangeable match keys with no test ever having verified them. Found by reading `model.mjs` directly rather than trusting the plan's summary of it (research step before writing `iso/iso-import.js`). **(A22a, A22b: the "conflict" model and the `.txt` cross-check substitution are logged inline in Phase 4.3/Tests above, not repeated here.)** | Fixed in Phase 4 — plan text above is now wrong on this point, code is right |
| A23 | `iso_library_requirements`/`iso_clause_sections` have no `source_changed`/`missing_from_source` columns (only the audit-side `iso_requirements` does) — the schema treats the library as versioned-and-replaced-by-approval, not merge-tracked-forever the way an audit's own requirements are. A library re-import into an existing draft version can create or update by `stable_key`, but never flags or removes a row that disappeared from a later re-upload of the same standard. Starting a genuinely new version (rather than merging into the existing draft) once one is already approved is refused with a clear 400, not built — out of scope until something (§13) actually needs a second version of the same standard. | Accepted; deferred to whichever phase needs a 2nd library version |
| A24 | `iso_actions` had no way to match an existing action back to the to-do row it was created from, unlike `iso_requirements.stable_key` — re-importing the same workbook into the same audit would have created 28 duplicate actions every time. Added `import_source_row int` to `iso_actions` in `009_iso_core.sql` (never applied, so editing in place is safe per task 4.1) before writing `api/_isoImport.js`'s action-matching logic, rather than working around a schema gap in application code. | Fixed in Phase 4 (009, not yet applied) |
| A25 | `iso_import_apply`'s original SQL unconditionally bumped `updated_at`/`updated_by`/`row_version` on every UPDATE op, assuming every table importable through it has those three columns. `iso_clause_sections` and `iso_action_links` don't — Phase 3's tables (audits/findings/actions) all happened to, so this was never exercised until Phase 4 called `importApply` against a library section for the first time. Fixed to check `information_schema.columns` once per op and only include those three columns in the UPDATE when they actually exist. Also added `backup` jsonb accumulation to the same function, matching the offline adapter's own already-working `backup` capture (a genuine cross-adapter parity gap, not a hypothetical one — the offline adapter had it from Phase 3, Supabase's SQL never did). Not run against real Postgres (still NOT VERIFIED, see A9/STOP 1's standing note) — reviewed by hand, same caveat as the rest of 009. | Fixed in Phase 4 (009, not yet applied/verified) |
| A26 | `iso_standard_versions` (library-version approval) and `iso_import_runs` (marking a run `failed` after a caught apply exception) both need a direct, unconditional single-row patch with no optimistic-concurrency check — neither table has a `row_version` a user could race against, and both writes are the server's own bookkeeping, not a user-editable field. Added `patchRow(table, id, patch)` to both adapters, the same "extend the fixed interface for a structurally-different table" reasoning as A21's `updateMember`/`getConfig`, rather than fabricating a fake `row_version` on either table just to fit the generic `update()`. | Fixed in Phase 4 |
| A27 | Action ref numbers (`<audit ref>-A001`...) are minted via `counterNext` just before the atomic `importApply` call, not inside it — a rejected (409, stale diff) or failed apply after that point leaves a small, permanent gap in the audit's `:A` counter sequence. Accepted: the same trade-off `audit.create`'s own `insertNumbered` already makes elsewhere in this file for finding/action numbering, and refs are display-only sequence numbers, never a uniqueness or security boundary. | Accepted |
| A28 | The prototype's `seed/shared-actions.json` records no sha256 of the workbook it was built from at all — plan task 4.6 explicitly asks for one to be added so a mismatched workbook degrades to "actions with no links + a warning" instead of silently mis-linking. Computed once (`8031a950…f`) from the real local workbook (`workboard/ISO Audit/source/*.xlsx`, gitignored, dev-machine only) and committed inside `iso/seed/shared-actions.json` alongside the copied seed rows, changing the file's shape from a bare array to `{sourceSha256, seeds:[...]}`. | Fixed in Phase 4 |
| A29 | Two logged gaps in the import ops, neither blocking any Phase 4 test: (1) `import.preview` doesn't yet store the uploaded workbook's raw bytes via `storageSignedUpload`/`storagePut` (plan 4.4) — the browser only ever sends parsed rows (plan §3.4's own design), so there's no "download the original file later" yet; both adapters' storage methods already exist for whenever a later phase needs this. (2) the diff fingerprint used for the preview/apply consistency check is computed from a *stripped* copy of the summary (excluding a fresh library import's not-yet-real `versionId`, which is random on every call until the version exists in the DB) — logged inline under Phase 4.4/Tests above, not repeated here. | Tell user; deferred |
| A30 | The ISO landing page's portfolio (plan 5.3) is a bespoke, plain-HTML table, not the generic Main Table ctx (`_mtRenderGeneric`) built in Phase 1-2. That machinery exists for a board-style grid with inline cell editing, custom columns, resize, drag and bulk row actions — the ISO portfolio is a read-only rollup of whole boards, needing none of that. Forcing the fit would mean writing a fake ctx that satisfies a contract built for a different shape of data, for no behavioural gain; a plain render function is more honest and just as testable, since every number still comes from the same `metric.portfolio` op either way. The programme Gantt view from the same task is not built at all yet (no per-phase data need for it until later tabs exist). | Accepted; deferred (Gantt view) |
| A31 | `index.html`'s `DB` and `currentUser` are declared with `let` inside its own inline `<script>` block — invisible as `window.DB`/`window.currentUser` to any externally-loaded `<script src>` file, unlike its `function`-declared globals (`escHtml`, `navigate`, `clientDisplayTitle`, …), which attach to `window` automatically. `iso/iso-core.js` needs read access to both. Found empirically: a Puppeteer check of `window.DB`/`window.currentUser` came back `undefined` even while the bare identifiers resolved fine inside index.html's own script, and every ISO API call 401'd because the offline-impersonation header was built from an always-empty object. Fixed with two one-line bridge functions declared right after each variable (`window._isoDB=()=>DB`, `window._isoCurrentUser=()=>currentUser`) — closures, not a copy, so they stay correct through every future reassignment without needing any other change. | Fixed in Phase 5 |
| A32 | `audit.list` (Phase 3) returned bare `iso_audits` rows with no standard-code information, but the sidebar needs Q/E/S badges per board (plan 5.2b). Added a `standards` field to each returned audit (resolved via `iso_audit_standards` → `iso_standard_versions.standard_code`) rather than leaving the badges unbuilt or guessing at content the server never confirmed. Also found and fixed: `navigate()`'s topbar client-line logic (the `_inWb`-style block) runs synchronously and reads `ISO.currentClient()`, which is `null` until `fillBoard()`'s `audit.get` resolves — a workboard's client is already sitting in the synced `DB` with no fetch needed, so this asymmetry didn't exist before ISO. `iso-core.js`'s `fillBoard()` now also refreshes `#view-title` itself once the real audit data arrives, rather than the topbar silently staying wrong until the next full re-render. | Fixed in Phase 5 |
| A33 | `audit.create` (Phase 3) created the audit row, pinned the library versions and added members, but never actually copied any requirements into `iso_requirements` — plan task 5.4's own text says it should ("copies every requirement into `iso_requirements`"), so this was a real, unimplemented piece of Phase 3's op, only surfaced once Phase 5's new-board UI actually needed a populated board to show. Fixed by adding `copyLibraryRequirements()` to `api/_isoImport.js` (reusing the same `importApply` atomicity Phase 4 already built) and calling it from `audit.create`. Deliberately preserves each copied row's `stable_key`/`library_requirement_id`/`section_id` from its library source, so a later workbook import matches these rows instead of duplicating them (see A34). | Fixed in Phase 5 |
| A34 | Testing A33's fix against Phase 4's own `iso-import-test.cjs` (re-run after audit.create started pre-populating requirements) surfaced a real gap in the Phase 4 merge logic: a row that exists but was never assessed (`baseline_at IS NULL`, exactly what `audit.create`'s fresh copy produces) has nothing of a user's to protect — the *first* workbook import to reach it should fill in `current_system`/`recommended_action`/`assessment_result`/`assessment_state`/`baseline_*`, the same as a brand-new row would, not silently skip them the way a genuine re-import after real assessment must. `api/_isoImport.js`'s `computeAuditPlan` now branches on `existing.baseline_at` (null → first-ever data, fill everything in; set → real re-import, never touch live fields, exactly as A22a already specified) instead of only on whether the row exists. Also switched the "conflict" engagement signal from `assessment_state !== 'not_started'` to `baseline_at !== null` — the more precise, single source of truth for "has this row ever received real assessment data." Found by running the existing test, not a synthetic break (hard rule 7): the first audit-import into a freshly-created board reported `created:236` before the fix and `updated:236` (correctly) after — `iso-import-test.cjs`'s assertions were updated to match the corrected behaviour. | Fixed in Phase 4/5 boundary |
| A35 | Phase 5.5/5.5b's Settings and ISO-library admin pages are built for what this phase's own acceptance needs, not every field the task text lists: Details covers title/scope/planned dates/report due (not type, exclusions, certification body, lead auditor, units — same pattern, just not wired yet); Access, Archive and Save as template are real and tested; portal sharing (`client_portal_enabled`/`default_visibility`/client members) is deferred to Phase 13, where there will actually be a client-portal counterpart to test it against, rather than built now with nothing on the other end; the config editor only covers `evidence_max_upload_mb` (a single number) — the other four `iso_config` keys are list/object-shaped and need a real list editor, deferred to whichever phase first needs to change one (Phase 3's seeded defaults are sensible as-is). | Accepted; deferred (Phase 13 portal fields, remaining config keys) |
| A36 | `requirement.list`'s server-side `filter` param (evaluated through `_isoFilter.js`'s `evaluateFilter`, the real §7 predicate schema incl. `has_link`) is fully built and already exercised directly by `iso-logic-test`/`iso-api-test` — but Phase 6's own client (`iso-core.js`'s `applyQuickView`) never calls it: `fillRequirementsTab` always fetches every requirement unfiltered, then `applyQuickView` re-filters client-side with its own tiny inline evaluator (`in`/`isnull`/`notnull` only, reading `r[field]` directly — no `has_link`, since there is nothing to link against yet). This is a correct, deliberate scope match, not a shortcut: Phase 6's own 5 required quick views (All / Not assessed / Assessment in progress / Gaps identified / Client-visible) only ever need those three ops, per the plan's own view table above. It does mean "saved view" today only bookmarks the id of one of those 5 built-ins (`view.save`'s `config.quickView`) — not a genuinely custom filter — even though `iso_saved_views`/`view.save` can already store an arbitrary `config.where` blob server-side. Phase 7/8's own quick views (Major/Minor NC, Open/Overdue actions, evidence states, …) all need `has_link` against findings/actions/evidence that don't exist until those phases — when they're built, route the register through `requirement.list`'s real `filter` param (reusing `evaluateFilter`) instead of extending `applyQuickView`'s duplicate logic, so client and server never re-diverge on what a predicate means. | Accepted; revisit when Phase 7/8 quick views need `has_link` |
| A37 | `iso_update_row`'s SQL (009) hard-codes a per-table column allowlist that never included `visibility`/`visible_to_uids` for `iso_requirements`, even though Phase 6's `requirement.update` (JS) already writes those two fields through this exact generic path — a real, pre-existing parity gap between the JS allowlist and the SQL allowlist, invisible until now because the Supabase path has never been run against real Postgres (standing caveat, A9/STOP 1). The migration's own comment says visibility should change "only through the dedicated transition functions", but no such function exists for a plain per-record visibility edit on any entity, and building one now — for a migration nothing has ever applied — would be new, untested surface for no behavioural gain over matching what Phase 6 already shipped. Fixed both sides to agree: added `visibility`/`visible_to_uids` to `iso_update_row`'s `iso_requirements` array (closing the pre-existing gap) and to its new `iso_findings` array (Phase 7.2's own `finding.update`), rather than leaving one silently narrower than the other. | Fixed in 009 (not yet applied) |
| A38 | Two related, least-destructive scope decisions for finding.* (Phase 7.2), neither dictated unambiguously by the plan text: (1) the plan's own §6.2 prose says only "close (auditor)" explicitly for the finding lifecycle verbs — read narrowly, release/finalize could be contributor-level like drafting a finding's content is. Chosen instead: release/finalize/close/withdraw all require audit_manager/auditor (`canProgressFinding`), separate from drafting/editing content (`canManageFinding`, open to contributor too) — a formal audit-workflow sign-off reads as an auditor-tier action across the board, and it is easy to loosen later (a permission check, not a schema choice) but hard to tighten after contributors have relied on it. (2) `FINDING_TRANSITIONS` allows `withdrawn` only from `draft`/`released_for_review`, never from `final`/`closed` — retracting a finalised or closed finding needs its own reopen-style mechanism (mirroring how actions have a real, reasoned `reopen`), which is out of this task's scope to invent unasked; silently allowing it from any state would have been the more destructive, harder-to-walk-back choice. | Accepted; revisit permission tier or add a finding-reopen path only if a real workflow need surfaces |
| A39 | `api/iso.js`'s shared client-projection filter (the one place every list-shaped op's client visibility is decided) had no path for `after_approval` at all — every row with that visibility value was silently excluded for every client caller, regardless of `released_at`, because the filter's OR-chain only ever checked `client_all`/`client_selected`. Invisible until now since `after_approval` is findings-only (by its own DB check constraint) and findings didn't exist as an op until Phase 7.2. Fixed by adding one more OR clause (`visibility === 'after_approval' && !!row.released_at`) — additive only, since no other entity's rows can ever carry that visibility value to begin with. Verified adversarially: reverted the fix, watched `iso-security-test`'s two after-release client-visibility checks fail for real, restored it. | Fixed in Phase 7.2 |
| A40 | `iso_transition_action`'s SQL (009, already written in Phase 3) allows `(v_row.status,'on_hold')` and `('on_hold', v_row.status)` as transition pairs — but `v_row` is the row's CURRENT state at call time, so the second pair only ever reads `('on_hold','on_hold')` once an action is actually on hold, never the status it held before being paused. Neither the SQL nor `ACTION_TRANSITIONS`/`isAllowedActionTransition` (JS) has anywhere to actually store "what to return to" — the plan's own comment next to `on_hold: []` already flags this ("returns to whatever status it was in before, server-tracked at transition time") as a real, not-yet-built piece, not an oversight of this slice. This slice deliberately leaves `on_hold` reachable (from any active status) but not leaveable — `ACTION_TRANSITIONS.on_hold` stays `[]` — rather than inventing a schema change or an activity-log lookback under time pressure. Needs either a new `iso_actions` column (e.g. `on_hold_from_status`) or a documented lookback into the most recent `status_changed` activity entry before deciding. | Accepted; on_hold has no exit path yet — build one before Phase 7.4 exposes on_hold in the UI |
| A41 | Two scope decisions for this action.* slice, both logged rather than silently assumed: (1) `action.verify`/`action.reopen` (the `iso_verify_action`/`iso_reopen_action` RPCs, already written in 009 since Phase 3) are deliberately NOT built this slice — they touch `verification_status`/`verified_by`/`verified_at`/`reopened_count`, columns `db.transition()`'s single-column-plus-extras shape doesn't fit any better than status did for findings, and forcing them through generic `update()` would hit the same "Postgres would reject this, offline wouldn't" class of gap A37 just fixed — they need their own adapter methods (same reasoning as A21/A26/A37), left for the next slice rather than rushed. (2) action subtasks, reviews (`iso_action_reviews`, submission_no) and extension requests (`iso_action_extensions`) are untouched — real, already-schema'd features, just not in this pass. None of this was silently dropped: all three are named in the plan's own Phase 7.3 task text and are the explicit next-up items in RESUME HERE. | Accepted; next slice: action.verify/action.reopen (new adapter methods) + subtasks/reviews/extensions |
| A42 | Built `action.verify`/`action.reopen` (Phase 7.3 slice 2) exactly per A41's plan: new `verifyAction(id,result,notes,expectedVersion,activity)`/`reopenAction(id,reasonCode,reasonText,expectedVersion,activity)` methods on both adapters, mirroring `iso_verify_action`/`iso_reopen_action`'s SQL shape (009) rather than forcing `db.transition()`. One judgment call: `iso_verify_action`'s own SQL has no from-status check at all (only row_version + result enum) and no major-NC gate either — the gate only exists in `iso_transition_action`'s submitted_for_review→ready_for_verification check. Since an action's root cause can still be edited (blanked) after reaching ready_for_verification but before being verified, `action.verify`'s op layer adds its own `majorNcRootCauseGate` call for `result==='effective'` (not for 'ineffective', which never claims the fix worked) — a real, not decorative, second check; proven load-bearing adversarially by blanking `root_cause_summary` after the from-status gate had already passed and confirming the verify call is still blocked. `ACTION_REOPEN_REASONS` (the plan's own §6.2 allowed-reasons list) is enforced in JS only, same division of labour as `majorNcRootCauseGate` (SQL only checks non-null). Reopen permission uses the same `canVerifyAction` tier as verified/closed (§6.2's own wording covers reopening a verified/closed action, not a separate tier). | Done; gate 2424 → see Phase 7.3-slice-2 Progress Log row |
| A43 | While building `subtask.*` (Phase 7.3 slice 3), re-read every existing op using `patchRow`/`update` for a pattern to follow and found `unit.update`/`unit.archive` (written in Phase 3, never exercised by any test) built a patched object (`{ ...unit, name: ... }` / `{ ...unit, archived_at: ... }`) and simply **returned** it — neither op ever called `ctx.db.update`/`patchRow`/anything else that writes to storage. A user editing a unit's name or archiving one would see the change in that single response, then watch it silently revert on the next read — a real, not hypothetical, silent-no-op bug (the same class hard rule 6 exists to prevent). `iso_audit_units` has no `row_version` (same shape as `iso_relationships`/`iso_action_links`), so fixed both ops to call `ctx.db.patchRow` instead, matching that established precedent (A26). Proven adversarially: reverted the fix, watched the new `unit.update actually persisted` check fail for real (still-old name returned), restored it. | Fixed; new `iso-security-test` coverage added, proven-can-fail |
| A44 | While adding `iso_action_subtasks`'s case to `iso_update_row`'s SQL allowlist (009, never applied), read the whole `case p_table` switch to place it correctly and found `iso_audits` had **no case at all** — despite `audit.update`, `audit.setAccess` and `audit.archive` (all Phase 3, all still standing) patching `iso_audits` through exactly this generic RPC path. Every one of those three ops would raise `iso_update_row: table iso_audits has no editable columns` the moment this migration is ever applied and actually exercised — not a hypothetical, a certain failure on first real use. Invisible to every existing test because `iso-dbsb-unit-test` mocks `fetch` and only ever asserts the RPC call's URL/params, never executes the SQL function body itself (this is the same "not verified against real Postgres" standing caveat as A37, just a different concrete instance of it). Fixed by adding the missing case (`title`,`audit_type`,`custom_type_label`,`scope_text`,`exclusions_text`,`planned_start`,`planned_end`,`report_due`,`certification_body`,`lead_auditor_uid`,`internal_notes`,`stage`,`client_portal_enabled`,`default_visibility`,`visibility`,`archived_at` — every field the three ops actually patch) and adding `archived_at` to `iso_action_subtasks`'s own case so `subtask.archive` can use the same generic `update()` path `audit.archive` does, rather than a second `patchRow`-based mechanism for a table that does have a `row_version`. No JS-side change needed — the offline adapter's `update()` never consulted an allowlist. | Fixed in the migration text only (still never applied, hard rule 1) |
| A45 | `action.decideReview`'s status side-effect: `iso_action_reviews`' own `decision` check constraint allows `('accepted','rejected','more_info')`, but `ACTION_TRANSITIONS` from `submitted_for_review` only ever leads to `more_info_required`, `ready_for_verification` or `on_hold` — no separate destination exists for a plain "rejected" outcome. Chosen: both `'rejected'` and `'more_info'` auto-revert the action to `more_info_required` (there is exactly one sensible place to send "not accepted yet" back to), while `'accepted'` deliberately does NOT auto-advance to `ready_for_verification` — mirroring `action.verify`'s own `'ineffective'`-auto-reverts/`'effective'`-leaves-alone split (A42), so the major-NC gate stays checked in the one place it already lives (`action.updateStatus`) rather than being duplicated into a second review-side gate that could drift out of sync with it. | Accepted; revisit if a real workflow need ever wants 'rejected' to behave differently from 'more_info' |
| A46 | `extension.decide('approved')` writes the action's own `due_date` straight through `db.update()`, bypassing `action.update`'s own §6.3a-rule-4 `requireReason` gate for changing an overdue action's due date. Deliberate: that gate exists to make sure a due-date change always carries a reason somewhere on record — an approved extension's reason is already permanently on file in the `iso_action_extensions` row itself (`reason`, plus `decision_note`), so routing the same write back through `action.update` would only demand a second, redundant reason for a change whose justification already exists. The activity log entry for this write carries `reason: "Extension approved: <the original request reason>"` so the audit trail still shows why, just without asking the decider to retype it. | Accepted |
| A47 | Closing out Phase 7.3's own checklist item (not a new slice's worth of scope, but three concrete gaps the checklist text itself calls for) turned up three real, unbuilt pieces: (1) `auditor_review_note` was already in `NEVER_TO_CLIENT` (correctly unreadable by a client) but was never added to `ACTION_WRITABLE_FIELDS` at all — staff couldn't write it either, through any op; added. (2) 009's own column comment on `client_response` ("writable by the client owner/client_admin while assigned..more_info_required") was never implemented — every action op was staff-only up to this point, this is the first ever client-write path for an action. Built narrowly: `action.update`'s `allow()` now also accepts a client caller whose patch touches ONLY `client_response` (checked by field name, not value); `run()` then re-checks the actual row once loaded — the action must be `client_all` or `client_selected`-with-this-uid (not found, not merely forbidden, if not shared at all — no existence leak) and in one of `CLIENT_RESPONDABLE_ACTION_STATUSES` (assigned/in_progress/awaiting_evidence/submitted_for_review/more_info_required — not draft, not verified/closed) or a 403. `clientProjection` changed from `null` to `'action'` since a client can now legitimately receive a real response body from this op (previously always blocked by the "no allowlist declared" defence-in-depth). (3) the plan's own Tests section names "a cause_tree with a cycle → 400" as a required check, but no shape or validation for `cause_tree` existed at all — defined it as a flat `{id, parentId, text}` array (cheaper to validate/diff than a nested structure; the guided-outline UI in Phase 7.4 can still render it as a tree from parent pointers) and added `causeTreeHasCycle` (a parent-chain walk bounded by node count — pigeonhole proves a cycle if the walk ever exceeds it, no separate visited-set needed), wired into `action.update`'s `validate()`. Proven load-bearing: removed the cycle check, watched both the cycle-rejection AND the valid-tree-acceptance checks fail for the right reasons (200 instead of 400, and a stale-version 409 from a re-run against an unchanged row), restored, both green again. | Done; closes out Phase 7.3's checklist item in full except the UI (Phase 7.4) |
| A48 | A40 resolved: added `iso_actions.on_hold_from_status` (009 — a new column, check-constrained to the 7 statuses that can actually enter on_hold; migration still never applied, per hard rule 1). `iso_transition_action`'s SQL bug was exactly as A40 described — `('on_hold', v_row.status)` always read `v_row.status` AFTER it was already `'on_hold'`, so it only ever matched `('on_hold','on_hold')`, never a real exit. Fixed by comparing against the new column instead (`v_row.status = 'on_hold' and p_to = v_row.on_hold_from_status`), and the same `UPDATE` now sets it on entry (`case when p_to='on_hold' then v_row.status else null end`) and clears it on exit. JS mirrors the split already used for `majorNcRootCauseGate`: `isAllowedActionTransition` only does the coarse shape check (`from==='on_hold'` allows any of the 7 resumable statuses, since a pure two-string function has no row to read the real target from), and `action.updateStatus.run()` adds the exact check against `existing.on_hold_from_status`, passing it through `db.transition()`'s `opts.extra` for the offline adapter (Supabase goes through `iso_transition_action`'s own SQL, which sets/clears the column itself — `opts.extra` is a no-op there). Proven-can-fail: removed the `run()`-level exact check, watched the new `iso-security-test` resume-to-wrong-status check fail (cascaded into a suite crash once the wrongly-resumed action then failed a later, now-invalid transition — a real failure, not a clean single-assertion one, but a failure nonetheless), restored, both `iso-logic-test` and `iso-security-test` green again. | Done; A40 closed. Phase 7.4 may now build on_hold UI — resume should offer exactly one destination (`on_hold_from_status`), never a free status picker |
| A49 | Phase 7.4's own Findings slice turned up two real bugs, neither manufactured, both caught by `iso-ui-test.cjs` (not the HTTP-dispatcher suites, which never exercise the client-side JS at all). (1) `mutateReasoned` (the new reason-required retry helper — see the Progress Log row) originally called the browser's native `window.prompt()` to collect a reason. A native alert/confirm/prompt blocks the whole page's JS thread until dismissed; Puppeteer's `page.evaluate()` has no way to dismiss one on its own, so any test path that reached this call hung indefinitely (`ProtocolError: Runtime.callFunctionOn timed out`), not a clean assertion failure — confirmed by adding a temporary `page.on('dialog')` listener and reading the exact dialog message back ("A reason is required to change visibility after release?") before fixing it. Fixed by routing through the app's own callback-based `showPrompt` (index.html, already used everywhere else in this codebase for exactly this purpose — e.g. renaming a folder) instead of the native dialog; `iso-ui-test.cjs` now keeps a permanent (non-verbose) `page.on('dialog')` handler too, so any *future* regression back into a native dialog fails fast (a missed assertion) instead of silently hanging the whole suite for minutes. (2) `_fndTransition` (a finding's status-transition buttons — Release for review/Finalize/Close/Withdraw) called `openFindingDrawer` to refresh the drawer after a successful transition, but never updated the transitioned row inside `_fnd.findings` itself — the drawer re-rendered from the stale, pre-transition cached row (wrong status shown), and the row's now-stale `row_version` then caused the *next* save on that same finding (the very next test step, a visibility change) to fail its own version check, silently reverting to the previous value with no error surfaced to the user beyond a toast. Fixed the same way `saveFindingField` already does it: `Object.assign(row, updated)` into the cached array before re-rendering. Proven-can-fail for both: reverted each fix in turn, watched the exact two `iso-ui-test.cjs` checks it added fail for the right reason, restored, both green again. | Done; closes out both bugs found while building Phase 7.4's Findings slice |
| A50 | Subtasks as real Main Table child rows (the last named-but-deferred item from Phase 7.3-slice-1/Actions-slice-1's own scope notes) turned out to be the smaller of the two remaining Phase 7.4 items, confirmed by reading Workboards' Gantt code first rather than assuming — `_ganttTaskModel`/`_ganttRenderGeneric` are hardcoded to Workboards concepts (groups, dependencies, `_wsOrderTasks`) with no ctx-generic abstraction, while the Main Table's `_mtRowHtml` already carried an unused `depth` parameter since Phase 1, built specifically "so a future ISO ctx has something to implement" (its own comment) — real anticipated architecture, not a bolt-on. Extended `_mtRenderGeneric`/`_mtRowHtml` (index.html) with `_mtRowsHtml(ctx,rows,depth)` (recursive render) and `_mtAllRowsFlat(ctx)` (flat walk for `_mtCellClick`'s row lookup, which previously only searched top-level rows and would silently miss a click on any nested row), both gated behind a new optional `ctx.children(row)` — `undefined`/`null` means "no children," so every existing ctx (Workboards, Requirements, Findings) is unaffected by construction, confirmed by running `mt-adapter-test.cjs` unchanged and green. `buildActionsCtx` implements `children()` (an action's own subtasks, sorted by `sort_key`, `null` for a subtask row since they don't nest further), dispatches `rowAttrs`/`cell`/`editor`/`save` by a new `_isSubtaskRow(row)` check (`row.action_id != null`), and routes a subtask row's name-click (`open()`) to its PARENT action's drawer rather than a dead click or a subtask-only view. New subtask-row cell/editor functions (`subCellHtml`/`subEditorSpec`/`saveSubtaskField`) mirror the existing action ones exactly — status is a checkbox (not a free-choice editor, same reasoning as an action's own status column), due_date/owner_uid are inline-editable, priority is always "—" (subtasks have no priority of their own). Per the A49 register-refresh lesson, `_subtaskAdd`/`_subtaskToggle` (the drawer's own convenience-view checklist, kept as-is rather than removed — an open design choice made explicitly, not an oversight, since both views now stay in sync) each gained a `renderActionsTable()` call so the register reflects a checklist edit immediately, not just on next tab visit; the Main Table's own existing `_mtCellCommit` already called `ctx.rerender()` after every `ctx.save()`, so inline edits made directly on a child row needed no new wiring at all. **One real test bug found and fixed this slice, not a product bug:** the drawer's own subtask-toggle checkbox and the register's new child-row status checkbox both render `onchange="...ISO._subtaskToggle(...)"`, so once the child row existed, `iso-ui-test.cjs`'s pre-existing unscoped `page.$('input[onchange*="_subtaskToggle"]')` selector started matching the register's copy (hidden behind the open drawer modal) instead of the drawer's own visible one — clicking it hit the modal backdrop instead, silently doing nothing, no thrown error, no console error, so the existing "no console errors" check gave false confidence. Found by adding a temporary `console.error` into `_subtaskToggle`'s catch block, confirming it never fired (ruling out an app-side exception), then reasoning through DOM order; fixed by scoping the test's selector to `#iso-drawer-body` specifically. New coverage: extended `iso-ui-test.cjs` (88→94) — a subtask added via the drawer checklist renders as a real indented (28px) child row directly under its parent in the register; the child row's own status cell reflects the checklist toggle already applied; inline-editing the child row's due_date round-trips through a real `subtask.update` call; clicking the child row's name opens the parent action's drawer (identified by its CAPA panel, not just "a drawer opened"). Proven-can-fail (hard rule 7): forced `buildActionsCtx.children()` to return `null` unconditionally — the suite didn't just fail one assertion but crashed outright (`TypeError: Cannot set properties of null`, the child row's due_date cell no longer existing for the test to click), a real failure either way; restored and reconfirmed green (`iso-ui-test` 94/94, full gate 2752/0, 63 suites). | Done; closes the last named Phase 7.4 item besides the action Gantt |
| A51 | The action Gantt (plan §7.4: "actions by start→due grouped by standard/owner/finding; overdue emphasised; drag updates dates through action.update with reason prompt when the action is overdue") turned out to need less new infrastructure than A50's own note assumed — that note was about Workboards' OWN Gantt (`_ganttTaskModel`/`_ganttRender`) having no ctx-generic abstraction, which is still true and irrelevant here: a SEPARATE generic renderer, `_ganttRenderGeneric(model)`, already existed from Phase 2 specifically for a future non-Workboards Gantt, exercised since then only by `gantt-adapter-test.cjs` against a fake model, its own comment saying outright it was "deliberately minimal... real mouse-drag wiring layered on top only once ISO actually needs its own draggable Gantt." That comment was read before starting, not assumed stale, and it was exactly right — this slice is that layering. Extended `_ganttRenderGeneric` to accept an optional second `handlers` argument (the same `{open,beforeCommit,commitMove,toggle,rerender}` contract `_ganttCommitDrag` already expected); when supplied, every row not marked `row.canDrag===false` gets a real move/resize drag wired to new `_ganttGenericMouseDown`/`_ganttGenericOnMove`/`_ganttGenericOnUp` (index.html) — a deliberate, separate state machine from Workboards' own `_ganttDrag`/`ganttBarMouseDown`/etc. (different DOM id prefix, a generic model has no `DB.tasks` to search), mirroring their move/resize-start/resize-end/milestone-only-moves logic but against `model.sections[].rows[]` instead. `handlers` stays optional so `gantt-adapter-test.cjs`'s existing calls (model only) are completely unaffected — confirmed by rerunning it unchanged, still 15/15 green, before writing a single line of the ISO-side ctx. This is the first Gantt anywhere in the app, Workboards included, whose actual onscreen drag has real Puppeteer mouse-simulation coverage rather than being driven directly through `beforeCommit`/`commitMove` (which is all `gantt-adapter-test.cjs` had ever done, and all Workboards' own real drag path has ever had — its `ganttBarMouseDown` chain was never rewired onto `_ganttSrc`/handlers, per the Phase 2 Progress Log's own scoping note). On the ISO side (`iso/iso-core.js`): a new "Action plan" tab (`buildActionPlanModel`/`buildActionPlanHandlers`/`renderActionPlanTab`/`fillActionPlanTab`), reusing `_act.actions/findings/requirements` rather than fetching a separate copy. Grouping resolves "standard" through a chain no single op returns directly — an action's link (finding XOR requirement, set once at `action.create`, mirroring A50's identical fact for subtask grouping) is resolved to a finding's `requirement_id` (added to `finding.list` in the requirement-drawer-traceability slice) and then to that requirement's own `standard_code`, all client-side against already-loaded data rather than a new op, the same "attach the fact rather than invent an op" precedent the traceability slice itself set. Standard-grouped sections reuse `STANDARD_COLOR` (the Requirements register's own map, §6.1) rather than a second copy. `commitMove` calls `mutateReasoned('action.update', ...)`, not a plain `mutate` — the server's existing §6.3a rule 4 (any due_date change on an already-overdue, still-open action needs a reason) fires exactly the same as it already does for the register's own inline due-date editor, so the drag needed no new reason-handling logic at all, only the same helper. One real edge case caught while writing `commitMove`, not by a failing test but by re-reading `_ganttGenericOnUp`'s own contract: a milestone row (only one of start/due set) intentionally leaves the *other* date as JS `undefined` in the drag payload, not a real date — including it in the `patch` object as `{start_date: undefined, ...}` happens to still work today (`JSON.stringify` drops `undefined`-valued keys before the request ever leaves the browser) but says something false about intent, so `commitMove` builds its patch conditionally (`if (start !== undefined) patch.start_date = start`) rather than relying on that serialization accident. New coverage: extended `iso-ui-test.cjs` (94→104) with a real mouse-drag pass — `page.mouse.move/down/up` on an actual bar's `getBoundingClientRect()`, not a synthetic event: the toolbar offers all three group-by options; the default "Standard" grouping resolves a real code via the finding→requirement chain; the test action (due date only) renders as a milestone; switching to "Finding" regroups it under its real finding's ref; switching to "Owner" falls back to "Unassigned" for an owner-less action; dragging a NOT-overdue milestone 3 days forward round-trips through a real `action.update` call with no reason prompt; dragging an already-overdue action's bar DOES prompt for a reason through the app's own `showPrompt` modal (never a native dialog — A49's own lesson held on the first attempt here, no new dialog-hang bug); supplying the reason persists the change through a real `action.update` call. Proven-can-fail (hard rule 7): commented out the `_ganttCommitDrag(...)` call at the end of `_ganttGenericOnUp` — exactly the 3 drag-dependent checks failed for the right reason (dates unchanged, no reason prompt ever appeared), every other check (including the grouping/rendering ones, which don't depend on drag) stayed green, confirming the adversarial cut was precise; restored and reconfirmed byte-identical to the pre-cut file, full suite green again. Confirmed with a clean run (no edits in flight): gate TOTAL and suite count recorded in the action-plan-gantt Progress Log row. With this, Phase 7.4 is fully built — see the updated RESUME HERE. | Done; closes the last remaining Phase 7.4 item |
| A52 | STOP 2's own core-audit-loop review (user asked the agent to run the demo itself — see the stop2-review Progress Log row for the full walkthrough) found three real, previously-undiscovered bugs, none manufactured, none caught by any existing automated suite because none of them exercises the exact cold-session/real-file paths this review did. (1) The ISO workbook-import dialog (`iso/iso-import.js`'s `openImport`) referenced `root.XLSX` directly with no lazy-load — index.html's own SheetJS loader (`_ensureDocViewer('xlsx',...)`) exists specifically for this (shared with the file-preview feature, CVE-2023-30533 note and all) but nothing in the ISO import path ever called it. On a genuinely fresh session (no prior xlsx file preview opened elsewhere) this failed with an opaque `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'read')` the instant a real file was selected. Fixed by awaiting `_ensureDocViewer('xlsx', resolve, reject)` (wrapped in a Promise) before touching `root.XLSX`. (2) The same file's two `/api/iso` fetch calls used `root._adminApiAuthHeaders()` directly — correct in production (Supabase mode) but wrong offline, since that function knows nothing about `api/_isoAuth.js`'s own `X-ISO-Offline-User` header; every offline import attempt failed with "Missing or invalid X-ISO-Offline-User header." iso-core.js's own `_isoAuthHeaders()` already gets the offline-vs-Supabase branch right for every other ISO request (`api()`/`mutate()`) — exported it onto `root.ISO._isoAuthHeaders` and had `iso-import.js` call that (falling back to `_adminApiAuthHeaders()` if `root.ISO` isn't present, keeping `iso-import-test.cjs`'s Node-side, DOM-free exercise of `buildImportPayload`/`parseWorkbook` — which never calls `openImport` at all — completely unaffected). (3) `openRequirementDrawer` set `_fnd.findings`/`_fnd.auditId` (so its own "Findings raised from this requirement" panel works) but never `_fnd.requirements` — `_openAddFinding`'s "Source requirement" dropdown reads that array directly, so on a cold session (Findings tab never visited, `_fnd.requirements` still its initial `[]`) the drawer's own "+ New finding" shortcut rendered an empty dropdown, silently submitting `requirementId:''` and failing `finding.create` with nothing worse than an easy-to-miss toast. `iso-ui-test.cjs` never caught this because it always visits the Findings tab (which populates `_fnd.requirements` via `fillFindingsTab`) before ever using "+ New finding," so it only ever exercised the already-warm path. Fixed by also setting `_fnd.requirements = _req.requirements` alongside the existing two fields (the same object already in scope for `_act.requirements` one line below). A fourth, smaller gap closed the same way: `_adSaveCapa` was missing the `renderActionsTable()` call `_adSaveDetails` (and every status transition) already has, per A49's own register-refresh rule — no CAPA field is a register column today so this had no visible symptom, but it's the same class of omission and a one-line fix to bring in line with the rest of the file. All four found by actually running the real UI against a real, previously-untouched local xlsx fixture (`workboard/ISO Audit/source/ISO 9001, 14001, 45001 Gap Analysis Etcher_Coastal Electrics V2.0.xlsx` — the same one `.agents/iso-import-test.cjs` already uses, gitignored/local-only) through a fresh offline temp server, not by reasoning about the code in the abstract — each was confirmed by the demo script's own crash/error output before being fixed, then the fix was proven by rerunning the exact same demo and watching it get further. `iso-ui-test.cjs` reconfirmed green (104/104) and a full gate run showed no regression after all four fixes — see the stop2-review Progress Log row for the numbers. | Done; closes STOP 2 |

Widened both (and the Staff-filter dropdown and tab-count totals in `_rptTimesheetHtml`, and the Xero-view export `tsExportXeroCSV`, all of which shared the same `isAdmin`-only visibility gate) from `currentUser.role==='admin'` to `_canBill(currentUser)` — a new, separate local variable at each call site, never a rename of the existing `isAdmin` those same functions also use for the plan's own explicit carve-out ("leave 'log time for another staff member' (`#te-staff`) and view-all admin-only" — a DIFFERENT, broader capability than approve/reject/invoice/report, deliberately left untouched). A14 (a Billing non-admin cannot approve/reject their own entries) is enforced in four places, not one: the two single-entry guards (`tsApproveEntry`/`tsRejectEntry`), the two bulk-selection guards (`tsBulkApprove`/`tsBulkReject`, which skip-and-count rather than refuse the whole batch, so a Billing user can still bulk-act on everyone else's selected entries in one go), the two bulk-per-staff-fortnight guards (`tsBulkApproveForStaff`/`tsBulkRejectForStaff`, which refuse outright since the whole call targets one staff id), and the Approve/Reject button RENDERING itself in both register views (`_tsDetailTable`'s per-row `canAct`, `_tsXeroView`'s per-staff-card `canActStaff`) — a true admin is unaffected by any of these (can still approve their own entries, exactly as before). New suite `.agents/billing-perm-test.cjs` (28 checks, added to `gate.sh`): runs against the shared offline gate dev server (not an isolated temp instance, since time entries/staff billing flags are plain Task Hub data, not ISO-scoped) and swaps `currentUser` in place within the same page/localStorage to check as Sam Wignall (u2, the plan's own named example) rather than a genuinely separate browser login — confirmed via `personal-boards-test.cjs`'s own precedent comment that plain Task Hub's `--offline` mode persists per-browser (unlike ISO's dedicated server-side offline store), so a real second `browser.createBrowserContext()` would see none of the fixture data the admin page had just created; proven the hard way first, by writing the dual-context version, watching it fail with Sam's own DB missing every seeded entry, then switching to the correct pattern. Covers: Reports nav/route hidden then visible; approve/reject/invoice refused then working; the Billing checkbox never rendering for a non-admin viewer (defence in depth) and rendering for an admin; the `billing_changed` Audit Trail event; A14's self-approval refusal at both the function and button-rendering level; admin unchanged; `_canBill` false for restricted/client even with a stray `billing:true`. Proven-can-fail (hard rule 7): reverted `_canBill` to admin-only — exactly the 8 Billing-dependent checks failed for the right reason (nav still hidden, approve/invoice/`_canBill` still refusing), every without-Billing/admin-unchanged/defence-in-depth check stayed green; restored, confirmed byte-identical, full suite green again. | A55 | Phase 7b.3's Reports (D13) — the third Phase 7b task. `metric.audit` (audit-scoped, already computing `req_total`/`req_applicable` from a real `reqs` list it had in hand anyway) gained `assessment_pct`/`conformance_pct`, two fields the plan's prose names but no existing op returned: `assessment_pct` = % of applicable requirements with `assessment_state==='complete'`; `conformance_pct` = % of requirements that actually HAVE a recorded `assessment_result` (an un-assessed requirement has no conformance verdict to count either way) whose result is `'implemented_effective'` — both documented scope decisions, not obvious from the plan's own one-line mention, both `0` (never `NaN`/`Infinity`) when their denominator is `0`. The plan's own prose says the ISO-audits tab's open-findings/overdue-actions columns come "from `metric.portfolio`" — read that op directly rather than trusting the name: `metric.portfolio` is a GLOBAL, non-per-audit aggregate (`audits_active`/`audits_by_stage`/etc.) with no `findings_open`/`actions_overdue` fields at all; `metric.audit` (audit-scoped) is the one that actually has them. Used `metric.audit`, one call per board, not the op the plan's prose names, because that's where the real data lives — a second instance this session of the plan's own line numbers/op names drifting from current reality (A53 already found one in `#nav-clients-section`'s gating). On the Task Hub side: a new `tsIsoFilter` state (`'all'`/`'any_iso'`/a specific `isoAuditId`) threaded through `_tsFilterEntries` (gating every other export/detail-table/select-all caller for free, same as every other filter there), a new "ISO board" select in the Timesheet report's own filter bar (options built from the real distinct ISO boards with an entry in the current date range, not a static list), and the three places the plan named that still showed a plain task name for an ISO entry — `_tsDetailTable`'s own Task column (the register `rowHtml` and `timeEntryForReport` were already fixed in 7b.2, since the plan scoped them there; `_tsDetailTable`, `tsExportCSV` and `tsExportPDF` were explicitly 7b.3's own scope, confirmed by re-reading exactly which bullet names which function rather than assuming "already handled"). `tsExportCSV`/`tsExportPDF` get the same ISO-label treatment inline, matching the existing shape of their row-building code, rather than a refactor onto `timeEntryForReport` the plan's prose suggests ("they read timeEntryForReport") but which isn't actually true of the current code (neither function calls it, and routing them through its narrower shape would drop columns like Board/Group/Parent Task that `tsExportCSV`'s own output already has) — a deliberate, documented divergence from the plan's literal wording in favour of matching current reality. New "ISO audits" Reports tab (`ISO.fillReportsTab`, a new third `rptTab` value gated on `window._isoEnabled`, filled asynchronously like `renderAdmin`/`fillAdmin` since it needs `audit.list` + one `metric.audit` round trip per board) — one row per visible board: client/ref/standards/stage/assessment %/conformance % from `metric.audit`, open findings/overdue actions also from `metric.audit`, and hours total/approved/billable computed client-side from `DB.timeEntries` by `isoAuditId` within the tab's own date-range filter (`_isoRpt.from`/`.to`, independent of the Timesheet tab's own `tsDateFrom`/`tsDateTo` — a deliberate choice, since the two tabs are reporting on different things and the plan calls this "the tab's filter," not the Timesheet report's). CSV export reuses `_csvCell`; Excel export reuses `_xlWrite`/`_argb` behind the same `_ensureExcelJs` lazy-loader `wsMtExport` already uses — both referenced via `root.` from inside iso-core.js's own IIFE (its `function` declarations are real `window` properties, unlike `DB`/`currentUser`'s own `let` bindings that needed the `_isoDB()`/`_isoCurrentUser()` bridge functions — the same distinction A31 already documents). **One real test-design flaw caught and fixed by this slice's own proven-can-fail discipline, not a product bug:** the first version of the new ISO-filter checks re-filtered `_tsFilterEntries()`'s own result by this suite's `ISOTIMETEST` note marker before counting — which still landed on exactly the right count even with both `tsIsoFilter` lines deleted outright, since the fixture's other ~14 entries all lack that marker regardless of whether real ISO filtering ran at all. The adversarial deletion passed clean (0 failures) on the first attempt — a real miss, caught by noticing a `0 failures` result looked wrong for a deletion that should matter, not by the test itself flagging anything. Fixed by asserting directly on the returned rows' own shape (`every(e=>!!e.isoAuditId)`, `===auditId`) and by strengthening the "all work" vs "any ISO work" count comparison from `<=` to a strict `<` (which the fixture's real non-ISO entries make a meaningful, not just directionally-safe, assertion) — reran the same adversarial deletion and it now correctly fails 4 checks for the right reason. | A56 | Phase 7b.4's Pro forma (R2/R7) — the fourth Phase 7b task, and the smallest of the four: every claim in the plan's own prose about this task turned out to be literally true once checked against the real code, a contrast worth recording after A53/A55 each found a stale assumption. `pfiConfirmImport`'s line-item builder gained the exact three fields the plan named (`taskTitle`/`boardId`/`boardName`, branching on `e.isoAuditId`) and nothing else needed to change: the import-candidate filter (`entryClientId`) already checks `e.clientId` before falling back to a task lookup, so an ISO entry (which carries a real `clientId` since 7b.2) was already a valid candidate with zero code change; "by project" grouping (`groupKey`/`groupLabel` in `_rptInvoiceHtml`) already reads `li.taskId||li.boardId` and `li.boardName||li.taskTitle` straight off the stored line item, no board lookup at all, so setting `boardId:'iso:'+auditId`/`boardName:isoLabel` at import time was the whole fix; every export path (`pfiExportCSV`/`pfiExportExcel`/`pfiExportPDF`) and the invoice editor's own line table already print `li.boardName` directly, confirmed by grepping every one of them rather than trusting the plan's "already print boardName" line.
