| A57 | Phase 7b.5's Digest (R9) — the fifth Phase 7b task. New op `digest.items {clientId, since}` (staff-only caller; a client has no reason to generate their own digest) returns plain summary STRINGS only, never a raw field from any record — "safe by construction," confirmed by reading the op's own output shape: it only ever interpolates a ref, a stage name, computed percentages and computed counts, never `title`/`internal_notes`/`scope_text`/any finding or action free-text field, so there is no code path through which an internal note could leak even accidentally. One line type named in the plan's own example, "Report issued: …", was deliberately NOT built — no report-issuance feature exists anywhere in this codebase yet (that's Phase 11, unbuilt), confirmed by grepping for any `iso_reports`/`report.issue`-shaped table or op and finding none; inventing a line for a feature that doesn't exist would be fabricating data, a different and worse problem than a client-safety leak. Every audit contributing lines is gated on `client_portal_enabled` (the exact same flag `audit.list` already uses to decide what a client caller can see) — a board not yet shared with its client produces zero lines, not a partial or hedged line. "Actions closed"/"actions due soon" counts are filtered through the existing `isClientVisibleRecord` helper (already used elsewhere in `api/_iso.js` for exactly this purpose) before counting, so an internal-only action is invisible even as a bare number, not just its text. `since` (named in the op's own signature) anchors the "closed" window to a real prior-digest date when the caller has one, falling back to a week otherwise, rather than a hardcoded 7 days that would miss or double-count closures depending on how often staff actually run digests. On the Task Hub side: `_dgOnClientChange` now also calls the new `ISO.digestItems(clientId)` (a thin, uncached wrapper — digest generation is an explicit, infrequent per-client action, not a re-rendered view, so caching would only risk staleness for no benefit) and appends its lines into `#dg-items`, guarded against the exact race the plan's own prose names: the fetch is async, so by the time it resolves the user may have already picked a different client (or none) — the guard re-reads `#dg-client`'s live value and only appends if it still matches the client the fetch was FOR, discarding the result silently otherwise rather than risk writing one client's progress into another's draft. The edit form (`openDigestDetail`) gets a parallel `_dgAddIsoProgress` behind a small "+ Add ISO progress" link, reading `#dg-edit-client`'s live value (not the digest's own possibly-stale stored `clientId`, since the edit form's own client select can be changed mid-edit) with the same still-matches guard before appending. New coverage (`iso-time-test.cjs`, 43→50): the sentinel sweep (every plausible internal/free-text field — `internal_notes`, `scope_text` — salted with a random `SENTINEL-<random>` string, confirmed never to appear anywhere in `digest.items`'s real output); the portal-gate proof (zero lines before `client_portal_enabled`, a real line with a real computed percentage after); a second, portal-disabled board for the same client contributing zero lines of its own (the private-board-leak check the plan names explicitly); the real New-Digest-form client-switch wiring appending real lines; and the client-switched-away-before-the-fetch-resolves race, proven by clearing `#dg-client` immediately after triggering the fetch and confirming the textarea stays empty once it resolves. Proven-can-fail (hard rule 7): removed the `client_portal_enabled` filter from `digest.items`'s own audit query — exactly the 2 portal-gating checks failed for the right reason (a portal-disabled board's line appeared both on its own and alongside the real one), every other check (sentinel sweep, UI wiring, the race guard) stayed green since none of them depend on that one filter; restored, confirmed byte-identical, suite green again. Confirmed with a clean run (no edits in flight): gate TOTAL and suite count recorded in the 7b.5-digest Progress Log row. | Done; closes 7b.5 |
| A58 | Phase 7b.6's Send the Gantt (R2, D14) — the sixth and LAST Phase 7b task, closing out Phase 7b entirely. New op `export.gantt {auditId, clientVersion, groupBy}` (`api/_iso.js`) replicates the Action plan Gantt's own standard/owner/finding grouping server-side (via `iso_action_links`/`iso_findings`/`iso_requirements` lookups), returning the exact Phase 2 generic row model (`{sections:[{id,label,color,rows:[{key,id,name,start,end,color,depth,canDrag}]}]}` — `_ganttRenderGeneric`'s own shape from Phase 2, NOT Workboards' separate HTML-string `_ganttTaskModel`, a plan-wording ambiguity resolved by re-deriving the correct model from the actual code, same practice as A53/A55). When `clientVersion` is true, `actions.filter(isClientVisibleRecord)` runs once, server-side, before any row is built — the same shared predicate `digest.items` (A57) already uses for "would a client ever see this record" — and every returned row is built field-by-field (`id, ref, name, start, end, color, ownerName, status, progress`) rather than spread from the raw DB row, so there is no path for an internal/CAPA/raw-`owner_uid` field to leak even by accident, independent of (and stronger than) the generic `projectForClient` allowlist machinery used elsewhere. `CLIENT_FIELDS.action` gained `start_date` (nothing new exposed beyond "when does this action span," the same date a client could already ask about via `client_response`). Cross-system name resolution (ISO's server has no native access to Task Hub's own client/staff data) extends the exact dual-path precedent `checkClientExists`/`listClientUsers` already established in `api/iso.js`: new `getClientName`/`getStaffName` ctx helpers use a hardcoded fixture map in OFFLINE mode (no real Task Hub blob exists there) and `readBlob()` in production — kept to exactly these two name lookups, never a general "give me the whole blob" ctx method, so nothing broader than a display-name string has a path out of Task Hub's own data. Rendering: a new `_ganttPdf`/`_ganttPdfNow`/`_ganttHexToRgb` (`index.html`, beside `_ganttCommitDrag`) draws the row model as plain jsPDF primitives via the app's existing `_ensureJsPdf` lazy-loader (shared with every other PDF export in the app) — header band (client · ref · title · data-as-at), A3/A4 landscape auto-selected by `spanDays > 120 ? 'a3' : 'a4'` (a simple, documented heuristic, not a pixel-fit search), section bands, bars via `roundedRect` with a progress-fill overlay, milestone diamonds via two `triangle()` calls, a red today-line, page-break-repeating headers, and page-number footers — explicitly no DOM screenshot (no html2canvas/svg plugin loaded, per §3.2) and explicitly avoiding jsPDF's `GState`/alpha-transparency API (not guaranteed present across the pinned jsPDF version every other export already relies on) in favour of a hand-computed "lighten toward white by 38%" RGB blend for the progress fill. No dependency lines are drawn — ISO actions carry no dependency data model at all (unlike Workboards' tasks/`iso_dependencies`), so the plan's "dependency lines when present" wording is correctly satisfied by drawing none, a documented non-gap. UI: a new "Export PDF" button on the Action plan tab toolbar (`iso/iso-core.js`) opens a small modal (client version default-on vs. internal) via new `_openGanttExport`/`_ganttExportApply`, which calls `export.gantt` and hands the result straight to `root._ganttPdf(...)`, saving via `doc.save(filename)` — never `window.open` (hard rule 13), proven both dynamically (a `window.open` call-counter) and statically (grepping `_ganttPdf`'s own body and all of `iso-core.js` for the literal string). New suite `.agents/iso-gantt-pdf-test.cjs` (20 checks, added to `gate.sh`'s `SUITES` list): a fresh offline temp server seeds one client-visible bar action, one client-visible (via `visible_to_uids`) milestone action, and one internal-only action (title salted with a random `SENTINEL-<random>` string); asserts the client `export.gantt` payload includes both client refs, excludes the internal ref, never contains the SENTINEL anywhere in the JSON, and carries no raw `owner_uid` field on any row; the internal payload legitimately includes all three refs and the SENTINEL; a real PDF build (stubbing `window.jspdf.jsPDF`'s constructor to wrap `.text()`/`.roundedRect()`/`.triangle()`/`.save()`, run through the REAL `_ganttPdf` function, not a hand-built fake) confirms the client PDF's drawn text never contains the SENTINEL or the internal ref while the internal PDF's legitimately does, a bar is drawn for the dated action, and a milestone diamond (2 `triangle()` calls) is drawn for the due-date-only action; the `window.open` trap; zero console errors/native dialogs. No other errors were hit this slice — `export.gantt`, `_ganttPdf`, and the UI wiring all worked on the first real smoke-test attempt. Proven-can-fail (hard rule 7): removed the single `if (clientVersion) actions = actions.filter(isClientVisibleRecord);` line from `export.gantt` — exactly the 4 expected leak-detection checks failed for the right reason (the client payload wrongly including the internal ref, the SENTINEL leaking into both the raw JSON and the drawn PDF text), every other check stayed green; restored, confirmed byte-identical via `diff -q`, suite green again. Confirmed with a clean run (no edits in flight): gate TOTAL and suite count recorded in the 7b.6-send-the-gantt Progress Log row — **Phase 7b is now fully built**, all three of its own named acceptance suites (`billing-perm-test`, `iso-time-test`, `iso-gantt-pdf-test`) green together in the same run. | Done; closes 7b.6 and all of Phase 7b |

Built as an **explicit, idempotent admin op** ("Built-in library" card on the ISO admin page, Load / Load all) rather than the start-up load the task text says: a hidden start-up load would put 236 rows into every fresh store, including every test suite's, and `iso-ui-test` asserts exact row counts. | A79 | **8b.6 — Evidence tab, evidence drawer, supersede, board-wide queue.** *Server:* `evidence.get` (staff members of the board): the record, every link with a target label (standard + edition + clause for a requirement, "Finding/Action <ref>" otherwise), verdict, role, why-note, reviewer comment, reviewed revision, link version and linker, plus the record's history (its own entries and every entry about one of its links). `evidence.supersede` (contributor tier, versioned on the old record): the old record's live links (any status but rejected/superseded) become `superseded` with "Superseded by E…", logged on each target as `evidence_superseded`; the new record takes over each of those targets as "Not reviewed", with the same role and note, logged as `evidence_linked`. Search items gain `linkSummary` and `supersededBy`. `evidence.link` refuses a superseded record. **Deliberate consequence:** an acceptance belongs to the revision that was reviewed. When revision 3 is superseded by revision 4, the requirement's accepted count drops until someone reviews revision 4. This matches ISO's own rule that obsolete documented information is no longer valid evidence. It is recorded here so it isn't mistaken for a bug. *UI:* an Evidence tab between Action plan and Settings (5.4b's order), on the shared Main Table with ctx `iso:<id>:evidence`. Columns: type, revision, date, confidentiality, and review status as text + icon in the register Evidence column's words. Two views: "Evidence register (N)" and "⟳ Evidence to review (N)". The evidence drawer (row click) has: a superseded banner; the record's details, with the address as text + Copy link and never an anchor; and the "✓ Accepted for … / ○ Awaiting review for … / ◐ / ✕ / ⊘ Rejected for … / ↻ Superseded on …" summary. Below that sit "Used on (N)" — each target's verdict, role, note and reviewer comment, with the 8b.5 reviewer controls, which now return to the evidence drawer — then a "Superseded by a newer record" picker and the history. The 8b.4 selector still shows a superseded record, disabled, marked "(superseded — link the current revision)". Every change re-fetches the tab, the register summary and the drawer (A49). **Found on the way, fixed:** every ISO date taken from a timestamp used `fmtDate(ts.slice(0, 10))`, which is the UTC date. For a WA viewer (UTC+8) that is a day early for anything recorded before 8 am local. Six existing call sites (comments and activity in the requirement, finding and action drawers; the finding register's Released column; the profile's "confirmed … on") and the three new ones now use `fmtTs`, the viewer's local date. | A80 | **Supabase parity: generic updates lost their activity description.** Found while checking 8b.6's history on both adapters. `_isoDbSupabase.js`'s `update()` passed only actor uid/kind to `iso_update_row`, which then logged every generic update as entity_type = the table name, action `updated`, no field, no reason. The offline store logs the op's own activity object. So on Supabase, once applied: a reason-required change (§6.3a — N/A justification, result changes, overdue date moves) would lose its reason from the audit trail; the drawers' Activity lists (filtered on `requirement`/`finding`/`action`) would never show an update; and 8b.5/8b.6's `evidence_reviewed`/`evidence_superseded` would appear as "iso_evidence_links updated". Latent only, because 009 has never been applied (A9/STOP 1). Fixed both sides. `iso_update_row` (009, edited in place) takes seven optional parameters (entity type, id, ref, action, field, reason, source) and logs them, falling back to the old fixed values only when none is given. The defaults keep the six-argument call — and `iso-db-verify`'s probe — working. It is dropped first, so an older six-argument copy can never sit beside it as an ambiguous overload, and the grant/revoke list names the new 13-argument signature. The adapter passes the activity object's fields. The other write paths (`insert`, `updateMember`, `removeMember`, `upsertConfig`) already log the full object, and the numbered create functions log their own correct entity, so nothing else had this gap. `iso-dbsb-unit-test` 36→39: the update body carries entity, ref, action, field, reason and source; 009's function logs what it is given; the grant names the new signature. Proven-can-fail, two cuts (adapter drops the reason; 009 logs the fixed values again): exactly 1 failure each, restored byte-identical. | Fixed in 009 (not applied) + adapter |
| A81 | **Security: stored script injection through inline handlers (found and fixed).** Found while checking the 8b.6 drawer's Copy link. **What was wrong:** several ISO renderers put user-influenced text inside a JavaScript string in an inline handler, such as `onclick="ISO._evdCopyLink('${esc(e.url)}')"`. `esc()` turns `'` into `&#x27;`, but the HTML parser decodes that back to `'` before the handler runs, so the escaping protects HTML and not the JS string. The URL parser keeps `' ( ) ; /` as they are, so `https://example.com/');window.__xss=1;//` passes `normalizeEvidenceUrl` unchanged as a valid http(s) link. Its Copy link button then ran the attacker's code for whoever clicked it. Anyone allowed to add evidence (contributor and up) could plant it, and it would fire for an admin. **Proven real:** with the old rendering put back, the new regression test records `window.__xss === 1` and a truncated copy. The same class existed for cause-tree node ids: `_rcTreeEdit/_rcTreeAddChild/_rcTreeRemove('${esc(n.id)}')`, where the ids are client-supplied JSON that the server never checked and that a contributor could save. That one fired on typing in a node or clicking its buttons. The class also covered the extension button's `row.due_date`, which Supabase's date type would block but the offline store doesn't. **Fix:** those values now travel in `data-*` attributes read through `this.dataset` (`data-url`, `data-node`, `data-due`), so they are never inside a JS string. The 8b.6 evidence drawer uses the same pattern. Server-side, `action.update` refuses a cause tree unless every node id and parent id fits the existing id alphabet (`ID_RE`) and node text is a string of at most 2000 characters, because a JSONB column gets no type check from Postgres. A sweep of every inline handler in `iso/iso-core.js` and `iso/iso-import.js` found no other user-influenced value in a JS string: the rest are server ids (`ID_RE` excludes quotes), seed keys, constants or row indexes, and there are no single-quoted handlers. No other caller depends on the old forms. Tests: `iso-evidence-test` 25→26 (a crafted link is copied exactly and runs nothing, through the real panel) and `iso-security-test` 345→346 (a quote-bearing node id and parent id are both refused). Proven-can-fail, two cuts: the old Copy link rendering failed exactly the new UI check, with the payload actually firing; dropping the server check failed the new check plus the next save, which hit a version conflict because the crafted tree had been accepted. Both restored byte-identical. | Fixed |
| A82 | **8.2 — evidence file upload, server-controlled end to end (D18: links stay the default).** *Flow:* `evidence.uploadUrl` (contributor tier) checks the target. It then checks the extension against §5.4's allowlist (pdf, docx, xlsx, pptx, csv, txt, png, jpg, jpeg, gif, webp, heic, msg, eml, zip). Each extension has its own accepted types, so a declared type that doesn't match is refused; an empty browser type takes the extension's own. It checks the size against `evidence_max_upload_mb` (default 10, 1–25); the error names the limit and suggests a SharePoint link. It closes the caller's expired reservations (removing any bytes they point at) and allows at most 10 open. It then writes a reservation in the new `iso_evidence_uploads` and returns only the evidence id plus the one signed upload URL. The server picks the evidence id and the path `<client>/<audit>/<evidence>/<random16>.<ext>`; the filename lives only in `original_filename`, with control characters and path separators stripped and cut to 200 characters, extension kept. The form's fields travel in the reservation, so `evidence.register` takes nothing but the evidence id. `register` requires the same caller, still with upload rights, and an unexpired reservation; it reads the object's own metadata. An object over 25 MB, a size other than the declared one, or a type outside the extension's list is removed and refused (reservation closed as `refused`), and no record is created. Otherwise it creates the record — kind `file` (`photo` for an image), real size and type, **document status Draft** — and links it **"Not reviewed"** with the chosen role and note (D20, D22). It logs `evidence_uploaded` plus `evidence_linked` and closes the reservation as `registered`. No assessment field changes. `evidence.downloadUrl` never takes a path: it signs the record's own path for 120 s as an attachment under the original filename, or offline returns the bytes, and logs `evidence_downloaded` every time. Staff members of the board only; clients are refused, as for every evidence op. Responses never carry `storage_path`. `evidence.storageUsage` (admin) feeds "Evidence storage used: X in N files" next to the upload cap on the ISO admin page. That is the plan's "Settings shows storage used"; storage is global, so it sits where the cap is set. **Offline:** there are no signed URLs, so `uploadUrl` says `offline: true` and the bytes come through `/api/iso` as base64 (`evidence.offlinePut`, offline only, the same one-time reservation-scoped write, at most 4 MB so they fit serve.mjs's 6 MB body cap). They are stored under `.iso-offline/files/` named from the server path with separators flattened and the extension dropped — never the client's filename — beside a `.type` sidecar that stands in for Storage's object metadata, so register's type check runs offline too. Offline downloads return base64 inside the JSON response rather than serving the bytes as their own resource, which leaves no content-sniffing surface at all. *Preview:* following the old 8.3 instructions, index.html's `wsPreviewFile` viewer code moved unchanged into `_previewFileData(data, name, type, opts)`. `wsPreviewFile` calls it with `{id}`, so Workboards' output is identical (`file-preview-test` green). ISO calls it with `allowSvg:false`, so an SVG is never rendered inline. *UI:* the Add-evidence form still opens on the link option; "Upload a file instead" swaps the type and address fields for a file picker that accepts the allowed types only, and choosing a file fills the title. Evidence rows in all three drawers and in the evidence drawer show "File: name · size · Draft" with Preview (the shared viewer) and Download (Blob + `<a download>` — no navigation, no tab, hard rule 13). *SQL (010 amended in place, unapplied):* `iso_evidence.document_status` (draft/approved/superseded/obsolete, default draft), `approver_uid` and `effective_date` — the 8c.5 columns, needed now because the upload must start at Draft — plus their `iso_update_row` case. Also: the `iso_evidence_uploads` table (RLS on, no policies; not under the no-delete trigger, as §4.4 allows); `iso_create_evidence` taking the file and document fields as optional parameters (old signature dropped first, as in A80); and the bucket SQL as notes (private, 26214400 bytes, the allowed types, no policies). *Function lockdown:* 010's and 014's functions had been left executable by PUBLIC, Postgres's default. They are security invoker over revoked, RLS-locked tables, so nothing could run through them, but they now get 009's explicit revoke/grant. Adapters: offline `TABLES`, the storage naming and sidecar, and `storageGet`; Supabase `FILTERABLE`, `iso_create_evidence`'s new parameters, and `storageHead` returning the type (read from either object-info shape — not yet verified against a real project, the standing A9 caveat). **Known limitation:** no virus scanning exists. New suite `iso-upload-test` (55 checks, port 3142): the full offline round trip (server-chosen path, no extension or client filename on disk, one-time send, register checks, Draft and Not reviewed, byte-identical download, a caller path ignored, every step logged); every refusal (allowlist, type mismatch, the configured cap with a message naming it, the 4 MB offline cap, someone else's upload, not arrived yet, a size or stored type that differs, over 25 MB, expired, the 10-upload cap with expiry clean-up, clients, another client's board, a link); storage used; no assessment change; and the browser flow (link option first, the file picker, title from the filename, Preview of a PDF and a photo, Download of the exact bytes, SVG never inline for ISO while Workboards keeps its behaviour, the admin storage line, no window.open, no request leaving the local server). `iso-dbsb-unit-test` 39→42 (file columns sent, a link sends none, both object-info shapes). One test-harness fix on the way: the download check first fetched the blob: URL, which the page's CSP rightly blocks; it now captures the Blob where it is created. Proven-can-fail, five cuts: register skipping the size check failed exactly its 2 checks; downloadUrl honouring a caller path failed exactly 1; uploadUrl skipping the type match failed exactly 1; the viewer rendering SVG for ISO failed exactly 1; uploads landing as Approved failed exactly the 4 Draft checks (API, list, drawer, server). All restored byte-identical. | Done; closes 8.2 |

| A84 | **4b.2 — new library editions as DRAFT seeds, and "what changed".** Five new seed files, all `draft`. None is offered to a board until an admin approves it, having checked it against the licensed standard; that check stays with the user. **ISO 9001:2015+A1:2024, ISO 14001:2015+A1:2024, ISO 45001:2018+A1:2024**: the approved 4b.1 rows unchanged, plus the two climate-action statements the 2024 amendment adds (4.1: decide whether climate change is a relevant issue; 4.2: a note that interested parties can have climate-related requirements), in Etcher's wording. The amendment text was verified from the primary source, the IAF/ISO Joint Communiqué of 22 Feb 2024, read in full. It names ISO 9001:2015, 14001:2015 and 45001:2018 among the amended standards and says the overall intent of 4.1/4.2 is unchanged. The edition name uses the consolidated notation `2015+A1:2024`. **ISO 9001:2026** (published 16 Sep 2026; transition to 30 Sep 2029) and **ISO 14001:2026** (published April 2026; three-year transition): the 2015 rows, the amendment rows, and new rows for each change the free public material names, each change citing its source. For 9001: quality culture and ethical behaviour (5.1), the policy linked to context and strategy (5.2), risks and opportunities separated (6.1), awareness of quality culture and ethics (7.3), leadership driving improvement (10), clause 8 terminology only, and clause 9 unchanged. For 14001: environmental conditions such as climate, biodiversity and resources in context (4.1), the life-cycle perspective for scope (4.3), top management supporting all relevant roles (5.1), "Planning and managing of changes" (placed at 6.3, its harmonized-structure position, to be confirmed), "all potential" emergency situations, externally provided processes (8.1), EMS effectiveness up front (9.1), defined objectives for each audit (9.2), and definitive review inputs (9.3). Sources: ISO/TC 176's release note and BSI's key-changes pages for both standards (read), and ISO's own pages, which refuse automated reading, so they are cited as "search-engine extract only". Every 2026 change says it must be checked against the licensed standard, and 2015 numbering is kept wherever no source gives another. The published 2026 texts were not available to Etcher, so the drafts carry no claim beyond what the sources state. **ISO 45001**: still 2018 + Amd 1:2024; its revision is at DIS (ballot closed 9 Sep 2026, publication expected first half of 2027), so no 2027 seed yet. Add it when published. *Mechanics:* a one-off generator (scratchpad) copies every base row byte-for-byte and adds new rows with stable keys from the import's own `requirementMatchHash`, grouped like their sibling rows; no client data. `library.loadSeed` with no key now loads approved seeds only ("Load all" never pulls in a draft); a draft loads on its own key. `audit.create` accepts `\d{4}(+A\d:\d{4})?`, and `pickApprovedVersion` orders `2026` > `2015+A1:2024` > `2015`. **"What changed"**: `buildGuidance` returns, per requirement, the seed's changes for its clause or a parent clause (sources resolved) and whether the row is new in the edition. It is read from the seed file when requested, so no schema change. The guidance card shows "What changed in ISO 9001:2026" with a kind and clause tag, the summary, and each source named with Copy link (never an anchor; extract-only sources say so). The admin Built-in library card lists all eight seeds with "Load draft for review" and a "What changed (N)" list. **Approve now asks first**, in the app's own modal ("cannot be undone… only after checking every requirement against the licensed standard"), because library approval has no undo. New suite `iso-editions-test` (85 checks, port 3143): the seed files (status, base rows carried unchanged, added rows, stable keys, Etcher wording — not the amendment's sentences, no "shall" — no client name, sourced changes, extract labelling); loading (Load all = reviewed only; a draft by key; drafts not offered; edition validation; pinning by name); approval through the UI's confirmation; boards on each edition (100 / 106 / 73 / 75 requirements); guidance (new-in-edition, clause and clause-wide notes, nothing on a 2015 board); templates still mapping onto 2026 boards; the drawer card. `iso-library-seed-test` B1 now expects 3 reviewed + 5 drafted seeds. Proven-can-fail, four cuts: "Load all" pulling in drafts failed 7; guidance without changes failed 8; editions back to a bare year failed 2; Approve without asking failed 2. All restored byte-identical. The first runs of three of these cuts crashed the suite on missing data, the same test-design flaw as in 8c.5, now hardened. Cuts 1, 2 and 4 were run before the pinned-edition check was added, cut 3 after. | Done; closes 4b.2 (approving the drafts stays with the user) |
| A85 | **8c.6 — the remaining eleven templates, source-backed checklists, and a preview race fixed.** Eleven new draft templates (STOP R2), §5.8 shape, requirement mappings computed as in 8c.2, Etcher's own drafting. The nine left on the brief's list — IMS Scope Statement, Environmental Aspects and Impacts Register, Hazard and Risk Register, Legal and Other Requirements Register, Objectives and Targets Register, Competency Matrix, Communication Plan, Document Control Procedure, Emergency Preparedness and Response Plan — plus the two the workbook names: Supplier and Contractor Evaluation, and Training and Induction Record. **Checklists (the held-back registers, A63):** a new `checklist` question kind. Each item names one instrument or regulation part and cites a source verified on its official site this session (checked 2026-10-03). The aspects register has 9 areas: Environmental Protection Act 1986 (WA), Controlled Waste Regulations 2004 (WA), Contaminated Sites Act 2003 (WA), Dangerous Goods Safety Act 2004 (WA), Ozone Protection and Synthetic Greenhouse Gas Management Act 1989 (Cth), NGER Act 2007 (Cth), EPBC Act 1999 (Cth), and the IAF/ISO climate communiqué. The hazard register has 15 areas: the WHS (General) Regulations 2022 (WA) parts (electrical 4.7, falls 4.4, confined spaces 4.3, noise 4.1, manual tasks 4.2, high risk work 4.5, plant ch 5, construction ch 6, chemicals ch 7, asbestos ch 8, crystalline silica ch 8A, psychosocial 3.2 div 11, commercial driving 4.10) and the WHS (Mines) Regulations 2022 (WA) on electricity and principal mining hazards. The legal register has 11 instruments: the above plus the WHS Act 2020 (WA) and the Electricity (Licensing) Regulations 1991 (WA). The competency matrix has 6 credentials. A ticked item becomes a confirmed answer with its source; **nothing unticked ever reaches the prompt**, unknown ids are ignored, and the user's own additions are labelled as theirs. The builder never prints a template's source list, so unticked instruments can't leak through it. No item names a regulator or department. WA's 2025 changes moved the WHS Act and the Dangerous Goods Safety Act to the Department of Local Government, Industry Regulation and Safety (the official home pages say so), which is exactly why names stay out (A62). Instruments not verified this session (Mining Act, Biodiversity Conservation Act, Aboriginal Heritage Act) are left out on purpose; "Other" covers them. **Bug found and fixed (8c.4's dialog):** several quick changes start several preview refreshes, and a slower, older answer could overwrite the newest preview with stale text. The checklist's rapid ticks exposed it as an intermittent failure. Only the newest refresh may now update the preview (a sequence number), and each caller still gets its own result. A deterministic test holds the first request back with request interception, so it answers last; without the fix it fails. `iso-prompt-test` 145→218: the eleven drafts; checklist sourcing and no regulator names; the builder listing only ticked items with sources in template order (tick order irrelevant, byte-identical), placeholders for nothing ticked, an unreadable answer or Quick mode, a hazard area with its regulation part; the dialog rendering, live preview, saving, reopening; and the race. The 8c.2 counts now expect twenty templates. Proven-can-fail: the builder listing every item failed exactly 4; the dialog not collecting ticks failed exactly 4; removing the race guard failed exactly the 2 race-dependent checks. All restored byte-identical. | Done; closes 8c.6 (approving the templates stays with the user, STOP R2) |
| A86 | **library.retire — "how do I remove it" (the user asked, after seeing the admin library card with no way to undo a Load).** Never a real delete: ISO records are archived, never deleted everywhere else in this schema (`iso_no_hard_delete` raises on every substantive table), so the new op marks a version `retired` instead — a state the `status` check constraint already allowed since 009 was first written, just never used. `pickApprovedVersion` already excludes anything but `status==='approved'`, so a retired edition is automatically never offered to a new board, with zero change needed there; `library.approve` already refuses anything but `status==='draft'`, so a retired draft can never be re-approved either. One op serves two UI labels: an **approved** edition being withdrawn is "Retire" (logged `retired`); a **draft** nobody ever approved is "Discard draft" (logged `draft_discarded`) — same guarantee either way, since neither ever reached a real board. Boards already created on a retired edition are completely unaffected: `requirement.list` reads `iso_requirements` directly and never re-checks the parent version's status, confirmed by a real board created before the retire, then re-read after it, with its requirement count and pinned edition label both unchanged. Retiring is itself final (no un-retire), so — matching `library.approve`'s own precedent — it asks first, in the app's own modal, naming the real consequence ("no longer offered to new boards" / "it was never offered to any board", and that existing boards keep working). Admin-only; an unknown id 404s; retiring an already-retired version a second time is refused. Tests added as Part D of `iso-library-seed-test.cjs` (52→73): a draft loaded then discarded (logged correctly, can't be re-approved, can't be retired twice); an approved edition a real board already uses, retired, with that board's requirement count and edition label proven unchanged before/after, and a new board on the now-fully-retired standard correctly refused; the admin page's real DOM — a draft row offers both Approve and Discard draft, an approved row offers only Retire, a retired/discarded row offers neither — through an exact Standard+Edition cell match (never a fuzzy text search, which the version table's own concatenated row text makes unreliable); the confirmation modal's exact copy; nothing retired until the confirm button is actually clicked; the server (not just the DOM) really changed status. Proven-can-fail, three cuts: dropping the already-retired guard failed exactly the double-retire-refused check; collapsing the two logged actions into always `retired` failed exactly the discard-logging check; dropping the modal's "boards already created… keep working" sentence failed exactly the modal-copy check. All restored byte-identical. Gate: **TOTAL 3710 / FAILURES 0, 73 suites, clean run.** | Done |
| A87 | **9.1 — `api/_isoCalc.js` completed (plan §7's own "one module computes every number").** The module had never actually been extracted — `metric.audit`/`metric.portfolio` computed everything inline in `api/_iso.js` since Phase 3, with a comment describing the future file as if it already existed. It is now a real, pure, DB-free module (no `ctx`, no clock — `nowIso` comes in as a parameter), directly unit-tested; `metric.audit`/`metric.portfolio` are now thin wrappers that fetch the raw rows and hand them straight to it, so the server and the test suite can never compute two different answers for the same inputs. **Four real, pre-existing bugs found by cross-checking the live code against the plan's own §7 table** (not assumed — each confirmed by reading the exact code, the exact spec row, and in two cases an existing passing test that had locked in the wrong behaviour): (1) `conformance_pct` divided by `assessedReqs.length` (only requirements that already have a recorded result), overstating conformance by dropping every not-yet-assessed applicable requirement from the denominator entirely — §7 is explicit that unassessed must stay in the denominator and count against the figure. (2) The old `assessment_pct` counted `assessment_state='complete'` regardless of whether `assessment_result` was ever recorded — `requirement.update`'s own validation never requires the two together (confirmed by reading `validateRequirementPatch`: it only blocks a result *outside* complete/needs_follow_up, never requires one *inside* complete), so a requirement could be "assessed" with no verdict at all. Renamed to the spec's own name, `assessment_completion_pct`/`req_assessed`, now requiring both. (3) `shared_actions` counted any action with ≥2 distinct *target rows*, including two requirements of the *same* standard (not "shared" by §7's own definition: "≥2 standards") and including links already rejected by `action.unlink` (it never filtered by `link_state` at all, so an un-linked target kept counting). Fixed to resolve every action to the actual *standards* its confirmed links (direct, or via a linked finding's own requirement) reach, and only confirmed links count. One existing test (`iso-security-test.cjs`) had this exact wrong behaviour pinned (two same-standard targets expected to read `shared_actions >= 1`); updated to assert the now-correct `=== 0` for that fixture, with the real cross-standard positive case moved into the new `iso-calc-test.cjs` where metric correctness belongs. (4) `actions_by_owner` counted every action regardless of status and never split by `owner_kind`, so a client's own closed-out action still inflated an Etcher auditor's apparent open workload and the two owner kinds were mixed in one bucket; fixed to open-only, `{etcher:{}, client:{}}`. **New, genuinely computable from columns that already exist:** `req_by_result`, `req_by_state`, `req_conforming`, `findings_closed`, `actions_closed`, `actions_completed`, `actions_verified`, `actions_by_status`, `overdue_by_unit` and `findings_by_unit` (attributed only through a requirement's own `unit_ids` — findings and actions have no unit of their own), `risk_distribution` (risk_severity × linked finding classification). Portfolio gained `report_deadlines_upcoming`, `progress_by_audit`/`progress_by_client` (reusing the exact same requirement-level formula per audit, so portfolio and per-audit completion can never drift apart), `findings_by_standard`, and a real portfolio-level `workload_by_auditor` (audits led + open assigned actions + unassessed requirements assigned — the plan's own 3-part formula; the old per-audit field of the same name was a much narrower, unrelated calculation and is dropped, confirmed by grep that nothing ever read it). **Named, not built** (the project's own "don't invent scope for an unbuilt feature" discipline, same class as A41/A57/A59/A82): `baseline_vs_current` — `iso_requirements.baseline_result`/`baseline_state`/`baseline_at` exist in 009 but nothing anywhere has ever written them, confirmed by grep; capturing a baseline is its own real design decision (when is it taken?), the same class of gap as A40. `responses_awaiting_review` — the plan's own §7 table already says "added in Phase 13"; not a gap. The `{key,value,numerator,denominator,filter}` descriptor wrapper that would let a dashboard turn every number into a link — Phase 9.2's own job, since the dashboard that would consume it doesn't exist yet. New `docs/iso-audit/calculation-dictionary.md` (every metric, formula, inclusion/exclusion, the two deferred items and why). New `.agents/iso-calc-test.cjs`: a fixture per metric plus the plan's own named edge cases (zero requirements, all N/A, only-unassessed), including the dangling-link and rejected-link exclusion cases, plus a short HTTP section proving `metric.audit`/`metric.portfolio` are faithful wrappers (rebuilds the same answer independently from the same raw offline-store rows). Proven-can-fail, four cuts directly on `api/_isoCalc.js`: reverting the conformance denominator failed exactly 1; dropping the result-required rule failed exactly 1; reverting shared_actions to distinct-target-rows failed exactly 1; removing overdue_by_unit failed exactly 2. All restored byte-identical. Gate: **TOTAL 3761 / FAILURES 0, 74 suites, clean run.** **Correction (later, 2026-10-03):** the "nothing anywhere has ever written them" claim about `baseline_*` is wrong — `api/_isoImport.js` writes `baseline_result`/`baseline_state`/`baseline_at` on a requirement's first workbook import (line 321 for new rows, 351 for library-copied rows never baselined); the grep behind the claim missed it. So `baseline_vs_current` is computable for any board that has had a workbook import (the old standalone dashboard's "Original assessment (from the workbook)"); only a board never imported has no baseline. Also found: Not applicable can be stored two ways — `applicability='not_applicable'`, or `assessment_result='not_applicable'` (`ASSESSMENT_RESULTS` allows it) — and `computeRequirementMetrics` excludes only the first, while §7 says "result/applicability not_applicable". The workbook has no N/A status, so only a manual result edit triggers it; the fix needs a decision (the filter schema has no OR, so changing the formulas alone would break the count = filtered-view guarantee). Both carried into the dashboard rework. | Done; closes 9.1. Next: 9.2 (dashboard tab) and 9.3 (charts) |
| A88 | **9.2/9.3 — the audit dashboard tab and `iso/iso-charts.js`, closing the `{key,value,filter}` descriptor 9.1 named as this phase's own job.** `finding.list`/`action.list` gained the same `{filter}` parameter `requirement.list` already had (identical shape, same `evaluateFilter` path) — a dashboard click and its underlying register view now run through literally the same filter evaluator, so they can never silently diverge. New `buildDashboard(metrics, extra)` in `api/_isoCalc.js` wraps `computeAuditMetrics`'s own fields into the plan's `{key, label, value, filter}` tiles and `{key, value, filter}` chart segments — a number is never a second, separately-maintained definition of what it counts, only ever the exact field plus a filter object proven (by `iso-dashboard-test.cjs` Section B) to round-trip through the real list op to exactly that count. New `dashboard.audit` op (`api/_iso.js`) assembles it: `fetchAuditCalcInputs` (factored out of the old inline `metric.audit` body, now shared by both ops so they can never compute different answers for the same audit), `computeProgressByGroup` (generic — by unit or by owner, excludes `not_applicable` requirements from every group even when they carry a unit tag), and `computeProgressOverTime` (replays `iso_activity` rows where a requirement's `assessment_result` actually changed, chronologically regardless of input order, into a daily `{conforming, with_result}` count series — a count, not a percentage, because the applicable denominator can itself shift over time and isn't reconstructed; named in the dictionary rather than approximated). **13 KPI tiles:** applicable requirements, assessment complete %, conformance %, open findings, open actions, overdue actions, actions verified effective, requirements with accepted evidence (a real `has_link` filter), shared actions across standards, evidence accepted/awaiting review (all targets), plus baseline-vs-current and client-responses-awaiting-review — the two 9.1 already named as not-built — shown as visibly-inert "Not yet available" tiles with the real reason, never a fabricated number (same precedent as 8c.4's disabled Upload button). **Two KPIs stay deliberately non-clickable** (`filter: null`): `shared_actions` (">=2 distinct standards" is an aggregate across all of an action's own links — the filter schema's `has_link` can only express "at least one matching link", never that), and `evidence_accepted`/`evidence_submitted` (span requirement+finding+action targets together — no single `<entity>.list` view could ever match the count exactly, so a filter here would fail the plan's own testing bar rather than satisfy it). **15 charts** (`iso/iso-charts.js`, loaded before `iso-core.js`): four clickable bar charts with a real per-segment filter (`req_by_result`, `req_by_state`, `findings_by_classification`, `actions_by_status`); `actions_open_vs_closed`, `evidence_status` and seven 2D cross-tabs (`req_by_result_by_standard/clause`, `actions_by_owner`, `overdue_by_unit`, `findings_by_unit`, `risk_distribution`, `progress_by_unit/owner`) shown as plain text/colour data with no per-cell drill-down — a cross-tab cell would need two combined predicates the current filter-building code doesn't assemble, named here rather than quietly promised; `progress_over_time` as an SVG line. Every chart: `role="img"`, a real `aria-label`, a visible "As at" caption (`data_as_at`, never a live clock read in the browser), text values alongside colour, and its own honest empty-state sentence (`emptyMessage`, e.g. "No findings raised on this board yet" instead of one generic phrase everywhere). Click-through reuses A81's pattern exactly: the filter travels as `data-filter="${esc(JSON.stringify(...))}"`, read via `this.dataset.filter` in `ISO._dashOpenFiltered` — never built into an inline `onclick` string. "Print-friendly" is satisfied only in the narrow sense that every chart is plain inline SVG/HTML with no canvas bitmap, fixed overlay or screen-only trick that would break in a print context; no dedicated print stylesheet or page-break rule was added and no print preview was visually checked, so this is named rather than claimed as verified. **Three bugs caught and fixed before anything shipped, none released broken:** (1) the first draft of `buildDashboard` stored a `whereFor` *function* per chart segment — a function cannot survive `JSON.stringify` over an HTTP response; rewritten as `segmentsFor`, which resolves a real, plain filter object per segment server-side before it ever reaches the wire (`iso-calc-test.cjs` now asserts the whole dashboard payload survives a JSON round trip unchanged). (2) `iso-core.js`'s per-tab `dashFilter` was computed by checking `_req.auditId === audit.id` *before* consuming a fresh `_pendingDashNav`, so a just-clicked dashboard filter could be silently dropped whenever `_req.auditId` still pointed at the previous board; fixed by consuming the pending nav first, letting it unconditionally override a stale persisted filter. (3) **the double-filter bug, the significant one:** `buildRequirementSections`/`buildFindingSections`/`buildActionSections` each apply their own local quick-view predicate unconditionally on top of whatever rows they're given — so a dashboard click's server-correct, already-filtered rows could be silently re-narrowed (in the worst case to zero) by an unrelated quick-view a user had left active in that tab (e.g. "Verified & closed" hiding a dashboard-selected overdue action). Fixed in all three builders by forcing the pass-through "all" view whenever `.dashFilter` is set, and by hiding (not just disabling) the quick-view button row itself so nothing on screen contradicts the dashboard's own filter banner. This is the one regression `iso-dashboard-test.cjs` Section C pins directly: it sets Actions to "Verified & closed" (correctly showing 0 of the fixture's open/overdue actions), clicks the dashboard's "Overdue actions" KPI, and requires exactly 1 row to still show. New suite `iso-dashboard-test.cjs` (48 checks, port 3145, three sections): **A** (8) — `dashboard.audit`'s numbers equal `metric.audit`'s own fields exactly; chart segments sum to their parent total; the two not-built tiles show `value:null` with a real sentence, never a number. **B** (22) — every clickable KPI and every 1D chart segment round-trips through `requirement.list`/`finding.list`/`action.list` to exactly its own count, including the `has_link` filter (the reviewer fixture needed an explicit `member.add` with `auditRole:'auditor'`, since neither plain staff nor `{role:'restricted'}` alone ever grants `can_verify` — found while wiring the fixture, now asserted with explicit status checks on both setup calls so a silent 403 can't recur); `shared_actions` confirmed `filter:null`. **C** (18) — tab position, KPI/chart render counts, accessibility, a real chart click navigating and filtering correctly, the double-filter regression above, Clear filter restoring the quick-view row, honest empty states on a fresh board (including progress-over-time's own specific message), no `window.open`, no request leaving the local server, no console errors. `iso-calc-test.cjs` grew 51→70: pinned unit tests for `computeProgressByGroup` (zero requirements, by-unit, by-owner, a requirement with no group keys), `computeProgressOverTime` (zero activity, a non-change, a non-requirement row ignored, same-day coalescing, order-independence), and `buildDashboard` (JSON round trip, a KPI's value/filter, the two not-available tiles, a chart segment's value/filter, the `isnull`-not-`eq-undefined` unassessed case, `shared_actions`/`req_with_accepted_evidence`'s filters). Proven-can-fail, six cuts: removing the `not_applicable` exclusion from `computeProgressByGroup` failed 2; dropping the chronological sort from `computeProgressOverTime` failed 1; reverting the `unassessed` segment to `eq: undefined` failed 1 in `iso-calc-test` and 1 in `iso-dashboard-test`'s own HTTP round trip; denying `dashboard.audit`'s `allow()` unconditionally surfaced as a loud crash (an authorized admin call failing is not a case the harness needs to degrade gracefully for); removing `action.list`'s filter block failed 1; reverting the Actions double-filter fix failed exactly the 1 regression check Section C exists to catch, with the other 17 checks in the same section staying green (confirming the test isolates that one behaviour precisely). All six restored byte-identical. `.agents/gate.sh` gained `iso-dashboard-test` in its suite list. First gate run **3833 / 1**: the one failure was `iso-evidence-review-test`'s 8b.6 check pinning the old tab order (Evidence directly before Settings); the Dashboard tab now sits between them on purpose, so the check expects `…|Evidence|Dashboard|Settings` (178/0 alone straight after). Rerun: **TOTAL 3834 / FAILURES 0, 75 suites, clean run.** **Correction (found after closing, same day):** the baseline-vs-current tile should not say "Not yet available" — the workbook import does save the original assessment (see A87's correction); fixing it belongs to the dashboard rework. | Done; closes 9.2 and 9.3. | A89 | **9.4 + 9.5 — dashboard stage 1, after the user's review of a ChatGPT-drafted dashboard brief (D26–D31).** The user compared the old standalone dashboard with 9.2's and asked for a review; the agreed direction and the user's answers are D26 (the ISO dashboard is the tab inside one board — per client, never Task Hub's home Dashboard, staff-only until Phase 13), D27 (Dashboard first and the landing tab), D28 (conformance as "X of Y conforming (Z%)" with the formula on the page), D29 (standard is the only filter in stage 1), D30 (N/A is an applicability decision only) and D31 (yearly maintenance audits as linked boards — Phase 9b, not built yet). **9.4 corrections:** `computeBaselineVsCurrent` — the workbook import does write `baseline_*` on a requirement's first import (A87's "nothing writes it" was wrong), so the dashboard now shows "Workbook baseline vs now" over the same baselined requirements on both sides, or says plainly that the board was never imported. D30: `requirement.update` refuses result `not_applicable` (pointing to Applicability) and both result pickers drop it, so every formula excludes N/A through `applicability` alone and every filter (no OR in the schema) still reproduces its count. The 9.2 "Assessment complete" tile's filter matched the state only; the new Assessed figure's filter also requires a result. **9.5:** `openBoard` lands on Dashboard unless a caller names a tab (it used to keep the last tab — and from the landing page that was Settings, because `openLanding` set `route.tab = 'settings'`); a reload still keeps the current tab. The tab order is Dashboard · Requirements · Findings · Actions · Action plan · Evidence · Settings. A header adds only what the top strip lacks: the client's name, audit type, stage, planned dates, report due and last update (the newest activity that changed something — downloads and prompt copies excluded). **Standard filter (D29):** `dashboard.audit {standard}` (validated: digits, and one of the board's own standards) narrows every input through the new pure `scopeInputsToStandard` — findings by their source requirement, actions by any confirmed link reaching an in-scope requirement directly or through a live finding (so one action can count under two standards, by design), evidence links, requests and suggestions by their target — so every figure and chart follows it. Click-through keeps it: `finding.list` now attaches each finding's `standard_code`, `action.list` each action's `standard_codes` (resolved exactly as the scope function does), and the filter schema gains one op, `contains`. **KPI row:** nine figures as real buttons in two groups — Assessment (applicable · assessed "X of Y" with the percentage and how many have a result but need follow-up · not assessed · gaps identified · conforming "X of Y") and Follow-up work (evidence to review · open findings · open actions · overdue actions). Each is described by its formula (kept outside the button so a screen reader doesn't read the sentence as its name), and "How these numbers are calculated" lists all nine on the page. Each opens its register filtered to exactly what it counted; the evidence figure opens Requirements on the register's own "Evidence to review" view, carried in the pending navigation alongside the filter. The banner names what was clicked and the standard, counts the rows actually shown, and gains "Back to dashboard" (the standard is kept in the session route, never across boards). A sequence guard stops a slower, older dashboard response overwriting a newer one (the A85 race class). `evidence.summary` now delegates to the new pure `computeEvidenceSummary`, so the register column, its view and the dashboard figure share one computation. **Bug found and fixed:** the register's "Evidence to review" view set its per-row flag only after filtering, so on a freshly loaded register it came up empty — latent since 8b.5 (in normal use the toolbar view was picked after a first render), but it is exactly what a dashboard click does first. **Visual review, two passes with screenshots (desktop 1440 and 390 wide):** pass 1 found the nine tiles breaking 6 + 3 (now two labelled groups that each fill their row), single-column tiles on a phone (now two-up) and a long evidence label wrapping (now "Evidence to review" with a sub-line); pass 2 clean; no page-level horizontal scroll at 390 px. | A90 | **9.6 — dashboard stage 2: the promoted results summary, standard cards and the clause matrix.** Three additions, all reusing 9.5's own machinery rather than inventing a parallel one. **Assessment results summary:** the existing `req_by_result` chart promoted out of the small Charts grid into its own prominent section directly under the KPI row (the brief's own "prominent assessment-results panel") — full, never-truncated labels (the small charts' fixed 120px label column was the exact truncation named as deferred in A88), a bigger proportional bar per result, every row a real clickable button carrying the same server filter `req_by_result`'s segments already had. Removed from the Charts grid it used to live in, so the same number is never shown twice on the page. Results are sorted into one fixed display order (best to worst, `unassessed` last) — `req_by_result`'s own key order follows row-insertion order (whichever result a scan meets first), which read as a shuffled list in the first visual review and is now fixed everywhere the same segments are shown (the summary and each card's own strip). **Standard cards (D26):** new `api/_isoCalc.js` has no new descriptor shape for this — `dashboard.audit` simply calls `computeAuditMetrics(scopeInputsToStandard(fullInput, code))` and wraps it in `buildDashboard` again, once per board standard, always, regardless of the dashboard's own single-standard filter (which narrows the KPI row and charts above, never the cards — their whole purpose is the side-by-side comparison a single-standard view would remove). A card's numbers are therefore provably the exact same `buildDashboard` output selecting that standard would show (`iso-dashboard-test` asserts this directly, byte for byte except the per-request timestamp), never a second computation that could drift. Each card groups 9 of its own kpis into four sections — Coverage (applicable, assessed, not assessed), Results (conforming, gaps, plus the result distribution as a compact strip), Evidence review (evidence to review), Findings & actions (open findings, open actions, overdue actions) — one deliberate wording choice: the plan's own "coverage / results / evidence review / action completion" folds open findings in with actions here, matching the brief's own card design (section 6), rather than inventing a fifth section for one number. Cards use the standard's own fixed colour (9001 blue `#1d6fb8`, 14001 green `#0d6e4e`, 45001 orange `#b45309`, already defined since 9.5's filter bar) as a top border and dot, never reused for a result colour. Each card's "Open register" opens every requirement of that standard with no applicability restriction (N/A included) — deliberately the board's whole register, not the same filter any of the card's own kpis use. A board with one standard shows no cards (nothing to compare against). The per-standard "workbook baseline vs now" line reads the card's own already-scoped `baseline_vs_current` — no extra calculation, since computing that card's metrics from standard-scoped input naturally scopes its baseline too. **Clause matrix (`computeClauseMatrix`, D29):** always every board standard × clause family 4–10, independent of the dashboard filter (same reasoning as the cards). A new `clauseFamilyOf(clause)` helper (the top-level clause digit) is shared with `req_by_result_by_clause`, refactored to use it, so the two can never group a requirement under different families. `requirement.list` now attaches this as a real `clause_family` field on every row it returns, so each cell's filter stays a plain `standard_code eq` + `clause_family eq` — the filter schema has no prefix operator, and named in A88/9.3 as needing exactly this; it never needs one now. Each cell shows "assessed of total" plus gaps/evidence-to-review sub-lines when nonzero; a cell with `total === 0` (that standard genuinely has no rows in that family) renders as an honest "—", not a clickable zero — a structural absence is not the same claim as a real, clickable zero count. Rendered as a real `<table>` (semantic rows/columns) with a real `<button>` per populated cell. **Also fixed, found while reviewing this file:** the Requirements register's "Save view" called the native `window.prompt` — the exact A52 bug class (blocks the page's JS thread until dismissed, undetectable headless) that every other prompt in this file was already fixed to avoid; this one call site was missed. Switched to the existing `_promptAsync` helper (A49/A52's own fix), already used elsewhere in this file. B gained the clause matrix's full round-trip (every cell's filter, both standards, ≥10 cells actually checked) and proof that cards/matrix never change with the top standard filter; C gained the results summary's accessibility and fixed order, both card interactions (a stat filtering to that standard, "Open register" opening the whole register), the matrix table's shape and a real cell click, and the one-standard board's cards/matrix each behaving correctly (no cards, one matrix column). `iso-ui-test` 105→107 (the Save-view fix). **Visual review, two passes, screenshots at 1440 and 390 px:** pass 1 found the results list in an arbitrary shuffled order (the fix above); `requirement.list` no longer attaching `clause_family` failed all 7 of one standard's cells at once; a cell filter dropping the `clause_family` predicate over-matched every cell to the whole-standard count; User text is written only as XML-escaped strings, never as `<f>`; text starting with `= + - @`, tab or CR gets a leading `'` (formula-injection guard). 10.2's wording ("the browser loads the template with ExcelJS") is superseded by this. |
| A95 | **10.1 — the sanitised template, api/_isoZip.js, and its allowlist check.** Read the real `Etcher_ISO9001_Gap_Analysis.xlsx` structurally (row/style signatures, formula and range text, data-validation and conditional-formatting `sqref`s — never printed to chat as a block; specific cell text was read only where needed to classify it, and only the minimum). Findings: no Excel Tables, no comments/externalLinks parts already; the Summary sheet's COUNTA/COUNTIF/COUNTIFS/SUM/AVERAGE formulas reference the Register by a fixed `$6:$110` range, so they need no edit at all — a deleted row within that range is just a blank cell to them, true for any standard's row count up to 105; the Register's own conditional-formatting and data-validation `sqref`s (`F6:F110` etc.) are left untouched for the same reason, pre-declaring room 10.2 can fill without touching that XML. **Built `api/_isoZip.js`**: a dependency-free zip reader/writer (Node's own `zlib` for deflate/inflate, a hand-rolled CRC-32) — neither ExcelJS nor the vendored xlsx-0.20.3's `CFB.write` round-trips a real xlsx byte-for-byte (A94; the latter defaults to the OLE2 binary format, not zip, confirmed in the spike for this phase). It copies every untouched part's raw compressed bytes through unchanged (`getPart`/`setPart`/`removePart` touch only named parts) — proven by writing the real template back out and diffing every part byte-for-byte (0 mismatches), and ExcelJS opens the result. New `iso-zip-test.cjs` (24 checks: round trip, CRC, get/set/removePart, a malformed-zip refusal, the real template). **The template itself** (`iso/templates/gap-analysis-template.xlsx`): the Register keeps exactly rows 1-5 (title/subtitle/spacer/instructions/column headers), one section-header row (6, "4 CONTEXT OF THE ORGANIZATION" — ISO's own clause title, not a finding) and one data row (7) as style exemplars (dimension `A1:M7`); that kept row's own client-finding columns — D "Current System", E "Planned Action", M "Linked Actions" — are blanked, keeping A-C (clause/name/requirement, the standard's own wording) and F-L (dropdown-driven status/priority/progress). Source Data keeps only its header row (the import field-key names `api/_isoImport.js` maps a re-uploaded workbook by — kept deliberately, not a finding). `docProps/core.xml`'s creator is blanked (title/subject were already blank). Every shared string no cell references any more (319 of 400) is blanked **in place** — never removed — so no other cell's string-table index needs renumbering; the client's candid findings text (confirmed absent by a direct substring check for three known phrases) is gone rather than merely unlinked. Everything else — styles, theme, conditional formatting, data validations, the logo drawing, headers/footers, column widths, print areas, the Summary sheet's own formulas — is byte-identical to the source (checked part by part). **New `iso-export-test.cjs`** (10.1's own share, 12 checks): every non-blank string left in the template is on an explicit allowlist (and the allowlist has nothing the template doesn't); no inline strings (which would bypass that check); no comments/externalLinks parts; docProps blanked; Source Data and Register row counts exact; D7/E7/M7 carry no string reference. Cuts proven both ways: a re-introduced finding fails the test; a stale allowlist entry with nothing behind it also fails. 10.2 (export.dataset + filling the template per standard) is next; its own tests extend this same file. |

## 15. Deploy checklist and rollback (for the user — Sonnet does NOT run this)

Only after the user approves the reviewed build:
0. Local `main` and `origin/main` are in sync as of `8802ea2` (see the RESUME HERE note above) — the Task
   Hub fixes that were separated out along the way (`61acecb`, `8863987`, `b0db6c6`, `b328502`, `506902d`,
   `2bbaaf7`, `55e076a`, `8802ea2`) are already deployed, each on its own explicit go-ahead. Nothing is
   being held back for this checklist to release;
   this step is only relevant again if a future session separates out and commits another Task Hub fix
   before the ISO commit itself is ready.
1. Production prerequisites: migrations **007 and 008 applied**, "Allow new users to sign up" **off**
   (Supabase → Authentication). Verify before anything else.
2. Commit (user instruction) — including `.vercelignore` with `workboard/` **and** `docs/`. Never add
   `workboard/`, `appdata.json`, `.env*`, `.iso-offline/`.
3. User pastes `009`–`013` into the production SQL editor in order; runs each file's Check section.
4. Create bucket `iso-evidence` (private, 25 MB) in production.
5. Feature flag: `ISO_ENABLED=1` in Vercel env; `/api/config` returns `isoEnabled` and the nav item/portal
   tab render only when true. Deploy with the flag **off**, smoke-test, then turn it on.
6. Read-only live smoke (staff login → ISO list loads; client login sees no ISO tab until shared).
7. Import the workbook in production through the Import screen (dry run → apply).

Rollback: turn `ISO_ENABLED` off (instant, no deploy); revert the commit; if tables must go, run each
migration's Rollback section in reverse order (013 → 009) and delete the bucket. Workboards are
unaffected by all of this because ISO never writes the blob. The only ISO traces in the blob are ordinary
time entries carrying `iso*` label fields (they stay valid, billable Client entries with a readable label)
and the `billing` flag on staff records (a Task Hub feature that stays useful without ISO).

| 2026-09-30 | Phase 7.3, slice 2 | `action.verify`/`action.reopen` — the two ops A41 deferred out of slice 1. Neither `iso_verify_action` nor `iso_reopen_action` (009's SQL, written since Phase 3) fits `db.transition()`'s (column,from,to) shape: verify sets `verification_status`/`verification_notes`/`verified_by`/`verified_at` in one call and only conditionally moves `status` (back to `in_progress` on `'ineffective'`, left alone on `'effective'` — the real status-advance to `'verified'` stays a separate `action.updateStatus` call, both gated by `canVerifyAction`); reopen gates on a `status IN ('verified','closed')` *set*, not a single from-value, plus a required reason. Added dedicated `verifyAction`/`reopenAction` methods to both adapters instead of forcing either primitive, mirroring `updateMember`/`patchRow`'s "extend the interface" precedent (A21/A26). A real, not-decorative design point (A42): `iso_verify_action`'s own SQL has no major-NC root-cause check at all (only `iso_transition_action`'s submitted_for_review→ready_for_verification move does) — but the root cause can be edited/blanked again after an action already reached `ready_for_verification`, so `action.verify` calls `majorNcRootCauseGate` itself for `result==='effective'` (not `'ineffective'`, which never claims the fix worked). Proven load-bearing adversarially: reached `ready_for_verification` with a valid root cause, blanked `root_cause_summary` via a normal `action.update`, confirmed `action.verify` with `result:'effective'` is still refused. `ACTION_REOPEN_REASONS` (§6.2's own 7-value allowed-reasons list) is enforced in JS only, matching the SQL's own coarser non-null-only check — same division of labour as the root-cause gate. | 2026-09-30 | Phase 7.3, slice 4 | `action.submitForReview`/`action.decideReview` (`iso_action_reviews`, `submission_no` incrementing per resubmission cycle) and `extension.request`/`extension.decide` (`iso_action_extensions`) — the last two items on A41's original next-up list. `submitForReview` moves status to `submitted_for_review` via the existing `db.transition()`/`ACTION_TRANSITIONS` machinery AND creates a numbered review row in the same call (`submission_no` = 1 + the highest existing one for that action, so a rejection-and-resubmission cycle is fully traceable, not just the latest attempt). `decideReview` (A45): a `'rejected'`/`'more_info'` decision auto-reverts the action to `more_info_required` (only one sensible destination), mirroring `action.verify`'s own `'ineffective'` auto-revert (A42) — but an `'accepted'` decision does NOT auto-advance to `ready_for_verification`, mirroring `'effective'` leaving status alone, so the major-NC gate stays checked in exactly the one place it already lives (`action.updateStatus`), never duplicated into a second review-side gate. Neither `iso_action_reviews` nor `iso_action_extensions` has a `row_version` — both use `patchRow` for their one-shot decision write, the same precedent `relationship.decide` already set (including its "only an undecided one can be decided" guard, refusing a second decision on the same row with a 400 rather than silently overwriting it). `extension.decide('approved')` (A46) writes the action's own `due_date` straight through `db.update()` rather than back through `action.update`'s own overdue-due-date `requireReason` gate — that gate exists so a due-date change always carries a reason, and an approved extension's reason is already on file in the extension record itself, so routing it back through `action.update` would just demand a second, redundant reason for the same change. Since `iso_action_reviews`/`iso_action_extensions` have no `audit_id` column of their own (child of an action only), `review.list`/`extension.list` take an `actionId` and verify it belongs to this audit before listing by `action_id`, rather than a direct audit-scoped `where` filter. Two real bugs caught while writing this slice's own tests, neither shipped (hard rule 7): (1) `extension.request`'s insert never set `decision: 'pending'` explicitly — the exact same class of gap as slice 1's `action.create` missing `status` — so the offline store left every extension's `decision` permanently `undefined`, which `extension.decide`'s own "already decided" guard then misread as *always* already decided, refusing every real decide call; caught immediately when the very first `extension.decide('approved')` test failed, proven load-bearing by reverting the one-line fix and watching the same checks fail again before restoring it. (2) confirmed while adding this slice's tables to `iso_update_row`'s SQL switch that `subtask.list`'s own `where` filter (`audit_id`/`archived_at`) had silently relied on `iso_action_subtasks` having no `FILTERABLE` entry at all in `_isoDbSupabase.js` (an oversight from slice 3, caught and fixed in this same session before any gate run treated it as done — see slice 3's row). New coverage: `iso-logic-test` (`REVIEW_DECISIONS` enumerated against `iso_action_reviews`' own check constraint exactly); `iso-security-test` (a full submit→more_info→resubmit→accept cycle with `submission_no` incrementing 1→2, a same-status resubmit refused, a contributor refused `decideReview`, an invalid decision value refused, a second decision on the same review refused, `review.list` returning both cycles, all 3 review ops 403 for a client; an extension request refused with no due date to extend, a contributor successfully requesting one, a non-tier decider refused, an invalid decision refused, approval actually moving the action's `due_date`, a second decision refused, rejection leaving `due_date` untouched, `extension.list` returning both requests, all 3 extension ops 403 for a client). **Also closed out Phase 7.3's checklist item in full (A47), not a separate slice:** `auditor_review_note` added to `ACTION_WRITABLE_FIELDS` (already correctly unreadable by clients via `NEVER_TO_CLIENT`, but staff couldn't write it through any op either — a real gap); `client_response` made genuinely client-writable — the first client-write path for any action field, built narrowly (`action.update`'s `allow()` accepts a client only when the patch touches exactly `client_response`, `run()` re-checks the row is actually shared with that client and in a respondable status, `clientProjection` changed from `null` to `'action'` since a client can now get a real response back); and `causeTreeHasCycle` (flat `{id,parentId,text}` array, a bounded parent-chain walk) added and wired into `action.update`'s validation, since the plan's own Tests section explicitly names "a cause_tree with a cycle → 400" as required and nothing enforced it before now. Cause categories was already a real, final flat-tag checkbox list from the Actions slice — a hierarchy was never the right UI for a flat set, so it needed no further work; the other three each get their own widget: 5 Whys (up to 5 why/answer row pairs, add/remove, mirroring `action.update`'s own <=5 validation), fishbone (a fixed, standard 6M taxonomy — method/machine/material/manpower/measurement/environment — each with its own add/remove cause list), and a cause-and-effect tree (an indented outline built from the flat `{id,parentId,text}` array the plan's own §14 calls for; "+ Child" appends a node whose `parentId` is always an existing node's real id, so a cycle can never be created through this UI at all — the server's own `causeTreeHasCycle` stays as the real backstop for any other future write path, per its own comment). Only the editor matching the currently-selected `root_cause_method` renders, so a user is never filling in three unrelated structures at once. Architecture: an in-memory `_capaDraft` (five_whys/fishbone/cause_tree) is edited locally via add/remove/edit handlers that only re-render this one card (`_renderGuidedRC`), not the whole drawer — no round-trip per keystroke — and is read directly by `_adSaveCapa` rather than re-scraping generated DOM, matching a real, deliberate design choice (not a shortcut) since the DOM for a variable-length list has no stable id-per-row to scrape reliably. **A second real, non-obvious bug found and fixed this slice, caught only by this session's own hard-rule-7 discipline, not by the tests as first written:** `_capaDraft` was never resynced from the server's own response after a save succeeded — an adversarial check that forced the *sent* `five_whys` to be empty still showed the typed values in the reopened drawer, because the untouched in-memory draft was never corrected to match what had actually persisted. This is the third instance this session of the same underlying lesson (A49, the register-refresh fix, and now this): a UI can hold more than one cached copy of the same data, and a mutation must resync every one of them from the server's own authoritative response, not just optimistically assume its own local state is now the truth. Fixed by rebuilding `_capaDraft` from `updated.five_whys`/`fishbone`/`cause_tree` (the real `action.update` response) immediately after a successful `_adSaveCapa`. New coverage: extended `iso-ui-test.cjs` (83→88) — a 5 Whys entry, a fishbone cause filed under its real category, and (already covered in the Actions slice) a two-node cause tree with real parent/child indentation, each round-tripped through a real `action.update` call and re-read via `.value` on the actual input elements (not `innerText`, which never includes an `<input>`'s value — a genuine test-writing mistake caught and fixed along the way, not an app bug).
